- `size-picker` — collapsible categories, CPU/RAM bars per option
- `image-picker` — grouped by OS family, searchable, "owned" badge for account images
- `disk-picker` — searchable by name/zone/type
- `ssh-key-picker` — system keys from `~/.ssh/` (private keys only); on selection, reads `.pub` file to auto-populate SSH username from comment

Pricing UX:

- Header can show an "Estimated cost" badge when the selected `size-picker` option includes `priceMonthly`; host reads generic `SizeOption.priceMonthly` metadata and does not hard-code provider pricing logic.
- Host can call optional `getCreateSizePricing()` after initial form load to progressively fill `priceMonthly` for `size-picker` options (used for slow pricing APIs).
- Host can call optional `getCreateCostEstimate()` for full monthly totals from provider logic (e.g. VM + boot disk) so storage is included in the estimate badge.

On success: dispatches `iw:resources-changed`, navigates directly to the new resource's detail view.

### Extra / add-on disks

VM create forms expose an optional "Extra … Disk/Volume" section (a `select` toggle that reveals a disk-slider + disk-type select via `showWhen`). Wiring per provider:

- **GCP** `gce-instance` — extra disk goes into `disks[]` in the instance POST with `boot: false, autoDelete: false` (native inline).
- **AWS** `ec2-instance` — extra EBS goes into `BlockDeviceMapping.2.*` in `RunInstances` with `DeleteOnTermination=false` (native inline).
- **Azure** `azure-vm` — extra disk goes into `storageProfile.dataDisks` as an `Empty` managed disk at LUN 0 in the VM PUT (native inline).
- **Hetzner** `server` — 2-step: create the server, then POST `/volumes` with `server: {id}, automount: true` so the API handles attach.
- **DigitalOcean** `droplet` — 2-step: create the droplet, then POST `/volumes` with `droplet_ids: [id]` (attach happens at volume-create time).

---

## Drag-to-attach (disk → VM)

Resource types can declare `attachTargets` on their `ResourceTypeDefinition`. When the user drags a source pill whose type has `attachTargets` matching a target pill's `{pluginId, resourceTypeId}` **within the same account** (plus optional `matchField` equality, e.g. matching `zone`), the pill becomes a drop target with an "Attach" label. On drop, the host calls `PluginClient.attachResource(sourceTypeId, sourceResourceId, targetTypeId, targetResourceId, accountId)`.

Plumbing:

- `DndShell` exposes `onResourceAttach(source, target)`; target's `DraggableResource` is passed via the droppable's `data.target` so the root handler has both resources without re-fetching.
- Desktop: `__root.tsx` dispatches a `iw:resource-attach` window event; `accounts.$accountId.tsx` listens, builds a `PluginClient` via `createPluginClient(accountId, pluginId)`, calls `attachResource`, then dispatches `iw:resources-changed`.
- Web: `__root.tsx` POSTs to `/api/org/:orgId/resources/attach` (in `resource-detail.ts`), which dispatches to `ctx.client.attachResource`. The account metadata route now exposes `attachTargets` on `ResourceTypeInfo` so the pill can include it in its draggable data.

Currently wired (all validate the declared `matchField` before calling the provider API):

- **GCP** `gce-disk` → `gce-instance` — POST `.../instances/{instance}/attachDisk` with the disk `selfLink` (zone-matched).
- **AWS** `ebs-volume` → `ec2-instance` — `ec2:AttachVolume` on `/dev/sdf` (availabilityZone-matched). Also `elastic-ip` → `ec2-instance` uses the same primitive for IP association.
- **Hetzner** `volume` → `server` — POST `/volumes/{id}/actions/attach` with `{ server, automount: true }` (location-matched).
- **DigitalOcean** `volume` → `droplet` — POST `/volumes/{id}/actions` with `{ type: "attach", droplet_id, region }` (region-matched).
- **OVH** `volume` → `instance` — POST `/cloud/project/{p}/volume/{id}/attach` with `{ instanceId }` (region-matched).
- **Scaleway** `block-volume` → `instance` — PATCH `/instance/v1/zones/{zone}/servers/{id}` with a full `volumes` map (reads the server's existing volumes, appends the new entry at the next free integer-string key, writes back; zone-matched). Volumes are created via `POST /block/v1/zones/{zone}/volumes` and attached via the Instance API — Scaleway deprecated the Instances API for Block Storage management.
- **Fly** `volume` → `machine` — POST `/v1/apps/{app}/machines/{id}` with the existing machine config + a new entry appended to `config.mounts` (region-matched + same-app required); labelled "Mount" since Fly mounts are restart-scoped.
- **Azure** `azure-disk` → `azure-vm` — ARM PATCH on the VM with `storageProfile.dataDisks` appended, picking the next free LUN (location-matched).

---

## Delete resource flow

1. `client.deleteResource(typeId, resourceId, accountId)` — plugin API call
2. `DELETE FROM dashboard_pins WHERE resource_id = ?`
3. `DELETE FROM resources WHERE id = ?`
4. `window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent("iw:resources-changed", { detail: { accountId } }))`
5. Navigate to `/accounts/$accountId`

The sidebar and account page both listen for `iw:resources-changed` and re-fetch.

---

## Orphan & idle resource finder ("Potential savings")

Plugins declare waste heuristics **declaratively**: an optional `orphanRule` on `ResourceTypeDefinition` (plugin-base) — all-of field conditions (`empty` / `equals` / `notEquals`) plus a plugin-authored reason string. `evaluateOrphanRule` (plugin-base) is the single evaluator, so classification needs no plugin client, no credentials, and no provider API calls — it runs over already-synced `fields`. Semantics worth knowing: `empty` matches absent **or** `""` (but not `0`/`false`); `equals`/`notEquals` are case-insensitive and never match an absent field — the GCP `gce-disk` rule uses `equals: ""` on `attachedTo` deliberately, so rows synced before the lister populated that field are never falsely flagged. Only write rules over fields the lister actually sets (GCP `static-ip` declares `attachedVmName` but never populates it — a rule on it would flag everything).

**The aggregate is shared, the store is not.** `collectOrphanGroups` (`plugin-base/src/orphans.ts`) is the one scan: plugins + accounts + resource rows in, account groups out, pure. It lives in plugin-base rather than client-core because every host that _runs_ it (web server, electron main, CLI) already loads plugins at runtime, whereas client-core must stay type-only against plugin-base — mobile imports client-core's barrel and would otherwise pull zod/@smithy into its bundle. The response types moved there too; `client-core/src/orphans.ts` is now a type-only re-export, and `@infrawrench/ui/savings` re-exports those plus the shared `SavingsSection` (host-injected `OrphansClient`).

Two hosts feed it:

- **Cloud** — `GET /api/org/:orgId/orphans` (`web/src/services/orphans.ts`, permission `resources:read`) over the org's synced rows. Cost annotation is best-effort: `getResourceCostTotals` (server-core cost-readers) sums 30 trailing days of `cost_daily` grouped by provider-native `resource_id`, matched in memory against `resources.externalId` — only plugins emitting per-resource cost rows produce matches, mixed-currency matches are dropped, and a ClickHouse failure silently yields `cost: null` (the flag never depends on billing).
- **Local** — `listLocalOrphans` (`desktop/electron/local-orphans.ts`) over the desktop SQLite `resources`/`accounts` tables, exposed as the `local_orphans_list` IPC channel and imported directly by the CLI. It loads plugin _metadata_ only: no plugin client, no credential decryption, no network — the same credential-free property the classification always had, now with a store that needs no sign-in. **Caveat: the local `resources` table only holds what the app created or pinned** (nothing writes `last_synced_at` locally, and the sidebar lists live from the provider) — so a local scan is narrow by construction, exactly like `local-dependency-graph.ts`. Widening it would mean a live per-account listing, which needs credentials, which is the line this feature does not cross.

**Missing cost is modelled, not faked.** `OrphanListResponse.costBasis` is `"billing"` (or absent, which means the same) when the host tried to match billing rows, and `"unavailable"` when there is no billing data at all. Local mode sets `"unavailable"` with `costWindowDays: 0`; `SavingsSection` and the CLI table then **drop the cost column entirely** rather than print a dash or a `$0.00` per row, and swap the footer note. The web API deliberately never emits the field, so the HTTP response shape — and `API_VERSION` — is unchanged by any of this.

CLI: `infrawrench orphans` (cloud) / `infrawrench orphans --local`, both `--json`/text. First release ships rules in digitalocean (volumes, unassigned `reserved-ip`s), hetzner (volumes, floating/primary IPs), aws (EBS `available`, unassociated EIPs), gcp (RESERVED external static IPs, unattached READY disks). The DO `reserved-ip` type was added for its rule (DO bills $5/mo for an address that isn't on a Droplet, $0 for one that is) and copies the GCP `equals: ""` guard on `dropletId`, plus a second `locked equals "false"` condition so an address with an assign/unassign still in flight doesn't flap in and out of the savings list.

**It is a section of Costs, not a tab.** It shipped as its own `savings` workspace tab and sidebar tile, and moved under `CostsPanel` (below the month-to-date chart and budgets) because spend and waste answer one question. Three consequences worth knowing:

- `CostsPanel`'s `orphans` prop is optional and the section is omitted when absent. Desktop used to leave it off in local-only mode; it now always passes a client (`lib/orphans-client.ts` picks cloud vs local at call time from `activeCloudOrgId`, so signing in or out under a mounted tab reaches the right store). The optional prop remains for any host that genuinely has nothing to scan.
- The `savings` tab kind is gone from `WorkspaceTabTarget`, so anyone with the tab persisted in localStorage would rehydrate a blank panel. `migrateWorkspaceTabs` (persist `version: 1`, `ui.store.ts`) retargets the first one to Costs in place, drops the rest, and moves the active-tab id — the reason the store has a `version`/`migrate` pair at all.
- `savings` is deliberately **still accepted** by the tab-validation route and its OpenAPI enum (`api/routes/dashboards.ts`, `api/openapi/paths/dashboards.ts`): an older desktop build still sends it and still has a panel to render it, and a strict enum would reject that client's whole tab list. Removing it would be a breaking API change; keeping it means the merge needed no `API_VERSION` bump.

`/org/:orgId/savings` (web) and `/savings` (desktop) survive as redirects to Costs, and both `syncWorkspaceRouteFromPath` implementations map the old segment to the costs target so a deep link doesn't flash a blank tab before the redirect lands.

**Mobile** renders it as the last section of the Costs tab (`mobile/src/features/savings/SavingsSection.tsx` + `useOrphans.ts`), same position and same reasoning — never a tab of its own, so no route and no `_layout` entry. Rows are pressable and open the resource detail screen. The fetch is `fetchOrphans` in `client-core/src/orphans.ts`, which is why that file gained a runtime export: the plugin-base import beside it stays `import type` so the module still erases to zero runtime dependency on plugin-base — a real import would pull zod and the provider SDKs' crypto helpers into the mobile bundle for five interfaces. Mobile honours `costBasis: "unavailable"` even though the web API never emits it (it is a local-mode signal and mobile is cloud-only), and a row whose `cost` is null renders no figure at all rather than `$0.00` or a dash — in a headerless native row a dash reads as "free".

---

## SSH terminal flow

1. User selects key in `SshQuickConnectPanel` → reads private key from `~/.ssh/` or decrypts saved key
2. Username auto-populated from `.pub` file comment (`user@host` → `user`)
3. `connect()` → `setResolvedKey(key)` → renders `SshTerminal`
4. `SshTerminal` defers `fitAddon.fit()` to `requestAnimationFrame` (avoids xterm "dimensions undefined" error). It also calls `attachTerminalClipboard(term)` from `@infrawrench/ui` — same helper is used by every other xterm.js terminal (desktop SSH/k9s/k8s-exec, web equivalents) for copy-on-selection + Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+V paste. The helper is duck-typed against the xterm `Terminal` shape so the `ui` package stays xterm-free.
5. `ssh_shell_spawn` IPC → ssh2 in Electron main → resolves to `shellId`
6. Data events: `ssh_shell_data_{shellId}` → `term.write()`
7. Cleanup: `ro.disconnect()`, kill shell, `term.dispose()`

Resource detail pages now expose SSH as a route-local tab. The bottom-docked SSH panel was replaced with an `Open SSH tab` action that promotes the terminal or quick-connect panel into the main content area.

**Terminal accessibility lives in `ui/src/xterm-options.ts`, in two halves.** `getXtermTerminalOptions()` sets `screenReaderMode: true`, which is the whole reason terminals are readable at all — xterm otherwise draws only to its canvas/DOM row layer and the session is invisible to NVDA/VoiceOver/Orca. Every terminal spreads those options (desktop and web SSH/k9s/k8s-exec, both `lib/playback-terminal.ts`), so the flag is set in exactly one place; the only divergent constructor is mobile's WebView bundle (`mobile/scripts/terminal-entry.js`), which cannot import the UI package and carries its own copy. It is **on unconditionally, not behind a preference**: xterm 5.x builds its accessibility tree lazily and sizes it to the _visible viewport rows_, not the 10 000-line scrollback, refreshes it from `onRender` through a `TimeBasedDebouncer` rather than a per-frame loop (the 60fps figure in xterm's wiki design doc predates the 5.0 rewrite), and caps live-region announcements at `MAX_ROWS_TO_READ = 20`. A preference would also have to default off, which is the defect restated — a screen reader user cannot find a setting in a UI they cannot read. The second half is `getTerminalContainerProps(TerminalDescription)`, which every mount-point `<div>` spreads: `role="application"` so NVDA/JAWS drop out of browse mode and keystrokes reach the remote shell, plus an `aria-label` naming the host/pod/container so several open terminal tabs are distinguishable. `kind: "playback"` is the deliberate exception and gets `role="group"` — the recording player sets `disableStdin`, so there is nothing to type into it and focus mode would remove the arrow-key navigation that is the only way to read a replay. **`getTerminalAccessibleName` states only what the caller actually knows**, because a name is heard as the destination and a guess sounds exactly as authoritative as a real answer: an SSH terminal with no `sshHost` is just "SSH terminal" (a web session without one takes the proxy's plugin-config branch and dials whatever `getSshConfig()` reads out of the account credentials, so the resource or account id is emphatically _not_ the host), and a k9s terminal with no namespace claims no scope (the `--namespace` flag is simply omitted, so k9s opens on the kubeconfig context's default, which need not be every namespace). Names and roles are asserted in `ui/src/__tests__/terminal-theme.test.ts`.

### External SSH agent support (Pageant, 1Password)

SSH key pickers (`SshKeyPicker.tsx`, `SshQuickConnectPanel.tsx`) surface external SSH agents as additional `KeySource` variants alongside system (`~/.ssh/`) and saved/app keys.

- **Pageant** — Windows-only. Detected by `ssh_check_pageant` IPC → `electron/pageant.ts`, which shells out to `tasklist` with a 5-second cache.
- **1Password** — any platform. Detected by `ssh_check_1password` IPC → `electron/onepassword-agent.ts`, which checks for the agent socket (macOS: `~/Library/Group Containers/2BUA8C4S2C.com.1password/t/agent.sock`; Linux: `~/.1password/agent.sock` or the snap path; Windows: `$SSH_AUTH_SOCK` if set, else `\\.\pipe\openssh-ssh-agent`) with a 5-second cache.

Each external agent has a sentinel "private key" string the renderer emits when its row is selected — `PAGEANT_SENTINEL` (`__pageant__`) and `ONEPASSWORD_SENTINEL` (`__1password__`). Main-process SSH call sites branch on these sentinels:

- `ssh-tunnel.ts` (`openTunnel`, `sshExecCommand`) — `withAgentOverride` strips `privateKey` and sets `agent: "pageant"` for Pageant or `agent: <socket path>` for 1Password before handing to `ssh2.Client.connect()`.
- `ssh-shell.ts` — sets the `connectAgent` for both auth and (optionally) forwarding. When the user enables agent forwarding alongside an external agent, ssh2 routes the forwarded sign-requests through the same agent — so e.g. `git clone git@github.com:…` on the remote authenticates against 1Password and surfaces a biometric prompt on the user's machine. PEM-key auth still uses the in-process `buildInProcessAgent` for forwarding.

The sentinel constants live in `electron/ssh-agent.ts` (main) and `src/lib/ssh-agent.ts` (renderer) — keep them in sync.

Desktop-only feature: the web app's server-side ssh2 has no path to reach a user's local agent.

---

## Common pitfalls

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| `Cannot read 'dimensions'` in xterm | `fitAddon.fit()` called before DOM layout | Defer to `requestAnimationFrame` |
| Duplicate `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header | CORS interceptor adding `*` when server already sent its own | Check `hasACAO` case-insensitively before injecting |
| OPTIONS preflight blocked with 403 | Server rejects preflight; headers were added to 403 but browser still rejects | Force `statusLine: "HTTP/1.1 200 OK"` on OPTIONS when injecting |
| GCP delete 404 with full resource ID in URL | `externalId` is `project/zone/name`; using it directly as instance name | Use `resource.fields["name"]` or `.split("/").pop()` |
| `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` TS error | `field.defaultValue = x                                                       | undefined` not assignable | Use `...(x ? { defaultValue: x } : {})` spread pattern |
| Plugin-base types missing after change | Dependent package built before plugin-base | Run `pnpm --filter @infrawrench/plugin-base build` first |
| SSH auth fails despite correct key | Username defaults to `root`; GCP/DO use key comment as username | Read `.pub` file comment, use `comment.split("@")[0]` as username |
| Sidebar shows deleted resource | Sidebar caches resource lists; `iw:resources-changed` event not fired | Dispatch event after delete; sidebar listener calls `loadAccountResources(..., true)` |

---

## Web app architecture

### Auth

WorkOS AuthKit — middleware-enforced on all `(app)/*` routes. Auto-provisions user/org on first login. `requireAuth()` returns `{ userId, organizationId, email }`.

`sessionMiddleware` also puts the WorkOS `sid` on the context as `session.sessionId` (from `authenticate()`/`refresh()` on the cookie path, from the `sid` claim on the bearer path). That's what lets the account settings UI flag — and refuse to revoke — the session making the request.

**Do not add `audience` or `issuer` options to `verifyWorkosAccessToken`.** It looks like missing hardening and it is not:

- AuthKit access tokens carry **no `aud` claim** (`sub`, `sid`, `iss`, `org_id`, `role`, `permissions`, `exp`, `iat`). Passing `audience` to `jwtVerify` rejects every token — a total outage for MCP, mobile, desktop sync, and chat bearer auth.
- `iss` is not stable across configuration or SDK version (`https://api.workos.com/`, the same without the trailing slash, or the custom AuthKit domain when set), so pinning it is a lockout waiting to happen.
- Neither buys anything: `getJwksUrl(clientId)` resolves to `/sso/jwks/<clientId>`, a **per-client** key set, so a token minted for any other WorkOS client fails the signature check. That is the tenant isolation an `iss`/`aud` pin would be standing in for.

`WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is for MCP OAuth **discovery** only — it is not a token-verification input.

`src/auth/__tests__/api-auth.test.ts` asserts both options stay absent.

**Security response headers** live in `src/api/security-headers.ts`, which defines the set once (`securityHeaderEntries`) and exposes two adapters, because responses leave this server by two different routes:

- `securityHeaders()` — Hono middleware, mounted **twice**: on `api` (all API responses, dev and prod) and on `prodApp` in `server.ts` (the SPA shell and static assets, which `api` never emits). Two apps serve responses; a header mounted on one only is a header half the surface doesn't get. Setting them twice is a no-op.
- `applySecurityHeaders(res)` — for the handlers that write to a raw `node:http` `ServerResponse`. **`server.ts` intercepts `/api/mcp` and `/healthz` at the Node HTTP level, ahead of the Hono listener**, so no middleware can reach them; `mcp/http-handler.ts` and `respondHealthz` call this themselves. MCP is the one that matters — it's an authenticated tool-calling surface, and it was the gap when this landed. Anything else added to that pre-Hono `if` chain needs the same call.

One set, two adapters, rather than two lists: a header set maintained in two places is one that will disagree in one of them. `src/api/__tests__/security-headers.test.ts` asserts the two adapters emit exactly the same pairs.

The CSP is `frame-ancestors 'none'` and nothing else, deliberately. Three things load or generate script from outside the bundle — `@monaco-editor/react` pulls the Monaco loader from jsdelivr unless handed a bundled instance and spawns workers from `blob:`, `/docs` loads the Scalar bundle from jsdelivr, and Vite dev serves inline modules and uses `eval` for HMR — so a real `script-src` needs nonce plumbing through `index.html`, Monaco pinned to the bundle, and a route-specific relaxation for `/docs`. Shipping `'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval' https://cdn.jsdelivr.net` in the meantime would look like a CSP while permitting exactly what a CSP exists to stop; the module says so rather than pretending. COOP/CORP are off for a similar reason — nothing needs them and the OAuth redirects want verifying against a real WorkOS tenant first. HSTS is production-only so a developer who once hits a local HTTPS proxy doesn't pin `localhost` for two years.

**Custom Authentication API domain (`auth-api.infrawrench.com`):** WorkOS has two separate custom domains — the AuthKit UI/authorization-server domain (`auth.infrawrench.com`, `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN`) and the Authentication API domain, which fronts `api.workos.com` itself. Production uses `auth-api.infrawrench.com` for the latter, and once a custom API domain is live in WorkOS, requests to `api.workos.com` are unsupported. Three places pin it: web reads `WORKOS_API_HOSTNAME` (hostname, no scheme; optional — unset falls back to `api.workos.com`) in `src/auth/workos.ts` and passes it as the SDK's `apiHostname`, which reroutes every SDK call including `getJwksUrl` for bearer verification; desktop `env.production.ts` and mobile `env.ts` hardcode `WORKOS_API_URL = "https://auth-api.infrawrench.com"` for their PKCE `user_management/authorize`/`authenticate` calls (desktop `env.example.ts` stays on `api.workos.com` for self-builds against a vanilla WorkOS app).

### Personal account settings (`api/routes/profile.ts`)

`/api/profile/*` is user-scoped, deliberately outside the org tree: one WorkOS identity is shared across every org a user belongs to. It wraps WorkOS user management for name, password reset, TOTP factors, and active sessions, and backs **Settings → General** on web plus **Settings → Account** on mobile.

Things worth knowing before touching it:

- **Ownership is re-checked on every mutation.** `mfa.deleteFactor` and `revokeSession` take a bare id with no user binding, so each handler first lists the caller's own factors/sessions and 404s ids that aren't in that list.
- **Enrolment creates the factor immediately.** WorkOS returns the TOTP secret and a first challenge up front; the factor only becomes usable once a code verifies. `listAuthFactors` exposes no verified flag, so an abandoned enrolment is indistinguishable from a live one — the client DELETEs the factor on cancel to compensate.
- **Password changes go through a hosted reset link**, not a current-password form. `authenticateWithPassword` would fail for exactly the users who enabled MFA, and it's also how an SSO/OAuth-only account sets a first password.
- **Email changes use WorkOS's two-step flow** (`POST /user_management/users/{id}/email_change/send` then `.../confirm`): the code goes to the _new_ address and the account only moves when it comes back, so an abandoned or mistyped change can't strand anyone. These are called via `workos.post` because they postdate @workos-inc/node 8.11, which is what we pin — swap them for SDK helpers on the v10 bump (latest is 10.x; the major carries breaking session/auth changes, so it's its own piece of work).
- **`/api/auth/me` reads the email from our `users` row, not `session.email`.** The sealed cookie caches the user WorkOS returned at sign-in, so it reports the pre-change address until the session refreshes.
- Client contract (types, formatters, and `CloudFetch` helpers) lives in `client-core/src/profile.ts` so mobile shares it; `@infrawrench/ui` re-exports the types for web and desktop.

#### Account deletion (`DELETE /api/profile`, `services/account-deletion.ts`)

Exists because **App Store guideline 5.1.1(v)** requires any app that can create an account to delete one from inside the app — sign-in goes through AuthKit, whose hosted page offers sign-up, so the rule applies and a first iOS submission fails without it. Step-up guarded like the other takeover-adjacent routes.

The user row is the easy half. The hazard is the organizations hanging off it: `organization_members.user_id` **cascades**, so a naive delete walks straight past the `countOwners() <= 1` guards in `routes/team.ts` and can leave an ownerless — or memberless — org with a live Stripe subscription still charging. Nothing in the schema forbids that; there is no partial unique index requiring an owner, and no org-delete route existed before this. So every membership is classified first, by `classifyMemberships` in **`services/account-deletion-plan.ts`** — deliberately a pure function in its own module, importing nothing that touches the database, because that is the part that must not be wrong and the DB-touching module can't be imported in a unit test (`db/client.ts` throws without `DATABASE_URL` at import time). Only member → delete the org and cancel its subscription. Only owner but others present → **409 `transfer_ownership_required`** naming them; promoting another owner is already possible in Settings → Team, so it's a detour, not a dead end. Anything else → just leave.

**The step order in `deleteAccount` is load-bearing, top to bottom:** plan and refuse early → audit the orgs being _left_ (the row is org-scoped and FK-cascades, so it has to be written while the membership still exists; orgs being deleted take their audit log with them) → cancel Stripe (a failure aborts everything: deleting an org while its subscription keeps charging is the one outcome the user can't recover from, and they can cancel in Billing and retry) → **revoke every WorkOS session** → delete local rows in a transaction → delete the WorkOS user last. Two of those deserve their own line:

- **Revoking sessions before the DB delete is not politeness, it's correctness.** `sessionMiddleware` calls `provisionUser` on every authenticated request — an `insert(users).onConflictDoNothing()` (`auth-middleware.ts:196`) — so any request in flight after the delete silently re-creates the row. Cutting the sessions closes the window; deleting the WorkOS user closes it for good.
- **WorkOS last, not first.** If WorkOS deletion is what fails, the data is already gone and the user can sign in again (getting a fresh empty account) and retry. The reverse order fails into "can't sign in, data still here", which needs an operator. The `deleteUser` call is therefore logged-and-swallowed rather than fatal.

This is also **the first `db.transaction` in the codebase** — `grep db.transaction` had zero hits before it. `chat_conversations.user_id` became **nullable + `set null`** (migration `0037`) in the same change: it used to cascade, which took unreported `chat_usage` rows with it, so an account deleted inside the Stripe reporting window silently cost the org its charge. Every read scopes conversations by `userId === auth.userId`, so a null-owned row matches nobody and the history stops being reachable — which is the intent — while the usage rows underneath survive to be billed. The dual-source owner rule (`roles.systemKey` wins, legacy `organization_members.role` text column is the fallback) moved to `services/org-roles.ts` as `isOwnerRole`; `team.ts` had it inline twice and now calls it.

**Desktop has no account surface at all** — no settings route, no `/api/profile` call, and no sign-out — so deletion lives on web and mobile only. Adding it there means building a settings surface and a token-clearing IPC first.

### Database

Drizzle ORM + Neon PostgreSQL. Schema at `web/src/db/schema.ts`. 13 tables total:

- Core: organizations, users, plugin_installations, accounts, resources, secret_field_states, associations, dashboards, dashboard_pins
- SaaS: audit_logs, api_keys, subscriptions, invitations

Sync columns on accounts/resources/dashboards/dashboard_pins/associations: `syncVersion` (monotonic counter), `deletedAt` (soft delete).

Migrations generated via `drizzle-kit generate` — never write SQL directly.

### Server Actions

All mutations in `web/src/actions/`: accounts, resources, dashboard, associations, api-keys, billing, team, audit. Each calls `logAudit()` for the audit trail.

### Server build

`pnpm --filter @infrawrench/web build` produces:

- `dist/client/**` — Vite client bundle (static assets served by Hono in prod)
- `dist/server.mjs` — esbuild-bundled Hono server (ESM, single file, all `node_modules` left external via `--packages=external`)

`pnpm --filter @infrawrench/web start` runs `node dist/server.mjs` with `NODE_ENV=production` + env vars supplied by the caller (dev loads `.env` via `env-loader.mjs`; prod relies on the process environment). The bundler is used instead of a plain `tsc` emit because the source imports are extensionless (`moduleResolution: "Bundler"`), which Node's ESM loader rejects.

### WebSocket proxy

`web/server.ts` (Hono + `@hono/node-server` + `ws`) handles WS upgrades at `/api/ws`. Auth via `?token=` query param (API key or OAuth token). Channels:

- `ssh:open` → SSH terminal session via ssh2; `ssh:data` / `ssh:resize` → bidirectional terminal I/O
- `sql:query` → SQL execution via plugin drivers
- `k8s:exec:open` / `k8s:exec:data` → pod exec
- `k9s:open` / `k9s:data` / `k9s:resize` → the hosted k9s session
- `k8s:pf:open` / `k8s:pf:data` / `k8s:pf:close` → port-forward
- `workflow:run` / `:continue` / `:step` / `:stop` / `:prompt:response` → the live workflow step-debugger (`services/workflow-ws.ts`)
- `deploy:run` / `:stop` / `:prompt:response` → Infrafile deployments

Every channel is gated on `resources:execute` — the browser gets it from `POST /ws-token`, API keys via `requireScope`.

### SSH SSRF: resolve once, dial the address, verify the name

`services/host-validation.ts` exports exactly one function, **`resolveSafeHost(host)` → the address it cleared**, and every server-initiated SSH dial goes through it. The shape is the point: a `assertHostNotInternal(host)` that returned `void` used to sit beside it, and two call sites (`ssh-proxy.ts`'s direct-SSH session, `ssh-fanout.ts`) called it and then handed the _hostname_ to ssh2 — which resolves again. That is a DNS-rebinding TOCTOU, not a guard: `sshHost` arrives on the `ssh:open` frame, so any member with `resources:execute` and a short-TTL record of their own answers the check with a public address and the connect with `169.254.169.254`. Returning the address makes ignoring it a visible choice; nothing enforces this but the absence of the wrapper, so **do not reintroduce a validate-only helper**.

- **The socket goes to the IP, the host key is verified against the name.** Pins live in `ssh_host_keys` keyed by `(org, host, port)`, so verifying against the dialed IP would invalidate every existing trust record and re-prompt operators on hosts they already trust — and again on every address change. `makeHostKeyVerifier` therefore takes the identity separately from whatever `connect()` got: ssh2's `host` is transport only. Two mechanisms, same effect: `ssh-proxy` and `services/ssh.ts` (`sshExec`/`sshExecCapture`, via the optional `SshDialOptions.dialAddress`) pass the IP as `host`; `ssh-tunnel.ts` pre-opens a `net.Socket` and passes `sock`, because `ssh-tunnel-core` owns the opts and derives identity from `opts.host`.
- **Bastion-routed hops are exempt on purpose.** Reaching a private host through a jump host is the documented feature, so `ssh-proxy` skips the guard entirely when `connectThroughAccountId` is set. Hops after the first are dialed over `forwardOut` (`sock`) and never resolved here at all; the first hop's endpoint comes from a stored SSH account written with `accounts:write`, not from the frame, and is routinely private by design.
- **Validate-only calls that remain are the ones whose dial re-pins downstream** — `ssh-tunnels.ts` `/create-account` and `/open` discard the result because `openTunnel` resolves and pins for itself; they exist to turn a bad host into a 400 before a key is decrypted. `/exec` is not in that group (`sshExec` dials what it is given) and threads `dialAddress` through.
- Pinning costs Happy Eyeballs failover across a multi-record name: `net.connect` given a name may try several addresses, given an IP it tries one. Accepted — the same trade `ssh-tunnel.ts` already made.

**The line is who named the host, not how the dial happens.** Every host the _request_ names is guarded; hosts that come from stored credentials or provider API data are not. The four caller-named ones are all guarded now: the `ssh:open` frame (`ssh-proxy`), `/ssh-tunnels/exec`, `/connect/env-deploy` (`targetSshHost` is request body, and it also decides where another resource's secrets get written), and the MCP `ssh_exec` tool. That last one was the easy variant of the same finding and shipped unguarded for a while — worth understanding why it is the shape it is:

- **`risk: "destructive"` is not a reachability control.** `tools/types.ts` says it outright: MCP exposes every tool regardless of risk, and risk only affects the chat surface where a human is in the loop. So `ssh_exec` was unguarded outbound SSH for any API key holding `resources:execute` — and unlike the rebinding case it needed no DNS control at all, just typing `169.254.169.254`.
- **`ssh_exec` guards only the caller's host, by an explicit `config.host === sshHost` test.** That reads as the security property directly ("pin the host we are about to dial iff the caller named it") rather than as a coincidence, and it is correct for the branch structure: when the plugin supplies the endpoint, `resolveSshConfig` ignores `sshHost` entirely.
- **The plugin `getSshConfig()` branch stays unguarded — deliberately, and this is the argument.** That host is either provider API data about the user's own fleet or an SSH account's stored credentials, and writing credentials needs `accounts:write`, which the **member** system role does not have (`server-core/src/permissions/system-roles.ts` — members get `accounts:read` and `resources:execute`). More decisively, SSH is not the door: `docker_command` in the same file dials `credentials[dockerDriver.credentialKey]`, and the SQL proxy and KV console dial credential hosts, all unguarded. An admin who wants the server to reach the metadata endpoint has a dozen ways and needs none of them, so guarding SSH's credential path alone buys nothing while breaking self-hosted servers that legitimately sit inside the private network they manage. `tunnel-ssh-attach.ts` (host from the target resource's own `sshEndpoint` outputs) and `ssh-proxy`'s plugin branch are unguarded for the same reason — the latter also being the one path with a jump-host escape hatch.
- **Fan-out is stricter than this rule** and stays that way: `runFanout` guards account targets too, whose host came from `getSshConfig()`. It shipped that way, `features/ssh-fanout.md` documents it, and a blocked result is a per-host outcome rather than a failed run — but do not read it as the rule.
- The workflow sandbox's SSH (`server-core/workflows/ssh-host.ts`) is outside this call graph and resolves its host from the resource's outputs or the plugin's native config, never from a caller-typed string.

### Resource poller (`@infrawrench/poller`)

A standalone Node microservice at `app/packages/poller/` that keeps `resources` rows fresh so the web UI never has to call provider APIs on page load. It imports shared sync logic from `@infrawrench/server-core` (db client/schema, plugin loader, sync-resources, host services, encryption, tunnel resolver) — see "Server-core package" below.

- **Multi-instance safe (competing consumers)** — run any number of replicas against the same DB; no shard config. Each tick atomically **claims** work via a single `UPDATE … WHERE id IN (SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED) RETURNING` (`src/claim.ts`): concurrent instances skip each other's locked rows, so a due row is only ever handed to one claimer. The claim writes a **lease** into the row's own due column — `next_poll_at = now() + 5min` for accounts, `next_run_at = now() + 10min` for workflows — so **no schema change** was needed; the normal completion path overwrites the lease with the real schedule, and if an instance dies mid-work the row simply comes due again at lease expiry. Two intentional consequences: an account whose poll _throws_ retries at lease expiry (~5 min) instead of hot-looping every tick, and a workflow whose true-next reschedule write fails re-fires at lease expiry instead of duplicating on the next tick (both were pre-existing failure modes).
- Tick every 15s (`src/loop.ts`), claiming due accounts (`deletedAt IS NULL AND (next_poll_at IS NULL OR next_poll_at <= now())`, ordered `last_polled_at NULLS FIRST`), limit = concurrency (default 8). Env overrides per replica: `POLLER_TICK_MS`, `POLLER_CONCURRENCY`.
- Token buckets stay in-process-memory and that's still correct: they're keyed `(pluginId, accountId)` and the lease guarantees an account is polled by at most one instance at a time. (A rate-limit _penalty_ learned on one instance isn't visible to another, but hard failures also persist backoff via `next_poll_at`.)
- **`github-watcher` is multi-instance safe via CAS, not leases** — it has no due-time column to lease (every tick checks every repo), so the claim is a compare-and-swap on `git_last_sha` itself: `UPDATE … SET git_last_sha = $new WHERE id = $id AND git_last_sha = $observed (or IS NULL) RETURNING id`. Zero rows back = another instance recorded the transition = skip the run, so a commit fires exactly once even with overlapping instances (e.g. rolling deploys). Caveat: N replicas still each poll GitHub every 30s per repo — safe but redundant API reads — so one replica remains the sensible steady-state; the CAS is protection, not a scale-out mechanism.
- Per-account work (`src/poll-account.ts`) wraps `syncAccountResources()` from `web/src/services/sync-resources.ts`. Gates each `client.listResources(typeId)` call through an in-memory token bucket keyed by `(pluginId, accountId)`. A type whose bucket is empty that tick is skipped — its existing rows stay put.
- Token bucket config comes from `PluginManifest.rateLimit` (`capacity` / `refillPerSecond`). Default 60/1. Rate-limit errors halve bucket capacity for 5 min.
- On hard failure (429/5xx/timeout): `pollFailureCount++`, `nextPollAt = now() + min(15s * 2^failures, 10min)`. Successful tick resets failure count and schedules `now() + 15s`.
- `upsertResource()` bumps `resources.syncVersion = MAX(sync_version) + 1` on every write, so desktop cloud-sync pull (`cloud-sync.ts`, 60s interval) picks up fresh data without the desktop itself calling provider APIs.
- Manual per-resource refresh: `POST /api/org/:orgId/resources/refresh` (body `{ resourceId, accountId, typeId }`) calls `client.getResource()` for that one row. The web detail page's refresh button (`REFRESH_RESOURCE_EVENT`) hits this endpoint. Account creation still calls `syncAccountResources()` inline so the user sees resources immediately; poller takes over afterwards.
- UI reads `GET /api/org/:orgId/accounts/:id/resources` on mount (no sync fan-out). The 30s client-side interval re-reads the DB.
- `iw:resources-changed` (see `ui/src/utils.ts`) carries `{ accountId?, resourceTypeId? }`. When `resourceTypeId` is set, listeners re-sync just that one type via `POST /sync-type/:typeId` for immediate feedback after create/delete.

### Change timeline / drift feed (cloud-only)

Cross-provider feed of resources that appeared / changed / disappeared between polls. The diff lives in `client-core/src/resource-changes.ts` (re-exported from `server-core/src/resource-changes.ts`, which keeps only the writes and retention — it moved when the [environment diff](#environment-diff-why-does-staging-work-and-prod-doesnt) became its second caller) and is deliberately generic: it compares the stored record (displayName + `fieldsJson` + `outputsJson`, outputs prefixed `outputs.` in the diff) against the fresh fetch — no provider knowledge outside plugins. `sync-resources.ts` loads prior snapshots **before** the upserts, then calls `computeResourceChangeEvents` (pure, unit-tested) + `recordResourceChanges` into the `resource_changes` table (org/account-scoped, `resourceId` intentionally not a FK so history survives upstream deletion; cleanup rides the account cascade). Two noise rules mirror the sync semantics: the diff merges like the upsert (`{...prior, ...fetched}`), so user-supplied fields the lister never echoes back don't read as removed every cycle; and "deleted" events only fire for types whose list call **succeeded** that cycle (same rule as soft-delete). Recording is wrapped so a feed failure can never break a poll. API: `GET /api/org/:orgId/changes` (paginated feed, filter by account/resource/kind/time) and `GET /changes/resource?resourceId=` (query param — composite ids contain slashes), both `resources:read`. UI: org **Changes** page (sidebar tile, workspace tab — see the tab-conversion note below) and a per-resource **Changes** tab (`renderChangesTab` render prop on `DetailView`; web passes `ResourceChangesPanel`). Formatting helpers (`CHANGE_KIND_LABELS`, `formatChangeValue`, `summarizeChange`) live in `client-core/src/resource-changes.ts`, re-exported from `@infrawrench/ui`. **Desktop parity** followed the Costs/Savings template: the feed itself moved into `ui/src/changes/` (`ChangesPanel` + the `ChangeKindBadge`/`ChangeDiffList` parts, host-injected `ChangesClient` — web wraps `apiGet`, desktop wraps a new `cloud_changes_list` IPC in `electron/cloud-data/changes.ts`), and web's `org.$orgId.changes.tsx` and desktop's `routes/changes.tsx` are now thin hosts around it. **Tab conversion (2026-08-04):** Changes, Expiring, Fan-out and Alerts started as plain routes ("no per-instance state worth keeping mounted"), but that left them outside `useWorkspaceTabDocumentTitle` — the browser/window title kept showing the previous active tab — and the user expects the active tab to _be_ the page. All four are workspace-tab kinds now (`changes`/`expiring`/`ssh-fanout`/`metric-alerts`, titles "Changes"/"Expiring"/"Fan-out"/"Alerts" matching the sidebar tiles), via the full chain: ui.store union + three switches, `ui/src/workspace-tabs.ts` factories, both hosts' `lib/workspace-tabs.ts` (navigate args + path parser), both `WorkspaceTabsViewport`s (page bodies moved from routes into `Web*`/`Desktop*` panel components; routes are URL-claiming stubs), desktop `__root` revalidator kind list, web `validate-tabs` kind list AND the openapi `TabTarget` enum (both had also been **missing `"logs"`** — a persisted Logs tab was silently dropped on every reload validation). The desktop cloud-only guards (Changes/Alerts need a poller) moved into the panel components so a restored tab in local mode explains instead of fetching. Remaining plain routes (Moment, settings, admin) get their document title from `plainRouteDocumentTitle` in each host's `lib/workspace-tabs.ts`, passed as `routeTitle` to `useWorkspaceTabDocumentTitle` — which prefers it over the active tab, since on a plain page every tab is background. CLI: `infrawrench changes` (`cli/commands/changes.ts`) — `--last/--days`, `--limit` (→ `pageSize`, 200 max), `--kind`, `-a/--account` (resolved through `resolveAccount`, not passed raw at a uuid-typed param), `--resource`; per-field diffs print only when narrowed to one resource. **Mobile** landed as `features/changes/` (`ChangesScreen` + native `ChangeKindBadge`/`ChangeDiffList` parts — `@infrawrench/ui` is DOM-only, so the panel is rewritten, not imported): `useInfiniteQuery` over `{entries,total}` with a Load-more button and pull-to-refresh, a row that expands into the single-change detail (full before → after list + Open resource), and a `ResourceChangesCard` on the resource detail screen standing in for web's Changes tab. Of the endpoint's seven parameters it exposes **kind**, **account** and a **time-window preset** mapped onto `from` (Any time / 24h / 7d / 30d, plus a "Since alert" preset that only exists when a drift push handed one over) — `to` needs a second date to be worth anything, and `resourceId` is reached by opening the resource instead. The Bearer readers (`fetchOrgChanges`, `fetchResourceChanges`, `changeFeedSearchParams`) went into `client-core/src/resource-changes.ts` beside the contract; they are deliberately named apart from `@infrawrench/ui`'s `ChangeFeedQuery`/`ChangesClient`, which is the host-injected-transport pair web and desktop use for the same endpoints.

**Retention.** The table is append-only, so `pruneResourceChanges()` (same module) drops rows older than `CHANGE_RETENTION_DAYS` = **90 days** — long enough that the feed reads as a quarter of history, and unbreakable for consumers because neither the UI nor the API asks for a fixed range (the org feed is offset-paginated, the resource tab asks for the last N; the weekly digest counts churn from `resources`/`paging_incidents`, never from this table). Deliberately a constant: a per-org window would need a schema change and a settings surface for a number nobody has asked to tune. It runs as the poller's **fifth** tick pass (`tickRetention` in `poller/src/loop.ts`), gated to once an hour by an in-process clock — hourly because the 15s tick doesn't need it, in-process because no claim is warranted: unlike the digest, the work is idempotent, so a duplicate pass from another replica or a restart costs one index probe per org and deletes nothing. The prune is driven **per org** so `resource_changes_org_created_idx` (`organization_id, created_at`) turns it into an index range scan; a single global `created_at < cutoff` has no usable leading column and would scan the table. Batched at 2 000 rows per `DELETE … WHERE ctid IN (SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED)` (SKIP LOCKED gives concurrent pollers disjoint batches), capped at 50 batches per org per pass with the remainder left for the next hour. Never silent: every pass logs the deleted count and cutoff, per-org failures and the batch ceiling log too, and nothing throws into the tick.

**Revert — time-travel undo** (migration `0106`, API `1.17.0`). The feed already records the inverse of every field change, so the button turns one back into a write. The whole design is in three rules. **(1) The writable surface is the plugin's.** `isFieldEditable(field)` moved into plugin-base as the single definition of "the Edit form offers this" (`editable !== false`, minus `secret`/`association`); `EditResourceModal`, the detail endpoint's `editableFields` and the revert planner all call it, so a revert can never issue a provider call an edit couldn't. `outputs.*` is excluded categorically — provider-derived, nothing to write — and a previous value that isn't a scalar is excluded too, because `updateResource` takes `Record<string, string>` and `"[object Object]"` would be an invented call. **(2) The plan is a reconciliation, not a replay.** `computeRevertPlan` (pure, `client-core/src/change-revert.ts`, unit-tested like `computeResourceChangeEvents`) diffs the recorded event against the resource's _live_ fields and classifies each field `revertible` / `already-reverted` / `conflict` / `not-writable` / `provider-derived`; a field that moved again since is a conflict and drops out rather than silently discarding the newer value. **(3) A leased claim, and a re-read that is honestly not a CAS.** Two different mechanisms doing two different jobs, and the distinction was a review finding worth keeping written down.

_Between reverts of the same event_, exclusion is real: the apply claims the row with `UPDATE … SET revert_claimed_at = now, revert_claim_owner = <uuid> WHERE id = ? AND reverted_at IS NULL AND (revert_claimed_at IS NULL OR revert_claimed_at < now - REVERT_CLAIM_LEASE_MS)` (5 min, sized like `CLAIM_LEASE_MS` in `alerts/pass.ts`), so the loser gets 409 `change_revert_conflict`.

**The claim and the completion are deliberately two columns.** The first cut claimed by setting `reverted_at`, which had no recovery path: a process that stopped between the claim committing and the provider call returning left the event marked reverted forever — blocking every retry _and_ lying about an undo that never happened. `revert_claimed_at` is a lease, `reverted_at` is a fact written only by `completeRevert` after the provider accepted. Crash-after-write is safe by construction rather than bookkeeping: the lease expires, the retry re-reads, every field is already at its old value, the plan is all `already-reverted`, nothing is written twice.

**And the lease has an owner, because a lease without one is only a timer.** The second cut had the lease but no `revert_claim_owner`, so an attempt whose provider call outlived five minutes would, on its way out, clear or complete _the claim that had replaced it_ — dropping the event open for a third attempt while the second was still writing. That is strictly worse than the stuck claim it replaced: concurrent writes rather than a blocked retry. Every write that ends a revert is now fenced on the token its claim minted (`fencedWhere`, the same shape as `network-flow/pass.ts`), so a superseded attempt matches no row and does nothing. `completeRevert` returns false in that case and the route answers 409 naming `appliedFields`, since the write did land and pretending otherwise would be its own lie.

**The whole claim lifecycle is enumerated as a table in the route's docstring** — nine terminal states over (provider write reached / threw / not reached) × (recorded / superseded / DB threw / never attempted). Three of the rows are one loop and are the reason the handler is not simply "write, then record": a write that lands and is never recorded (DB error, or the process dying) leaves the resource reverted and the event marked un-reverted, and the retry — finding nothing to write — used to release the claim and walk away, so the feed disagreed with the provider **permanently**. That is the shadow side of the "a retry finds everything `already-reverted`, so nothing is written twice" argument: true of the provider, but it also declined to record. Now a retry that finds every field already back, **on an event whose claim was still outstanding**, completes it instead (`revertLooksAlreadyApplied`, pure, in client-core). The stale claim is the discriminator that keeps this from recording a hand-edit as somebody's revert: both exits clear the claim, so a claim outliving its holder means precisely "an attempt was in flight and never recorded an outcome" — the only state in which an unrecorded write is possible.

**A lock cannot double as a journal, and trying was the single most expensive mistake on this branch.** Two versions inferred "an earlier attempt may have written" from "a claim is still outstanding". That is unsound — a claim outlives an attempt that died _before_ writing exactly as readily as one that died after — and it failed in both directions at once: events wedged behind a lease every retry renewed, _and_ an unrelated hand-edit recorded as somebody's revert. Patching the predicate produced a new P1 each round until the state was split three ways instead:

| column                                     | role                                              | written                              | cleared                                    |
| ------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------ |
| `revert_claimed_at` + `revert_claim_owner` | **lock** — who may act                            | at claim, leased 5 min, owner-fenced | every non-completing exit, unconditionally |
| `revert_write_attempted_at`                | **journal** — a write was issued, outcome unknown | immediately before `updateResource`  | only by `completeRevert`                   |
| `reverted_at`                              | **fact** — the provider accepted                  | after the write                      | never                                      |

The journal is the shape `managed_invoices` already uses for `delivery_attempted_at` vs `delivered_at` ("a person said this went out" and "we delivered it" are different claims). Its **absence proves no write was issued**, which is what lets every exit release the claim unconditionally — no `releaseIfSafe`, no wedging, no oscillation. It survives a release, because a write that threw may still have applied, which folds the old row-5 residual into the ordinary reconcile path.

The residual that remains is named in the route's table: a process dying between the journal write and the provider call leaves a journal entry for a write never issued, and a hand-edit that exactly matches that pending revert would then reconcile. Two statements wide, and the audit entry says `reconciled` ("recorded an earlier attempt's write") rather than claiming this user made the change. Closing it needs a confirmation the provider cannot give.

**The audit entry is written on the strength of the write, not of the claim** — `logAudit` runs before the 409/500 returns, or a real mutation to someone's infrastructure would have no actor against it, which is the one thing the audit log exists to prevent. One entry per attempt, with `metadata.outcome` ∈ `recorded` | `superseded` | `unrecorded` | `reconciled` (`RevertAuditOutcome`): that field is what stops a contested outcome reading as several independent reverts, and `reconciled` is kept separate from `recorded` precisely because it involves no provider call. An attempt that neither wrote nor recorded logs nothing. **This is the one `logAudit` call site in the package that is `await`ed rather than `void`ed** — 168 of the other 181 are fire-and-forget and rightly so, because they record changes to Infrawrench's own database; this one records an irreversible mutation to somebody else's cloud, so the insert is made durable before responding.

**Attribution here is best-effort, and saying so is the design.** Nothing transactional spans a third-party cloud API and our Postgres, so the gap between "the provider accepted" and "the audit row committed" cannot be closed at this layer — failing the response would not restore attribution, it would only change what the caller is told about a change that already happened. Closing it properly means a durable outbox (a row written in the same transaction as the claim, drained by the poller), which is platform-level work and not something to bolt onto one route. What the route does instead is make the gap loud: `logAudit` now **returns whether the row landed** (a boolean, never a throw — an audit failure must not make an applied mutation read as failed; the 168 `void` callers are unaffected), the route logs an `AUDIT GAP` line carrying actor, event, plugin, fields and outcome so the trail survives in the log shipper, and the response carries `auditRecorded: false`. **A durable audit outbox is an open platform gap**, not a change-timeline one. What survives an overlap is that the two attempts **cannot disagree**: both invert the same recorded event to the same values, so the later holder's patch is a subset of the earlier one's and an overlap is idempotent in effect.

_Against other writers_, there is **no CAS at all**, and the code says so rather than implying otherwise. The apply rebuilds the plan from a fresh `getResource` and writes through **the same client** (`buildRevertPlan` returns it — a second `getClientForAccount` would put a credential decrypt, the rewriters and a host-services build between the read and the write), but `PluginClient.updateResource(typeId, resourceId, accountId, fields)` takes no precondition: no expected value, no ETag, no version token. A third party writing inside that one-round-trip window is overwritten silently. Closing it means widening `updateResource` across every plugin — a plugin-contract change, not a feature change — so instead the window is documented as a window in the service, the route, the OpenAPI description and the user docs. Do not describe this as a compare-and-swap; the invoice-approval `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` is one, this is not.

Regression tests for all of it live in `web/src/services/__tests__/change-revert-claim.test.ts` (a fake Postgres that evaluates the claim's real predicate, following `network-flow-claim.test.ts`, and throws on any operator it doesn't recognise so a predicate that doesn't test staleness or ownership can't be waved through — covers abandoned-lease recovery and the whole "a superseded attempt can touch nothing" group) and `web/src/api/routes/__tests__/resource-changes-revert.test.ts` (asserts the provider call order is exactly `buildClient → getResource → updateResource`, plus the superseded-mid-write 409).

API is one path under two verbs: `GET /api/org/:orgId/changes/:changeId/revert` is the dry run (`resources:write`, because the plan names the write it offers), `POST` applies. The apply goes through `checkChangeFreeze` like a delete does, audit-logs `resource.change_revert` with field _keys_ only, and **deliberately skips the `resources` row mirror** that `POST /api/resources/update` writes — that mirror is what would erase the drift, so leaving the stored snapshot alone is what makes the next poll record the undo as an ordinary `updated` event instead of a special case. Only `updated` events with a diff are revertible: created/deleted are out of scope and the UI says so on a **disabled** button rather than hiding it (`localRevertRefusal` in client-core words the three refusals once). Surfaces: `ui/src/changes/RevertChange.tsx` (button + dialog, reusing `formatChangeValue`) on the shared `ChangesPanel` — so desktop got it for free over two new IPC channels — plus web's per-resource `ResourceChangesPanel`, and a native `RevertChangeSection` on mobile's feed and `ResourceChangesCard` (the plan renders in place; a phone row is already an expanded detail). The revert transport is an optional `revert?: ChangeRevertClient` on `ChangesClient`, the `MetricAlertsPanel` convention: a host that hasn't wired it renders no button, and the server enforces the permission regardless.

**Drift alerts** (follow-up to the above; migration `0054`, reshaped by `0073` — see "Alert routing rules" below, which deleted the six-file problem this entry described). A fifth trigger `"resourceDrift"` fans the change feed out over push/Slack/Teams. It **defaults off**, alone among the triggers: anomalies could default on because 3σ + a cooldown makes them rare by construction, whereas drift is continuous — a healthy busy org produces it constantly — and defaulting on would have started posting into every already-connected channel the moment the migration landed.

Volume is the whole design and it is answered in three places. `server-core/src/drift/summary.ts` is pure (batching, capping, all three transports' rendering — unit-tested without mocks, same split as `digest/compose.ts` and `anomaly-detect.ts`): `MAX_SCANNED_CHANGES` = 500 read cap (the query asks for 501 so "exactly 500" and "at least 500" are distinguishable, and the overflow prints `500+`), `MAX_LISTED_CHANGES` = 12 named changes with creations/deletions ordered first, `MAX_DIFF_FIELDS` = 4 per updated line, remainder → "…and N more in the change timeline". `drift/alerts.ts` owns the real cap: **one message per org per cooldown window**, claimed with a single conditional UPDATE on `org_drift_alert_settings.last_notified_at` — the `PageCooldownStore` protocol from `paging/deliver.ts` and the `org_digest_settings.last_sent_week_start` claim, reused rather than reinvented, so N poller replicas produce one message and a window whose transports all reached nobody is rolled back rather than spent. That rollback is an **invariant of the claim, not a branch**: everything past the claim runs inside a guard (`releaseUnlessDelivered`) whose rule is _keep the window iff at least one transport delivered_, so a throw releases too. It has to — the claim advances `last_notified_at`, which is what the next digest computes `since` from, so a claim kept without a delivery drops that window's changes from drift notifications permanently and silently. The guard is deliberately keyed on _delivered_, not on _threw_: a throw **after** a transport already succeeded keeps the claim, because those changes really were reported and rewinding would re-send them (the `undelivered` rule, applied mid-fan-out). It also never throws itself (a failed rollback must not mask the error that caused it) and runs at most once. The failure path returns its own `{ status: "failed", error, released }` — it used to return `no-changes`, which made an outage read as a quiet window. Per-**org**, not per-account, deliberately: per-account would scale messages with account count, which is the coupling being broken. The digest covers everything since the previous notification (not just the pass that triggered it), so a suppressed window folds into the next message instead of being lost. `drift/settings.ts` is the third: `org_drift_alert_settings` (one row per org, missing row = shipped defaults, modelled on `org_digest_settings`/`org_cost_anomaly_settings`) carries `notify_created`/`notify_deleted` on, `notify_updated` **off** (field updates are the bulk of the volume and are usually a provider restating a value), `cooldown_minutes` 60 floored at **5** server-side (below the poller's cycle the message rate follows the sync rate again), `min_changes`, and `account_ids` (empty = all).

Wiring: `sync-resources.ts` gained an `onChanges(events)` hook rather than importing the notifier — it must keep knowing nothing about transports, the same separation `onTypeDone` gives the pager — and `poller/src/poll-account.ts` wires it to `notifyResourceDrift`, so a manual UI refresh records the feed but never notifies (the `notePollOutcome` rule). Cheap guards first: no events, or an account/kind the org filtered out, costs one indexed settings read and touches no cooldown row. API: `GET|PUT /api/org/:orgId/changes/alert-settings` (`org:settings:write` — it decides who the org's channels hear from, like the Slack/Teams/digest settings beside it); contract + limits in `client-core/src/resource-changes.ts`, re-exported from `@infrawrench/ui`. Push payload `type: "resource_drift"` carries `changeCount`, `since` and an `accountId` **only when the window covered one account**; mobile's `pushDataToPath` turns it into `/org/[orgId]/changes?since=…[&accountId=…]`, which the screen reads as its `from` filter and account chip (`parsePushData` gained the matching arm — without it the payload validates to `null` and the tap opens nothing). No Twilio: SMS is for things that should interrupt a human, and a drift digest is a thing to read. Web UI: `DriftAlertsSection` on the paging settings page plus the `resourceDrift` entries in `ALERT_TRIGGERS`, the push-preference checkboxes and the admin roster. Desktop has no notification-settings surface at all, so there is nothing to mirror there.

### Environment diff ("why does staging work and prod doesn't?")

Two accounts of the same provider compared side by side: resource types present in one and not the other, per-type count deltas, and the fields on which two corresponding resources disagree. **The second caller of the change-timeline differ** — `computeResourceChangeEvents` was already pure, provider-agnostic and unit-tested over one account against its own past; feeding it two _accounts_ keyed by a name-derived pairing key turns the same comparison into an environment diff, and `created`/`deleted`/`updated` fall out as only-in-B / only-in-A / changed. Nothing in the computation knows what a provider is.

- **The move that made it shareable**: the pure half of `server-core/src/resource-changes.ts` (`diffResourceRecords`, `computeResourceChangeEvents`, `valuesEqual`, the snapshot types) moved into `client-core/src/resource-changes.ts`, beside the wire contract it already owned; server-core re-exports it, so `sync-resources.ts`, the poller, `drift/alerts.ts` and their tests import it from exactly where they always did. The reason is the desktop and CLI: neither can import server-core, and both run this diff. One signature loosened in the move — `computeResourceChangeEvents`'s `fetched` is now a structural `FetchedResourceSnapshot` rather than plugin-base's `ResourceInstance`, so stored rows can play the "after" side without inventing `secretStates`/`createdAt` that a diff never reads. `ResourceInstance` still satisfies it.
- **Computation** (`client-core/src/environment-diff.ts`, pure + unit-tested): `computeEnvironmentDiff({a, b, ...})` → per-type summaries (`countA/countB/delta/onlyInA/onlyInB/changed/identical/missingFrom`) + the differing entries + totals. Two pieces carry all the judgement:
  - **Pairing.** Two accounts share no resource ids, so the key is `resourceTypeId` + the display name with environment words stripped: a fixed vocabulary (`prod|production|prd|live|stg|stage|staging|dev|test|qa|uat|sandbox|preview|preprod|demo|canary|beta|alpha|local`) **plus every word in the two account names**. That last part is what makes it configuration-free — "Acme Blue" vs "Acme Green" strips `acme`/`blue`/`green` on its own. Names also split on letter↔digit boundaries so `web1` pairs with `web-1`. Several resources normalizing to one key pair up to the overlap in a deterministic order (displayName, externalId, id) via `key#ordinal` slot ids, and the leftovers read as only-in-one-side — no "ambiguous" concept for a user to interpret. Falls back to the un-stripped name when stripping would leave nothing (an account named `prod` holding a resource named `prod`).
  - **The identity filter** (`isIdentityChange`). Every prod resource has a different id, address and creation time from its staging twin, so three provider-agnostic rules hide those by default: the field's **last word** names an identifier/link/address (word-split, so `vpcId` hits and `valid` does not — a naive `endsWith("id")` would suppress the latter); both values are timestamps (ISO either side, or numbers under an `at`/`time`/`date` key); or each value is that side's own `externalId`/resource id. Everything else — instance class, engine version, replica count, a flag — is shown, and the filter never guesses "probably fine". Suppressed counts are always reported (`suppressedCount` per entry, `totals.suppressedFieldChanges`), and `includeIdentityFields` turns it off. `displayName` is dropped outright and not counted: pairing normalized it away, so it can never be informative here.
  - `unavailableTypes` is the honesty valve for live-listing hosts: a type whose list call fails on **either** side is excluded from the comparison rather than counted as missing, because "we couldn't ask" and "prod doesn't have one" are opposite answers. Always empty over HTTP (synced rows can't half-fail).
- **Server**: `server-core/src/environment-diff.ts` maps two accounts' Postgres rows onto the pure input (org scope enforced in the query, so guessing ids can't reach another org). `GET /api/org/:orgId/environment-diff?a=&b=[&resourceTypeId=][&includeIdentityFields=]` (`resources:read`), 404 for an account outside the org, 400 for equal ids or two different plugins (`EnvironmentDiffPluginMismatchError` — comparing DO to AWS is noise wearing a diff's clothes, so it's refused rather than rendered).
- **Surfaces**: Env diff workspace tab on web/desktop (shared `EnvironmentDiffSection` in `ui/src/environment-diff/`, host-injected `EnvironmentDiffClient` like `ChangesClient`; tab kind `"environment-diff"` — the full tab recipe, and it is a **single-instance tab with remembered state** like Settings/Deploy: fixed id, but the two account ids are compared in `workspaceTabTargetsEqual` and ride as `?a=&b=` on both platforms, so a comparison survives a reload and pastes as a link), desktop local mode, `infrawrench diff -a staging -b prod` (also positional `diff staging prod`; `-b/--against` because `-a` was already the account flag; `--all`, `--type`, `--json`), `diff_environments` MCP tool (accepts ids **or exact display names** — agents reach for names).
- **Local mode is genuinely different, not a cheaper copy.** The desktop's local `resources` table only holds what the app created or pinned, so there are no synced rows to diff; both local twins (`electron/local-environment-diff.ts` for the CLI, `src/lib/local-environment-diff.ts` for the renderer) enumerate **both accounts through the plugin**, then run the identical shared computation. That is why `unavailableTypes` exists, and why outputs aren't compared locally (`listResources` leaves `resolvedOutputs` empty by contract; resolving them would be a call per resource on both sides).
- **Not on mobile, deliberately.** A two-column field-divergence table is a poor phone surface, and the question it answers ("why is prod different?") is one you follow up by editing something, which mobile doesn't do. The contract lives in client-core, so adding it later costs a screen and nothing else.

### Provider status correlation ("is it me or is it them?", cloud-only)

Watches each provider's **public** status page (zero credentials, zero rate-limit risk) and correlates active incidents against the resources an org holds — "DigitalOcean NYC3 degraded — 12 of your resources there". Split exactly along the plugin/host line:

- **Contract** (`plugin-base/src/status-feed.ts`): the manifest gains an optional data-only `statusFeed: { url, format: "statuspage-v2" | "custom-json" | "rss" | "atom", statusPageUrl? }` (Zod-validated like every other section), and the `Plugin` object gains `parseStatusFeed?(body): StatusIncident[]` — a function, so it lives on the plugin object, not the manifest, and needs no credentials. `StatusIncident.regions` must be the exact strings the plugin writes into `fields.region`; `resourceTypes` are the plugin's own type ids; `providerWide` is the fallback scope. plugin-base ships the near-free paths: `parseStatuspageIncidents` (Atlassian Statuspage `/api/v2/incidents/unresolved.json`, component-mapper callback; also parses incident.io's Statuspage-v2 emulation — those hosts 404 on `unresolved.json`, so their plugins fetch `incidents.json` and filter resolved) and `parseStatusFeedXml` (hand-rolled RSS/Atom item scanner — no deps). 17 plugins wired: aws (custom JSON `health.aws.amazon.com/public/currentevents` — **UTF-16 BE body**, `service` field encodes `<svc>-<region>`), gcp (`incidents.json`, location ids are region slugs), neon (status.io REST API; also synthesizes incidents from degraded region containers), cloudflare/digitalocean/fly/netlify/vercel/anthropic (status.claude.com)/scaleway (Statuspage), openai/groq/replicate/planetscale/clickhouse (incident.io), hetzner (Atom), azure (RSS, empty channel when healthy). All URLs verified live 2026-08.
- **Collection** (`server-core/src/status/collect.ts`, poller **sixth** tick pass): global (not org-scoped) cache in `provider_status_incidents`, keyed `(plugin_id, external_id)`; per-plugin poll bookkeeping in `provider_status_feeds` whose `next_fetch_at` doubles as the claim lease via the same `UPDATE … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` statement as `claimDueAccounts`. ~3 min cadence, exponential backoff to 30 min on failure. Fetches go through the **egress proxy** (`workflows/fetch.ts`) when configured, with a direct-fetch fallback for proxyless deployments (manifest URLs are code, not user input); the fetch helper BOM-sniffs UTF-16 (AWS). Failures are never invisible: logged with plugin id + URL **and** persisted to `last_status`/`last_error` — a broken feed never silently reads as "no incidents". Incidents that vanish from a feed are closed (`lastSeenAt < fetchStart` ⇒ resolved), so unresolved-only feeds work.
- **Correlation** (`status/match.ts`): pure rule — provider-wide, region match (case-insensitive, plus hierarchical `us-central1` ⊇ `us-central1-a` via a `-` prefix check), or resource-type match — applied at read time against `fields_json`'s `region`/`location`/`zone`. `getOrgStatusIncidents(orgId)` returns active + resolved-within-24h incidents with affected counts, sample resources, and `overlappingChangeCount` (changes on that plugin during the incident window — the Changes-page correlation).
- **Notifications** (`status/notify.ts`): seventh trigger `providerIncidents` (defaults **on** — incidents are exceptional, unlike drift) over push/Slack/Teams, **never Twilio** (an upstream outage isn't fixable by your on-call). Exactly-once per (incident, org): the `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` insert into `provider_status_notifications` _is_ the claim, released (row deleted) when no transport delivered — the drift `releaseUnlessDelivered` invariant in insert form. Maintenance-impact incidents surface in the UI but never alert. Adding the trigger touched the usual six files plus the openapi path enums, client-core types, web paging settings and mobile toggles — the cost that motivated the routing table (below), after which the same job is one `ALERT_TRIGGERS` entry; push payload `type: "provider_incident"` deep-links to `/org/[orgId]/changes`.
- **Surfaces**: `GET /api/org/:orgId/status-incidents` (`resources:read`; wire types in `client-core/src/status-incidents.ts`); shared `ProviderIncidentBanner` + `ProviderIncidentChangesSection` in `ui/src/status/` (host-injected `StatusIncidentsClient` — web `apiGet`, desktop `cloud_status_incidents` IPC), mounted in both shells and both Changes pages; native `ProviderIncidentNotice` on mobile (home + Changes screens); `infrawrench incidents` CLI (`--json` + table); `list_provider_incidents` MCP tool; weekly digest "Provider incidents" line. Desktop never fetches feeds itself — cloud-only like the change timeline — so no Electron CORS work was needed.

### Alert routing rules (cloud-only; migration `0073`, API `1.0.0`)

The boolean-per-trigger-per-channel matrix is gone. It was three schemas' worth of columns (`push_preferences`, `slack_channels`, `msteams_webhooks`) plus a `TRIGGER_COLUMN` map in each of the three transports — the "adding a trigger touches all six files" the drift entry above used to admit to — and the only question a channel could answer was "everything of this kind, yes or no". Routing is now an **ordered rules table**, so the trigger is a _value_ rather than a _column_: adding one is an entry in `ALERT_TRIGGERS` and the `routeAlert` call at the place that raises it. Nothing else.

**The registry is the whole point.** `client-core/src/alert-routing.ts` holds `ALERT_TRIGGERS` (id, label, description, `pushDefaultMuted`, `channelOnly`, `defaultSeverity`), the rule/condition/destination types, the pure matcher, the quiet-hours arithmetic and `validateAlertRule`. It lives in client-core, not server-core, for the usual reason — the editor, mobile and the CLI all need the labels, and the editor needs the matcher itself. `server-core/src/push/types.ts` now aliases `PushTrigger = ChannelTrigger = AlertTrigger`; the two used to be distinct because the weekly digest reaches a channel but not a phone, which is `channelOnly` on the registry entry now and enforced in exactly one place (`route.ts` drops a `push` destination for a channel-only trigger) instead of by two aliases every call site had to choose between.

**`routeAlert` replaced the three-call pattern at all 14 raise sites.** Each used to build a push message, a Slack alert and a Teams alert, await `sendPushToOrg`/`sendSlackToOrg`/`sendMsTeamsToOrg`, then OR the success counts. Now: one call, one trigger name, one result. `slack.ts` and `msteams.ts` lost their trigger columns entirely and take **stored row ids** (`sendSlackToChannels`, `sendMsTeamsToWebhooks`); `push/dispatch.ts` filters on `push_preferences.muted_triggers @> ARRAY[trigger]` instead of picking a boolean column out of an eleven-entry map.

**The one invariant that matters.** Several detectors gate a cooldown or claim on "did this reach anyone" — `cost_anomalies.notified_at`, drift's `releaseUnlessDelivered`, `PageCooldownStore.release`, `provider_status_notifications`' insert-claim, `budget_alert_events.notified_at`. Those protocols are **untouched**; routing slots in exactly where the three sends were. But quiet hours introduce a third state: a **held** alert has `succeeded === 0` and yet _will_ go out. Treating it as undelivered would roll back the very claim that stops the next pass re-raising it — and for drift, rewinding `since` means the released window is re-scanned and the held copy arrives beside a duplicate. So `AlertRouteResult` carries `held` separately and every call site gates on **`alertReached(result)`** (`succeeded > 0 || held > 0`), never on `succeeded > 0`. `byTransport`/`attemptedByTransport` keep the per-transport counts the digest status line, `infra.page()`'s return value and the drift outcome all report.

**Personal mutes stayed personal.** `push_preferences` is the one half of the matrix that did _not_ become a rule: an `alert_rules` row is an org decision about where the org is told, while whether _your_ phone rings at 3am is yours, and folding it in would have let an admin un-mute somebody else's notifications. It just stopped being one column per trigger — eleven booleans became `muted_triggers text[]`, so an unknown or newly added trigger is on by default and needs no migration. The "no row means the shipped defaults" contract survives as `DEFAULT_MUTED_TRIGGERS` (`["resourceDrift"]`), and `resolveTargets` branches on it: a default-**on** trigger treats a missing row as yes, a default-**muted** one requires a row that positively does not mute it. Getting that backwards would buzz every phone in every org that never opened the screen — exactly what the old `.default(false)` prevented.

**Absent facts never match, in either direction.** `accountId in [prod]` does not match an alert with no account; neither does `accountId notIn [prod]`.
