A provider-status incident spans the org, and letting the negative form catch it would route org-wide pages into a per-account channel. Same rule for `amountCents`: a drift digest is not a $0 anomaly, so it fails an under-$500 condition rather than satisfying it. The pure unit tests under `client-core` (alert-routing.test.ts) pin both directions, plus first-match-wins, the tee (`continueOnMatch`), the destination-less swallow rule, and the quiet-hours wrap/DST/weekday-attribution arithmetic — 36 cases, no mocks, because the whole module is pure.

**Quiet hours hold, they do not drop.** A window is `[start, end)` in local minutes-of-day, may wrap past midnight, and `days` names the day the window _opened_ (so a Friday-night window covers Saturday 02:00 without Saturday being listed). `start === end` is empty, not all-day — far likelier a mis-set form than a request for total silence, and disabling the rule is what total silence is for. `quietHoursEnd` does one DST correction pass by re-reading the local clock at the candidate instant: adding N minutes of _real_ time across a spring-forward would otherwise land an hour off the time the user typed. Results floor to the minute so everything held in one window converges on one release instant and flushes as a batch. Four callers set `bypassQuietHours`: `infra.page()` and both approval paths (a blocked run plus "no decision counts as a denial" means holding is silently denying) and the weekly digest (already scheduled to an hour the org chose — two schedules arguing).

**`alert_deliveries` is one table for two features**, because both are "a row with a deadline and a claim column": `deliver_after` for a quiet-hours hold, `escalate_at` for an unacknowledged alert. `alerts/pass.ts` is the poller's **twelfth** pass and claims both with the account-claim idiom (`UPDATE … WHERE id IN (SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED) RETURNING`, lease written into the row's own due column), so N replicas release a held alert once and a replica that dies mid-send lets it come due again at lease expiry. `payload` stores the **rendered** `AlertEvent` because the flush runs hours later in another process, and re-deriving an anomaly's wording from the cost tables would be a second implementation of the message. A flush replays through `routeAlert` with `pinnedLegs` — the destinations the rule named _when the alert happened_, not what the table says now, so the message does not depend on when somebody saved a form — and with `ackDeliveryId` so it attaches the button for the row that already exists rather than arming a second clock. Retention rides the existing hourly retention tick (30 days, terminal states only).

**Escalation is one hop, acknowledged from Slack.** `routeAlert` attaches an Acknowledge button whose value is `{o, d}` — the org alongside the delivery id, matching the status-picker's shape, so the inbound handler can resolve the clicking Slack user to a member of _that_ org before touching a row. Resolving to a member is necessary but not sufficient: the button re-checks `org:settings:write`, the same gate the web endpoint uses, because cancelling an escalation decides that nobody else gets woken up. `acknowledgeAlert` is a conditional UPDATE gated on `state = 'awaiting_ack'`: two people pressing at once produce one winner, an already-escalated row cannot be retroactively silenced, and clearing `escalate_at` in the same statement is what actually stops the pass. It arbitrates races; it is **not** an authorization check, which is why the permission gate lives at both call sites. A row that fails the update reports what it actually is — `already_escalated`, `already_acknowledged`, or `not_pending` for a held/sent/expired row that was never awaiting one — rather than claiming a phantom acknowledgement by somebody the row cannot name. An escalation whose original delivery reached nobody is released rather than armed — the `releaseUnlessDelivered` shape again — because there is nothing to escalate from; if the DELETE fails the row is expired in place instead, since leaving it armed produces exactly the "nobody acknowledged" page that branch exists to prevent. Teams and push carry no ack surface (Teams webhooks are one-way and an `Action.Http` would need an unauthenticated tokenized URL), so an alert routed only there always escalates; the docs say so.

**An alert with its own resolution settles its own deliveries.** A workflow approval is the case: approving, denying or timing out is the acknowledgement, so `decideWorkflowApproval` and `expirePending` call `settleDeliveriesForPushTarget` to move the matching `awaiting_ack` rows to `acknowledged` (a person decided) or `expired` (nobody did). Matched on `payload -> 'pushData' ->> 'approvalId'` rather than a stored id list — `routeAlert` already writes the whole `AlertEvent` into `payload`, so there is no new column and no bookkeeping that can drift from the rows it describes. Without it a decided request keeps its escalation clock and pages about a decision made ten minutes ago, which is worse than no escalation at all.

**No rows means the shipped default**, synthesized in memory by `resolveRoutingRules` and expanded over every live channel: "everything except drift → every channel + push", which is precisely the old matrix with every box ticked. Same idiom as `org_digest_settings`/`org_cost_anomaly_settings`, and it is what keeps "connect Slack, get alerts" true on day one. `POST /alert-rules/adopt-defaults` persists it for editing. Migration `0073` generates rules **per (org, distinct opt-in set)** rather than per channel — channels sharing a configuration share a rule, which is how a person would have written it — and every generated rule sets `continue_on_match` because the old matrix had no ordering: two channels taking the same trigger both got it, and first-match-wins would have silently cut the second off.

**The migration is expand/contract, and it has to be.** `0079` creates the tables, backfills, and adds `muted_triggers`. The 35 legacy boolean columns stay in the database for this release so a rolling deploy's old replicas keep reading them — Drizzle's `select()` names every column in the schema, so dropping them in the same wave as the expand breaks every read of `slack_channels`, `msteams_webhooks` and `push_preferences` until the rollout finishes. Ship a follow-up migration that drops those columns only after `0079`'s release is fully out. Any future migration that drops a column a deployed build still selects needs the same split.

**Surfaces.** `GET|PUT /api/org/:orgId/alert-rules` (+ `/adopt-defaults`, `/deliveries`, `/deliveries/:id/ack`, `/deliveries/cancel`), all `org:settings:write`. PUT is **whole-list** because order is part of the meaning — a reorder as several PATCHes has a window where alerts route somewhere nobody asked for — and it re-derives `position` from array order and rejects destinations belonging to another org. `ui/src/settings/AlertRoutingSection.tsx` is the editor (shared, so desktop's cloud Settings tab gets it; the desktop IPC allowlist gained `alert-rules`). `infrawrench routing` / `routing queue` print the rules as sentences and the in-flight queue. **Mobile deliberately omits the rules editor** — org-wide `org:settings:write` config, the same demotion drift-alert settings and the digest schedule already carry — but its push toggles are now driven by `PUSHABLE_TRIGGERS`, so a new trigger gets a row there for free. `API_VERSION` went to **1.0.0**: dropping the trigger booleans from the Slack/Teams/push shapes is a break an existing client can see.

### Expiry radar

One cross-provider countdown of everything with a clock on it — TLS certs, domain registrations, API tokens, key/secret rotation age. Same declaration-over-synced-state contract as `orphanRule` and `dependsOn`: **never an extra provider API call**.

- **Contract** (`plugin-base/src/resource.ts`): `expiryFields?: ExpiryFieldRule[]` on `ResourceTypeDefinition` — `{ fieldKey, from: "expiry" | "created", kind, label, maxAgeDays? }`, Zod-validated (`maxAgeDays` on a `from: "expiry"` rule fails the manifest — dead config). `from: "created"` derives the deadline from an age budget (`maxAgeDays`, else the per-kind default in client-core's `DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_DAYS`). Kinds (`tls-cert | domain | api-token | access-key | k8s-cert | ssh-key | secret-version | other`) drive grouping/labels only. `server-core/__tests__/plugin-loader.test.ts` validates the registry like `dependsOn`: every `fieldKey` must be a declared field. ~21 types across 14 plugins declare rules (ACM/Cloudflare/GCP/Fly certs, Vercel domains, AI-provider API keys, Neon/PlanetScale credentials + backups, BigQuery table expiry; age budgets on AWS Secrets Manager `lastChangedDate` 90d and OpenAI project keys 180d). Deliberately not declared: IAM access keys and Azure app-registration secret expiries (their listers never sync those fields — declaring would be a rule that matches nothing), and by-design-ephemeral objects (batches, files, cached content, ephemeral pods) whose expiry is noise.
- **Computation** (`client-core/src/expiry.ts`, pure + unit-tested): `computeExpiryFeed(plugins+accounts+resources, {now?, leadDays?})` → sorted `ExpiryItem[]` with severity buckets `expired / critical(<7d) / warning(<30d) / upcoming(≤leadDays, default 60) / ok`. Unlike orphans this lives in client-core, not plugin-base — it needs no plugin-base runtime (the `ExpiryFieldRule` import is type-only) and the CLI/desktop reach it the way `graph` does. `parseExpiryInstant` accepts ISO, date-only, epoch s/ms (number or numeric string, <1e8 rejected so ports never read as dates) and Go-style `"… +0000 UTC"` (Cloudflare origin certs); sentinels (`""`, `"—"`, `"NEVER"`) drop the item, never alarm.
- **Server**: `server-core/src/expiry/` (feed over synced rows, settings, alert pass), `GET /api/org/:orgId/expiring` (`resources:read`) + `GET|PUT /expiring/settings` (`org_expiry_settings`: `enabled`, `lead_days`, `last_notified_at` — one row per org, missing row = defaults, modelled on `org_drift_alert_settings`). Poller pass reuses the drift claim protocol: conditional-UPDATE claim on `last_notified_at` with a 24h cooldown; **a completed scan with nothing due keeps the window spent** (it's a scan cadence, not a notification promise), delivery failure releases it (`releaseUnlessDelivered` semantics). Errors log `[expiry]`-prefixed, never silent. Eighth trigger `expiryAlerts` (defaults **on** — deadline alerts are rare and actionable, unlike drift) over push/Slack/Teams, never Twilio; push payload `type: "expiry_alert"` deep-links to the mobile Expiring screen. Adding it touched the usual six files + openapi + client-core contracts + web/mobile toggles.
- **Surfaces**: Expiring screen on web/desktop (shared `ExpirySection` in `ui/src/expiry/`; desktop local mode computes over the local workspace like `local-orphans`), mobile `features/expiry/`, `infrawrench expiring` CLI (`--json`/`--local`, zero new deps), `list_expiring` MCP tool, weekly digest "Expiring soon" line, org lead-time card on the paging settings page.

### Posture checks

"Potential savings, but for exposure": plugin-declared security check rules evaluated over already-synced fields, producing severity-ranked findings — public buckets, 0.0.0.0/0 ingress, unencrypted disks, publicly-reachable DB endpoints, stale credentials, missing backup/deletion protection. Same declaration-over-synced-state contract as `orphanRule` and `expiryFields`: **never an extra provider API call**.

- **Contract** (`plugin-base/src/posture.ts`, `postureChecks?: PostureCheckRule[]` on `ResourceTypeDefinition`): `{ id, title, severity: critical|high|medium|low, category: public-exposure|encryption|credential-age|data-protection|other, conditions (ALL must hold), reason }`. Conditions extend the orphan operators — `empty | equals | notEquals` (identical semantics: case-insensitive, absent never matches equals/notEquals) plus `truthy`/`falsy` (word-lists `true/1/yes/enabled` vs `false/0/no/disabled`; absent and unknown strings never match — missing data must not alarm) and `olderThanDays` (parses with `parseExpiryInstant` tolerance: ISO, date-only, epoch s/ms; unparseable fails the condition). Zod-validated in `validation/resource.schema.ts` (`value` required for equals/notEquals and forbidden elsewhere — the orphan-condition stance). `evaluatePostureRule` lives in plugin-base; `computePostureFindings` re-implements the same semantics in client-core because its plugin-base import must stay type-only (the expiry stance — mobile must not pull zod). Both sides are tested against the documented contract.
- **Rules are honest**: ~22 rules across aws/gcp/azure/digitalocean/hetzner/cloudflare, written only over fields the listers genuinely store (audited per-lister). Notable: aws `ec2-instance` matches the lister's precomputed `sshAccess` sentence for world-open SSH; gcp `firewall-rule` needs 4 conditions (`sourceRanges equals "0.0.0.0/0"` catches single-range rules only — the `", "`-join has no contains operator); hetzner `server.firewallIds`/DO `droplet.nextBackupStart` use `equals ""` not `empty` because the listers always write the key (pre-field rows must not flag). Deliberately NOT written (listers never sync the fields): S3 public-access blocks, AWS security-group CIDRs (counts only), RDS-instance publiclyAccessible/storageEncrypted/deletionProtection, IAM access-key ages, Azure allowBlobPublicAccess/NSG rule bodies, DO Spaces ACL (lister hardcodes `"private"` — a rule would lie), all of Scaleway (nothing security-relevant syncs).
- **Server**: `server-core/src/posture/` (feed/settings/summary/alerts, mirroring `expiry/` including the claim protocol — conditional upsert on `org_posture_settings.last_notified_at`, 24h cooldown, quiet scan keeps the window, undelivered releases it, `[posture]` log prefix). Settings are just `enabled` — findings have no clock, so no lead-days analog. `GET /api/org/:orgId/posture` (`resources:read`) + `GET|PUT /posture/settings` (`org:settings:write`). Alerts fire on **critical + high only** (medium/low are hygiene, never page). Tenth trigger `postureAlerts` (defaults on) over push/Slack/Teams; push payload `type: "posture_alert"` deep-links to the mobile Posture screen. Adding the trigger touched the usual set: schema columns on all three tables, `push/types` + `dispatch` prefColumn, slack/msteams TRIGGER_COLUMN maps + add-webhook defaults, client-core push/slack/msteams contracts, web push-devices/slack/msteams routes + openapi, paging settings UI, mobile notifications screen.
- **Surfaces**: Posture workspace tab on web/desktop (shared `PostureSection` in `ui/src/posture/`, tab kind `"posture"` — full tab recipe incl. validate-tabs chain + `TabTarget` enum + route stubs + regenerated routeTree.gen.ts), desktop local mode (`electron/local-posture.ts` for the CLI, `src/lib/local-posture.ts` for the renderer, `cloud_posture` IPC), mobile `features/posture/`, `infrawrench posture` CLI (`--json`/`--local`, zero new deps), `list_posture_findings` MCP tool (severity/category filters, whole-feed counts), weekly digest "Posture" line (critical+high, `.catch()`-guarded like `expiringSoon`).
- **Dismissals** (migration `0073_oval_jimmy_woo.sql`; `API_VERSION` 0.38.0 → **0.39.0**): accepting a finding as a known risk. A finding is recomputed on every read and has no identity, so the decision is stored against `(organizationId, resourceId, ruleId)` in `posture_dismissals` (unique index on the triple; `resource_id` is **not** a FK — the `resource_changes` stance — and `dismissed_by` is `set null` so the decision outlives the person). Both key halves are stable: resource ids come from the lister and are what sync upserts, rule ids from the manifest. `computePostureFindings` takes an optional `dismissals` and _partitions_ rather than filters — dismissed findings leave `findings`/`counts`/`totalCount` and reappear in `dismissed` (most recently accepted first) with note + author, which is what silently keeps them out of `alertablePostureFindings`, the poller, the digest and the MCP tool, since all of them read `listPosture`. A dismissal whose rule stopped matching is simply inert — never reported, never pruned. `postureFindingKey()` (NUL-joined; both halves carry slashes/colons) is the one key function every surface uses for row keys, lookups and requests.
- **Dismissal API**: `POST /posture/dismissals` (idempotent upsert; reason trimmed, capped 500, `""` → null) and `DELETE /posture/dismissals?resourceId=…&ruleId=…` (404 when not dismissed). Both `resources:write` — _not_ `org:settings:write` like the alert toggle: accepting a finding is a statement about one resource, and members deliberately can't silence what they can read. Both audited (`posture.finding.dismissed`/`restored`). The key is in the **query string, not the path**, because provider-native resource ids are routinely slash-paths (`projects/p/zones/z/instances/i`).
- **Dismissal surfaces**: shared `PostureSection` gains optional `onDismiss`/`onRestore` (absent ⇒ read-only) with an inline reason box and a collapsible "Dismissed (N)" table; web gates on `resources:write` read from `/team/me` **directly, not via `PermissionsProvider`** (workspace tabs mount in the root viewport, outside the org layout route that provides it); desktop wires cloud IPC (`cloud_posture_dismiss`/`_restore`) or a local SQLite `posture_dismissals` table (single-user ⇒ no author column; timestamps written as ISO explicitly because `datetime('now')` is not); `infrawrench posture dismiss|restore <resourceId> <ruleId> [--reason]` (`--local` refuses while the GUI holds the lock — the DB is read-only there and the write would be silently dropped); mobile dismisses behind a confirm but **without a note** (deliberate omission); MCP gains `dismiss_posture_finding`/`restore_posture_finding` plus `includeDismissed` on the list tool, which always reports `dismissedCount` so "clean" is distinguishable from "silenced".

### Cross-cloud access review (cloud-only)

The principals **inside the customer's clouds** — IAM users and roles, GCP service accounts, Azure app registrations and managed identities, directory users and groups, role bindings, and long-lived provider API keys. Three adjacent things share vocabulary and are none of them this, which is why every module doc and the page copy say so out loud: **Infrawrench's own team roles** (`permissions/catalog`, Settings → Team), **the credentials Infrawrench itself holds** (credential hygiene — our API keys, SSH keys, members' unused permissions, computed from Postgres we own), and **posture checks** (per-resource exposure rules over any type). API_VERSION 1.15.0 → **1.16.0**; **no migration** (see dismissals below).

- **Contract** (`plugin-base/src/principal.ts`, `principalRole?: PrincipalRoleDeclaration` on `ResourceTypeDefinition`): `{ role: "user"|"group"|"role"|"service-account"|"key"|"binding", lastUsedKey?, createdKey?, adminIndicatorKey?, adminValues?, parentKey?, mfaKey?, revokeActionId? }`, Zod-validated. `lastUsedKey` defaults to `"lastUsedAt"` and `createdKey` to `"createdAt"` — the names most listers here already use — and `resolvePrincipalKeys()` is the one place those defaults live. Dead config fails the manifest the way `maxAgeDays` and `privateValues` do: `adminValues` without `adminIndicatorKey`, and `mfaKey` on anything but `role: "user"` (a key or a binding cannot enrol a second factor, so the finding it produced would be a permanent false accusation).
- **The registry test's one asymmetry** (`server-core/__tests__/plugin-loader.test.ts`): an _explicitly declared_ key must be a field the type declares — that is a typo. A _defaulted_ key that resolves to nothing is fine and common: it means the provider does not report that fact, which the review renders as `unknown`. Also checked: the two `revokeActionId`s are pinned by name (nothing else type-checks those strings against `invokeAction`), and no plugin posture rule may claim the `access-review:` id prefix.
- **The load-bearing honesty rule: absent evidence is `unknown`, never "stale".** No `lastUsedKey`, an empty value, or an unparseable one all yield `lastUsedAt: null` / `activity: "unknown"`, which can never produce a stale finding however old the principal is. `unknownActivityCount` rides every response and is printed on the page — "we found nothing" and "we could not look" must not read the same. Half the declaring providers are in that position and the feature is only credible because it says so.
- **Declared on 30 types across 17 plugins**, audited per-lister. Rich rows: aws `iam-user` (`passwordLastUsed`/`createDate`), workos `user` (`lastSignInAt` — the strongest activity evidence in the repo), openai `project-api-key`, planetscale `ps-password`, neon `neon-credential`, mistral `mistral-api-key`, digitalocean `model-api-key`. Grant-shaped: workos `organization-membership` (admin/owner slugs, `revokeActionId: "deactivate"`), workos `role` and deepgram `api-key` (`adminValues` matched against the **whole** joined value — the `sourceRanges equals "0.0.0.0/0"` stance; a substring match would call `widgets:read, *` administrative). Inventory-only, deliberately: gcp `gcp-service-account`, azure `azure-managed-identity`, turso `turso-api-token` — the provider returns no timestamps, and "you have twelve tokens and no record of who owns any of them" is worth saying on its own.
- **Deliberately NOT declared** (the expiry radar's precedent — declaring over a field the lister never syncs is a rule that matches nothing, or worse a rule that lies): Kubernetes RBAC (the plugin lists workloads, not `Role`/`RoleBinding`/`ServiceAccount` — there is no resource type to declare on); AWS access keys and GCP service-account keys (`credentialFormats` on their parent, not listed resources; their age needs `ListAccessKeys` + `GetAccessKeyLastUsed` per user); Azure app-registration client secrets; AWS attached policies as an admin indicator (`ListAttachedUserPolicies` per user); IAM role last-used (`ListRoles` omits `RoleLastUsed`); `fireworks/api-key.lastUsed` (declared as a field, never written by the lister); **`openai/organization-user.apiKeyLastUsedAt`** — it is when that member's _key_ last made a request, so a console-only member would read never-used and a departed member whose cron still runs would read active, both backwards; and **`mfaKey` everywhere** — nothing in the repo syncs an MFA field on a principal, so the rule exists and no type declares it yet. `xai/api-key`'s "rotate" and DO's "regenerate" are rotations, not revocations, so neither is a `revokeActionId`.
- **Computation** (`client-core/src/access-review.ts`, pure + unit-tested): `computeAccessReview(plugins+accounts+resources+owners+dismissals, {now?, staleDays?, expiry?, includeUnowned?})` → principals + findings + per-severity/per-rule/per-role counts. Five rules, each raised only with evidence; a stale admin escalates to `high` and an admin without MFA to `critical`. **Key rotation is read from the expiry radar, not recomputed**: only `basis: "age"` items with `daysRemaining < 0` count, because those are the radar's `from: "created"` budgets — an absolute `expiresAt` is a different fact the radar already alerts on. `includeUnowned: false` exists so a host with no ownership store suppresses that rule rather than flagging everything (a claim about the host, not the cloud). Severity type is `AccessReviewSeverity`, declared separately from `PostureSeverity` on purpose: two sibling client-core modules re-exporting one name under the `ui` barrel makes `export *` ambiguous and the dts rollup drops it (the `ShowbackReport` incident).
- **Dismissals reuse `posture_dismissals` — no new table.** The mechanism is identical down to the key `(organizationId, resourceId, ruleId)`, the partition-not-filter semantics, and the decision outliving the person; a parallel table would be the same DDL twice and would let one resource's decisions live in two places. Disjointness is structural: every review rule id is prefixed `access-review:`, and the registry test forbids a plugin from claiming that prefix. A dismissal for one surface is simply inert on the other, which is the property the posture design already relies on. **`principals` is never filtered by dismissals** — an inventory that hid a principal because one of its findings was accepted would be a lying inventory.
- **Server**: `server-core/src/access-review/feed.ts` (`listAccessReview`, mirroring `posture/feed.ts`) and `summary.ts`. Ownership is joined only for rows whose type declares `principalRole`, so an org with 40 000 resources and 60 keys does not scan the whole table. The expiry read is `.catch`-guarded — a broken radar costs the review one rule, not the page.
- **Alerts ride the posture window, deliberately.** `runPostureAlerts` now computes both feeds under one claim and appends an access section to the same message, under the same `postureAlerts` trigger and the same `org_posture_settings.enabled` switch. Both are recomputed-on-read security findings over synced state sharing one dismissal store; two claims would mean two messages a day about one review, and an eleventh trigger would cost three schema columns, both webhook maps, the push contract and every settings surface to say what the existing one says. `securityAlertTitle` owns the mixed headline, because `postureTitle` on an empty posture feed reads "0 high-severity findings".
- **A half that threw is not a quiet scan** (`status: "scan-failed"`, covered by `__tests__/posture-alerts.test.ts`). The access feed is caught rather than thrown so it cannot stop a posture alert going out — but when it throws _and_ posture found nothing alertable, the window is **not** spent: the claim is rolled back and the next tick retries. Marking it quiet would suppress the retry and every access finding for 24h and leave a broken feed indistinguishable from a clean org, which is the same unknown-versus-clean distinction the findings themselves keep, moved onto the alerting path. When posture _did_ find something the message still goes out, with `ACCESS_REVIEW_UNAVAILABLE_NOTE` appended so it cannot read as a complete picture.
- **Synced names are escaped where they are composed, not where they are sent.** `escapeMrkdwn` has to leave `*` alone (the bodies are built out of `*bold*`), so a principal or resource name containing `*`, `_`, `~` or a backtick would otherwise change how a security alert reads — `~deploy~` renders struck through, which is what "already dealt with" looks like. `escapeMrkdwnFragment` wraps an untrusted fragment in a code span (Slack has no backslash escape; a backtick inside becomes a prime, the one character it cannot reproduce). Both posture's and the review's Slack bodies now take an `escape` argument beside their existing `bold` argument; Teams and push pass identity. Both escapers moved to the **db-free leaf `slack-escape.ts`** — importing them from `slack.ts` dragged `db/client` (and a `DATABASE_URL` requirement) into the pure `summary.ts` modules and broke their tests, the `probes/metric-ids.ts` split for exactly the same reason. `slack.ts` re-exports `escapeMrkdwn`, so there is still one definition and no caller changed.
- **API**: `GET /access-review?staleDays=` and `GET /access-review/export?format=csv|json` (`resources:read`), `POST|DELETE /access-review/dismissals` (`resources:write` — the posture stance: members read the screen, they do not silence it). **No settings route** — the one switch is `/posture/settings`. The export is its own route rather than a query parameter because it answers with a different content type and a `Content-Disposition`, and an auditor's link should paste into a ticket. `accessReviewToCsv` quotes every cell unconditionally and prefixes a leading `=`/`+`/`-`/`@` with a tab: principal names come from the customer's cloud, and an evidence file that executed them would be a formula-injection hole in a compliance artifact. Exports are audited (`access_review.exported`), as are dismissals.
- **Revoke goes through `POST /resources/invoke-action`**, never a bespoke provider call — so it inherits the change-freeze gate, the permission check and the audit row. Only two types declare one today (`workos/organization-membership` → `deactivate`, `anthropic/api-key` → `deactivate-key`); a row without the button is a provider that offers no revocation over its API, and a Revoke button that actually rotated would be a different promise.
- **Surfaces**: Access review workspace tab on web/desktop (shared `AccessReviewSection` in `ui/src/access-review/`, `AccessReviewIcon` — a key, deliberately not another shield, since Posture sits two tiles away and answers a different question; tab kind `"access-review"`, the full chain incl. validate-tabs + `TabTarget` enum + route stubs + regenerated `routeTree.gen.ts`). Desktop is **cloud-only** (`cloud_access_review*` IPC ×4, preload allowlist) unlike Posture/Expiring: two rules need the ownership records and the shared dismissal store. Weekly digest "Access review" line. Docs `features/access-review.md`. **No CLI, MCP or mobile surface yet** — deferred, not forgotten.

### Backup & restore coverage ("what is your actual RPO?", cloud-only)

The third question after "what expires" and "what is exposed": **what is unrecoverable**. Migration `0108_backup_coverage.sql`; `API_VERSION` 1.18.0 → **1.19.0**. Same declaration-over-synced-state contract as `orphanRule` / `expiryFields` / `postureChecks` / `dnsRole` — **never an extra provider API call**, so the whole surface recomputes on read and materialises nothing (the posture-findings stance: a finding is a fact about the world right now, and storing one only creates a second thing that can be stale).

- **Contract** (`plugin-base/src/resource.ts`, Zod in `validation/resource.schema.ts`): two mirror-image `ResourceTypeDefinition` fields, because "what protects this?" is answered by the _protected_ type — that is the type whose absent backup is the finding.
  - `backupRole?: BackupRoleDeclaration` on types that **are** backups: `{ role: "snapshot", backupTypeKey?, backupTypeValues?, sourceKey?, sourceTemplate?, createdKey?, sizeKey?, sizeUnit? }`. Every key defaults (`sourceId`/`createdAt`/`sizeGb`/`gib`). `sourceTemplate` is the `dependsOn` `matchTemplate` idea, and exists for exactly its reason: a bare PlanetScale `branchName` of `"main"` matches a branch in every database in the account, so a two-half id must be composed. `backupTypeKey`+`backupTypeValues` narrow types that bundle backups with non-backups (Hetzner `image` is one listing of public distro images, user snapshots and automatic backups) — load-bearing, not decoration. Refines: the two source forms are mutually exclusive, a template must interpolate ≥1 `{field}`, `sizeUnit` requires `sizeKey`, the two narrowing halves come together.
  - `backupPolicy?: BackupPolicyDeclaration` on stateful types that **need** protecting: `{ protectedBy: string[], automatedBackupFieldKey?, automatedBackupWhen?, retentionDaysFieldKey? }`. `automatedBackupWhen: "present"` is the other documented reading — _any non-empty value_ means on — for DO's `nextBackupStart` (ISO instant when enabled, `""` when off, which is exactly what the existing `droplet-backups-disabled` posture check reads with `equals ""`). A policy carrying none of the three fails the manifest (dead config, the `maxAgeDays` stance).
- **Registry tests** (`server-core/__tests__/plugin-loader.test.ts`): declared keys must be declared fields; `protectedBy` must name a same-plugin type that actually declares `backupRole`; a policy with nothing to judge by fails. Note the deliberate divergence from `dnsRole`: the **defaulted `createdKey` is not required to exist**, because an undatable backup is a degraded declaration (proves a restore point, cannot prove an RPO) rather than a meaningless one — a record with no target is meaningless, a backup with no clock is not.
- **Computation** (`client-core/src/backups.ts`, pure + unit-tested, plugin-base import type-only — the expiry/posture stance so mobile never pulls zod): `computeBackupCoverage(plugins+accounts+resources+policies, {now?, costsByResource?})` → findings, a per-resource coverage table (`protected | automated | stale | unprotected`) and a summary. Four finding kinds: `unprotected` (high under a policy, medium without — a broken promise vs a fact the org may have chosen), `rpo-breach` (high past 2× the objective), `retention-below-policy` (high at `0`, the providers' encoding for "off"), `orphaned-snapshot` (always **low** — pure spend must never outrank missing data).
- **Three states, not two, on the unprotected side.** `unprotected` is a _confirmed_ gap; `unknown` is a resource we could not assess — the type declares `automatedBackupFieldKey`/`retentionDaysFieldKey` but this instance's value is absent or unrecognised (the row synced before the plugin declared the field). `unknown` produces **no finding**, so it never reaches `kindCounts.unprotected`, `riskyBackupFindings` or the digest. The distinction is load-bearing in both directions: fold unknown into unprotected and you invent gaps in the weekly digest; fold _no declared signal_ into unknown and DO volumes, Hetzner servers, Neon branches and Spanner databases all go quiet, because for them the absence of a snapshot genuinely **is** the gap. `unknownCount` is surfaced beside the severity chips next to `unattributableBackupCount`, same reason.
- **The winning policy is tracked per objective, not per resource.** The strictest RPO and the strictest retention routinely come from different policies ("everything, 24h" plus "prod databases, 30 days"), so `rpoPolicy`/`retentionPolicy` are separate and each finding names the one supplying the objective it breaches — the reader follows that link to edit it. Coverage rows carry `rpoPolicyId`/`retentionPolicyId` (not one `policyId`) and the UI shows both when they differ. A single shared `policy` made retention findings cite the unrelated RPO winner.
- **The two silences are the design.** A backup whose source cannot be determined is **unattributable**, never an orphan — reported in its own count so "we found no orphans" and "we couldn't tell" don't render the same. That covers three cases: no source field, an empty one (Hetzner sets `bound_to` on automatic backups only), and an **ambiguous** match (two claimants ⇒ no match, the dependency-graph and DNS rule — but here it must additionally not fall through to "orphan", or we would invite deleting a live backup). And an automated-backup field whose value we don't recognise is neither clearing nor flagging: `parseFlag` is three-valued. `identityTokens` is deduped — a resource whose id, external id and display name are one string would otherwise read as three claimants, i.e. ambiguous, i.e. unmatched (this bit during development and every join silently failed).
- **Attribution is by declared field only.** `parentResourceId` is deliberately _not_ consulted: it is a containment link (a DO snapshot's parent is the project, a Spanner backup's is the instance), so reading it as "what this protects" attributes every snapshot to the wrong thing.
- **Declarations** — DO `snapshot`→`droplet`/`volume` (`resourceId`; no `createdKey`, the lister never puts `created_at` in `fields`), hetzner `image`(narrowed to `backup`/`snapshot`)→`server`, gcp `spanner-backup`(`{instance}/{database}`)→`spanner-database`, neon `neon-snapshot`(`{projectId}/{sourceBranchId}`)→`neon-branch`, planetscale `ps-backup`(`{databaseName}/{branchName}`, `sizeUnit: "bytes"`)→`ps-branch`, azure `azure-postgres-flexible`/`azure-mysql-flexible` (`retentionDaysFieldKey` only — no listable snapshot type). **AWS is deliberately absent**: there is no `ebs-snapshot`/`rds-snapshot` resource type at all and `rds-instance` never syncs `BackupRetentionPeriod`, so a declaration would report every EBS volume as unprotected. Also skipped: DO `image` (droplet backups land there with no droplet-id field), aws `backup-vault` (a container with no source), gcp `spanner-database.versionRetentionPeriod` (a PITR _version_ window `"3600s"`, not backup retention — reading it as days would fail every database), planetscale `ps-branch.production` (says a branch _should_ be backed up, not that it is), neon `neon-snapshot.size` (a pre-formatted `"1.2 GB"` string).
- **The per-org policy limit is enforced under `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('backup_policies:<org>'))` inside the insert's transaction** — the `synthetic_probes`/`resource_schedules` stance. Count-then-insert as two statements lets two concurrent creates both take the last slot, and under READ COMMITTED even a single `INSERT … SELECT … WHERE count < limit` would not close it, since each transaction counts against its own snapshot. Serializing per org is the only version that holds. `src/__tests__/backups-store.test.ts` asserts it structurally (lock first, count and insert on the `tx` handle, never on the outer `db`) because the suite has no Postgres to race against.
- **Org policies** — `backup_policies` (`server-core/src/db/backup-schema.ts`): selector by resource type (comma-joined text, the sync path's call for short opaque ids) and/or one tag, plus `max_rpo_hours` / `min_retention_days`, `enabled` (the schedules `paused` stance — turning a noisy objective off must not cost you the policy). Both selector halves empty = every stateful resource. Several policies on one resource resolve to the **strictest** (shortest RPO, longest retention) so two policies can't produce two contradictory findings. Validation is `validateBackupPolicyInput` in client-core, shared with the editors (the `probes/store.ts` rule), and mirrored as CHECK constraints.
- **Server** — `server-core/src/backups/{feed,store}.ts`, mirroring `posture/feed.ts`. The one non-Postgres read is billing: orphaned backups get a trailing-30-day quote through `getResourceCostTotals`, best-effort with the schedules/orphans annotation rules (ClickHouse down ⇒ every cost **null, never zero**; mixed currencies ⇒ quote dropped). `GET /backups` takes `resources:read`; `GET|POST|PATCH|DELETE /backups/policies*` take `org:settings:write` — the posture-settings split: a member can see that a database is unprotected and deliberately cannot relax the target that says so. Audited `backup_policy.{create,update,delete}`. PATCH validates **after** merging, since "would this leave the policy demanding nothing" is invisible from the patch alone.
- **Surfaces** — Backups workspace tab on web/desktop (shared `BackupsSection` in `ui/src/backups/`, `BackupsIcon`, tab kind `"backups"` — full tab recipe incl. validate-tabs chain + `TabTarget` enum + route stubs + both `routeTree.gen.ts`), three views (Gaps / Coverage / Policies) behind one fetch. Desktop is **cloud-only** (`cloud_backups`, `cloud_backup_polic*` IPC ×5, Changes/Costs-style "sign in" panel in local mode): the coverage could be computed locally, but the objectives it is judged against are org state with nowhere to live in a single-machine workspace, and a screen that could only ever say "there is a backup" is the wrong half. Weekly digest gains a Backups line (`skipCosts: true` — the digest wants risk counts, not a spend quote). **No alert channel, deliberately**: a backup gap is a standing condition, not an event, and a nightly "you still have no backups" trains people to ignore alerts.
- Not done, listed in the PR: mobile parity, an `infrawrench backups` CLI command, MCP tools, a per-resource Backups panel on the detail view, and the AWS lister fields that would unlock the biggest provider.

### Domains surface + dangling DNS (`dns-dangling-target`)

One cross-provider view of every DNS zone and record, with each record target judged against the rest of the workspace. Same declaration-over-synced-state contract as `orphanRule` / `expiryFields` / `postureChecks` — **never an extra provider API call, and never a DNS resolution**. A record's target is judged against what we synced, not against what a resolver answers now. API_VERSION 0.38.0 → **0.39.0**; no migration, no new table (deliberately — the whole feature is a view plus one computed posture rule).

- **Contract** (`plugin-base/src/resource.ts`, Zod in `validation/resource.schema.ts`): two new `ResourceTypeDefinition` fields.
  - `dnsRole?: DnsRoleDeclaration` — `{ role: "zone", domainKey?, recordCountKey?, statusKey?, privateKey?, privateValues?, isPrivate? }` or `{ role: "record", nameKey?, typeKey?, contentKey?, ttlKey?, priorityKey?, proxiedKey?, zoneKey? }`. Every key defaults to the name most providers already use, so Cloudflare's record type needs only `zoneKey`/`proxiedKey`. `privateValues` without `privateKey`, and `isPrivate` alongside `privateKey`, fail the manifest (the `maxAgeDays` / `runningValues` stance). A plain `z.union`, not `discriminatedUnion` — a refined option isn't a valid discriminated-union member in zod 3.
  - `dnsServiceHosts?: DnsServiceHostRule[]` — `{ id, label, hostPattern, labelIs?: "name"|"opaque", hostKeys?, severity?, reason }`. `hostPattern` is a regex source the host anchors itself (`^(?:…)$`, case-insensitive); capture group 1 must be the instance-identifying label. Validated like `sizeFamilyPattern`: must compile and must have ≥1 capture group. `labelIs: "opaque"` without `hostKeys` fails — nothing could ever claim a provider-minted hostname, so every record into the namespace would read as dangling.
- **Declarations**. Zones/records: cloudflare `zone`/`dns-record`, aws `route53-hosted-zone`/`route53-record-set` (`contentKey: "values"`, comma-joined → one target per element), gcp `cloud-dns-zone` (`domainKey: "dnsName"`, trailing dot stripped) / `cloud-dns-record-set` (`rrdatas`), digitalocean `domain`/`dns-record` (`data`, relative names + `@` qualified against the zone), netlify `netlify-dns-zone`/`netlify-dns-record`, azure `azure-dns-zone` + `azure-private-dns-zone` (`isPrivate: true` — the type _is_ the answer, there's no field), vercel `vercel-domain` (zone only; the API lists domains, not records). Namespaces: aws `s3-bucket` (all REST + website endpoint forms in one pattern) and `cloudfront-distribution` (`labelIs: "opaque"`, `hostKeys: ["domainName"]`), gcp `gcs-bucket`, azure `azure-storage-account`/`azure-app-service`, digitalocean `spaces-bucket`, netlify `netlify-site` (`hostKeys` on URL fields — the host reduces a URL to its host), vercel `vercel-project`, fly `app`, cloudflare `worker` (`workers.dev`). **Deliberately not declared**: `.elb.amazonaws.com` and `*.run.app` — their claimant hostname is an _output_, not a field, so nothing could claim them and every legitimate record would flag; `.r2.dev` (opaque hash, no field).
- **Computation** (`client-core/src/dns.ts`, pure + unit-tested): `computeDnsInventory(plugins+accounts+resources, {now?})` → `{ zones, records, counts, skippedNamespaces, generatedAt }`. Classifications `owned | dangling | external | not-analysed`; a record's `status` is the worst of its targets, and counts are **per record, not per target** ("3 dangling records" is what a reader acts on). Analysed types: A/AAAA/CNAME/ALIAS/ANAME/NS — MX/SRV/TXT/SOA/CAA are listed but never judged (priority/port prefixes would need per-type parsing, and a half-parsed target is worse than an unjudged one). Identity matching reuses `IDENTITY_FIELD_KEYS` **exported from `dependency-inference.ts`** rather than re-listing it — a second definition would make a record read owned on the canvas and dangling here — including its uniqueness rule (two claimants = ambiguous = not owned). Zone attribution: `parentResourceId` first (written by the sync path, no guessing), declared `zoneKey` as fallback, matched against both the zone's external id and its domain.
- **Why `dangling` is hard to reach** (the load-bearing design decision). A namespace is only evaluated when the org has a synced **account** for the plugin **and** ≥1 synced **resource** of the declaring type — no account means we can't tell your bucket from a stranger's, no synced claimant means an empty account is indistinguishable from a lister that lacks `s3:ListAllMyBuckets`. Skips are returned in `skippedNamespaces` and rendered on every surface: "we found nothing" and "we didn't look" must not render the same. Residual false positive, stated in the finding text: a bucket in an account you haven't connected reads as dangling. **Not implemented**: the released-Elastic-IP case. Addresses carry no namespace to check against, and there is no history table, so an A record whose IP matches nothing synced is `external`, not dangling — flagging it would flag most A records.
- **The posture bridge** — `computePostureFindings(input, { dns })`: the one built-in, cross-resource finding (`DANGLING_DNS_RULE_ID = "dns-dangling-target"`, category `public-exposure`, severity from the namespace rule, default high). Passed in rather than computed inside posture, because every other rule there is a pure per-resource predicate. One finding per record against the _record's_ resource id, so the surface links to the row you edit. **This is why there is no new alert channel**: dangling records ride the existing posture fan-out and the existing `/posture/settings` toggle. Hosts that call `computePostureFindings` without `dns` get the declarative rules alone.
- **Server**: `server-core/src/dns/feed.ts` (mirrors `posture/feed.ts`; needed `resources.parentResourceId` added to the posture feed's select too). `GET /api/org/:orgId/dns` (`resources:read`). `posture/feed.ts` computes the inventory over the _same_ row snapshot rather than calling `dns/feed` — two queries could disagree about what is synced.
- **Surfaces**: Domains workspace tab on web/desktop (shared `DnsSection` in `ui/src/dns/`, `DomainsIcon`, tab kind `"dns"` — full tab recipe incl. validate-tabs chain + `TabTarget` enum + route stubs + regenerated routeTree.gen.ts), desktop local mode (`electron/local-dns.ts` for the CLI, `src/lib/local-dns.ts` for the renderer, `cloud_dns` IPC). Both local-mode `local-posture.ts` files were rewritten to read rows through `local-dns.ts`'s `localScanRows()` — one query, one snapshot, feeding both scans (its `LocalScanRows` carries the plugins' full `ResourceTypeDefinition`s, since neither scan's narrowed view satisfies the other). Also mobile `features/dns/`, `infrawrench dns` CLI (`--json`/`--local`, zero new deps), `list_dns_records` MCP tool (`status`/`domain` filters, whole-inventory counts), docs `features/domains.md`.
- **Registry tests** (`server-core/__tests__/plugin-loader.test.ts`): `dnsRole` keys must be declared fields — including the _defaulted_ record keys (`name`/`type`/`content`), since a type that stores its target elsewhere and forgets to say so yields a record with no target; `dnsServiceHosts` patterns must compile with a capture group, `hostKeys` must be declared fields, opaque rules must have `hostKeys`, and rule ids must be unique per plugin.

### Sleep/wake schedules (cloud-only)

Off-at/on-at weekly windows on resources whose plugin declares a lifecycle start/stop pair. Migration `0060_bumpy_famine.sql`; API_VERSION 0.31.0 → **0.32.0**.

- **Contract** (`plugin-base/src/resource.ts`): `lifecycle?: { startActionId, stopActionId, statusFieldKey?, runningValues?, stoppedValues? }` on `ResourceTypeDefinition`, Zod-validated (identical action ids fail the manifest; value lists without `statusFieldKey` are dead config and fail too). The action ids are ordinary `plugin-action` invoke-actions the type's `renderDetail` already offers — eligibility is discovered from the declaration, never from provider names. Declared on 12 types across 10 plugins: aws `ec2-instance`/`rds-instance` (new invokeAction: EC2 Start/StopInstances, RDS Start/StopDBInstance), gcp `gce-instance` (new `setGceInstancePower`), azure `azure-vm` (start/**deallocate** — the one that stops billing), digitalocean `droplet` (existing `power_on`/`power_off` — hard off, not ACPI shutdown), hetzner `server` (poweron/poweroff), scaleway `instance` (reuses `serverAction`), fly `machine` (start/stop), neon `neon-endpoint` (start/suspend — a connection also wakes it), together `endpoint` (existing pair), clickhouse `ch-service` (state PATCH factored out of deleteResource). Docker deliberately not declared: the cloud poller can't reach a local daemon. Fixed alongside: the `plugin-action` Zod arm was silently stripping `destructive` (freeze-gating read it from re-validated schemas).
- **Shared math** (`client-core/src/schedules.ts`, pure + unit-tested): `computeUpcomingTransitions`/`computeNextTransition`/`computeMostRecentTransition` enumerate **calendar days in the schedule's IANA zone** (never fixed 24h steps — DST days are 23/25h) and convert wall times with the two-pass `Intl` offset trick (`wallTimeToUtc`); `weeklyOffFraction` integrates a cyclic-week state machine (handles overnight on-windows and unselected days with no special cases); `projectedMonthlySaving` = trailing window normalized by `AVERAGE_DAYS_PER_MONTH` × fraction, null when billing holds nothing (never $0.00). Server imports the same functions the editors preview with, so "next transition" can't disagree between poller and UI.
- **DB** — `resource_schedules`: timing columns + `paused` + `next_transition_at` (due time AND claim lease, the `accounts.next_poll_at` protocol) + `last_transition_key` (`"<ISO>:<stop|start>"` idempotency record) + `last_run_at/action/status/error` (never silent; status `ok | failed | skipped_freeze`). Unique per (org, resource) — two windows on one VM would fight. `resource_changes` gained a nullable `origin` column (`"schedule"`; null = sync-observed).
- **Executor** (`server-core/src/schedules/pass.ts`, poller **eighth** pass, limit 4/tick): claim via `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` lease (10 min), recompute the due transition from timing, skip if its key matches `last_transition_key` (restart-safe; downtime executes only the **latest** missed transition — the one deciding current desired state). Freeze check via `findActiveChangeFreeze` — moved to `server-core/src/change-freezes.ts` so the poller needn't import web; web's `getActiveChangeFreeze` now delegates. A frozen window is **skipped and key-spent** (logged + surfaced as `skipped_freeze`), never deferred to fire when the freeze lifts. Already-in-desired-state rows (per `statusFieldKey`/values) complete without invoking. Success writes the declared desired value into the stored row's `fieldsJson` and records an `origin: "schedule"` timeline event — the next sync sees the expected state, so a scheduled stop never reads as drift, and the feed shows "via schedule". Failures log `[schedules]`, land in `last_run_error`, and retry on a 10-min lease until the transition is 1h old, then spend the key and move on.
- **API** — `/api/org/:orgId/schedules` CRUD + `POST /preview` (off fraction, monthly cost from 30d `getResourceCostTotals` matched on externalId — the orphans annotation rules: mixed-currency dropped, ClickHouse failure → null quote — and next transitions). Reads `resources:read`, mutations `resources:write` (a schedule is a standing `invoke-action`). Audit `resource_schedule.{create,update,delete}`. `schedulable` flag on the resource-detail payload + account resource-types metadata; `origin` on both change-feed responses. MCP `list_schedules`/`create_schedule`.
- **Surfaces** — shared `ui/src/schedules/` (`SleepSchedulesSection` on `CostsPanel` under Potential savings, `ScheduleEditorModal` with debounced live preview quote, `ResourceSchedulePanel` behind a new host-driven `renderScheduleTab` on `DetailView` — the `renderChangesTab` pattern). Web wires both (`lib/schedules-client.ts`); desktop cloud-mode only (`cloud_schedules_*` IPC ×5, plugin-def lifecycle check in `-DetailViewContainer`); mobile `features/schedules/` is the Costs-tab list with **pause/resume only** (editing day/time/zone pickers stays web/desktop — the anomaly-tuning line); `infrawrench schedules` CLI (`--json`). `Intl.supportedValuesOf` is feature-detected in the editor (newer than the ES2022 lib) with a free-text zone fallback.
- Gotcha found en route: `ui`'s tsdown dts rollup **drops names re-exported by two sibling modules under one barrel** (`cost/config.ts` + `cost/types.ts` both re-exporting `ShowbackReport` etc. made `export *` treat them as ambiguous after chunking) — the cost contract re-exports now live only in `config.ts`.

### Resource leases (TTL, cloud-only)

An optional "expires at" on **any** resource — "give me a test cluster for 3 days" — that nags the owner through the expiry radar and can optionally auto-delete the resource at expiry.

- **Schema** — `resource_leases` (`server-core/src/db/schema.ts`, the `resource_schedules` sidecar stance: `resource_id` is NOT a FK, cleanup rides the account cascade, UNIQUE (org, resource) — an _active_ lease 409s on create, a terminal row (`deleted | failed | canceled`) is replaced in place so the unique index never blocks a fresh lease). `display_name` is denormalized so completion messages can name the resource after it's gone. `next_check_at` is the due-time column AND the claim lease (null = due); `first_warning_at`/`final_warning_at` record the two mandatory announcements.
- **Feed merge** — `expiry/feed.ts` reads active lease rows alongside the resource scan and merges them as kind-`"lease"` `ExpiryItem`s via `mergeExpiryItems` (client-core, re-applies the evaluator's exact sort + counts). `ExpiryKind` gained `"lease"` (plugin-base union only — deliberately **absent from the Zod enum**, a plugin can't declare it); `ExpiryItem` gained optional `leaseAutoDelete` so surfaces can badge "this one actually gets deleted". Everything downstream inherits leases for free: Expiring tabs (web/desktop/mobile), `infrawrench expiring`, `list_expiring` MCP, weekly digest, and the daily expiry alert pass.
- **Store** — `server-core/src/leases/store.ts` (CRUD + wire assembly; validation shared with the editors via client-core's `validateLeaseInput`/`LEASE_LIMITS` — 365d max horizon, 500-char note, 200/org). Editing the deadline or the auto-delete flag **re-arms** the announcement schedule (clears warning stamps + `next_check_at`), which also invalidates any in-flight pass claim.
- **Pass** — `server-core/src/leases/pass.ts` (poller pass after schedules, limit 4/tick), claims via the `next_transition_at` protocol (10-min lease, claim-token guard on completions). Pure timing in `leases/timing.ts` (unit-tested, `lease-timing.test.ts`): warning 1 at ~72h before expiry, warning 2 at ~24h, proportionally compressed for short leases (2/3 and 11/12 of lifetime; ≤24h leases warn immediately + at half-life), and **`nextLeaseStep` never yields `delete` until both warnings are stamped AND expiry passed** — a recovering poller pushes the delete later rather than skipping an announcement (min 10-min gap between late warnings). Announcements + outcomes fan out on the **`expiryAlerts`** trigger (push/Slack/Teams; the warning is stamped before fan-out so a transport outage can't re-announce forever). At delete time: change freeze → **defer** (bump `next_check_at` + record in `last_error`; unlike a missed sleep-window the delete is still wanted after the freeze, so defer, never skip, never silently execute); resource already gone → complete quietly; otherwise `ctx.client.deleteResource` via `getOrgAccountClient`, retried every 10 min for ~1h then `status = "failed"` + notification (never silent). Success soft-deletes the stored resource row (best-effort), writes a poller-side `audit_logs` row (`resource_lease.auto_delete` / `.give_up` — userId = lease creator), and sends "Lease expired: X was deleted". Nag-only leases are never claimed (the claim filters `auto_delete = true`).
- **API** — `/api/org/:orgId/leases`: GET / + GET /resource?resourceId= (`resources:read`), POST / + PUT /:id + POST /:id/cancel + DELETE /:id (`resources:write`); **setting `autoDelete: true` additionally requires `resources:delete`** (checked in the handler — the REQUIRED_PERMISSION map is one-permission-per-route). Audit `resource_lease.{create,update,cancel,delete}`. Wire contract + Bearer helpers in `client-core/src/leases.ts`.
- **Surfaces** — `ui/src/leases/` (`ResourceLeasePanel` behind a new host-driven `renderLeaseTab` on `DetailView` — unlike `renderScheduleTab` it isn't gated on a plugin declaration, any resource can carry a TTL; `LeaseEditorModal` with 1d/3d/1w/30d presets + custom datetime + note + the auto-delete opt-in copy "You'll be warned twice first. Freezes pause deletion."). Web wires it always (`lib/leases-client.ts`); desktop cloud-mode only (`cloud_leases_*` IPC ×6). `ExpirySection` badges `leaseAutoDelete` items red. `infrawrench leases` CLI (`--output json`). **Mobile deliberately has no lease editor** — leases render in its Expiring screen via the feed; setting/editing them is a web/desktop task (see the mobile omissions list up top).

### Log workspace (multi-resource tail, saved queries, alert on match)

Tail several log-capable resources in one pane, search across the merged stream, save the set-up per-org, optionally alert when a line matches. New table `log_workspace_queries` + a `log_match_alerts` boolean on `push_preferences`/`slack_channels`/`msteams_webhooks`.

- **Shared pure half** (`client-core/src/log-workspaces.ts`, unit-tested): `compileLogSearch` (empty = match-all; `/re/`+`/re/i` regex; otherwise AND-ed case-insensitive terms with `"phrases"` and `-negation`; invalid regex → `{error, test: never}` rather than a throw), `evaluateLogMatches` (newest-first scan, hard `matchCap`, clipped samples), `computeAppendedLines` (largest prev-suffix/next-prefix overlap — the tail-window diff the interleaved view appends with), `validateLogWorkspaceQuery`, `LOG_WORKSPACE_LIMITS` (100 queries/org, 8 streams/query, 400-line alert tail, 25-match cap, 30-min cooldown, 5-min eval interval), wire types + Bearer fetch helpers. The workspace filter box and the server-side alert pass compile the same expression, so "matches" can never disagree.
- **Log-capable discovery**: the `logs` capability lives on the _rendered_ `DetailViewSchema` (per instance in `renderDetail`), never on the type — so `GET /log-workspaces/resources` (web service `services/log-workspaces.ts`) instantiates one client per account, skips accounts whose client lacks `getLogs`, and `renderDetail`s each stored row in memory (cheap, sync; cap 500). Desktop local mode does the same scan over local SQLite + in-renderer plugin clients (`lib/log-workspace-client.ts`) — never a hardcoded provider list.
- **Sidecar streams** (added after prod showed "no log-capable resources" for a GKE + DO + CF org — pods aren't stored rows, so the stored-row scan can't ever see them): discovery also walks stored parents whose type declares `peerIntegrations`, builds the peer plugin's client through the host's own path (web/poller: `getClientForResource`; desktop local: `createPeerPluginClient`), and lists peer resources whose rendered detail declares `logs`. The walk lives once in `client-core/src/log-discovery.ts` (`discoverSidecarLogStreams`, injected data access, unit-tested) with two caches: log-capable type ids per **peer plugin** (10-min TTL — one probe of one cluster describes `kubernetes` everywhere, and `postgres`-style peers never rebuild a client just to rediscover they lack `getLogs`) and build-failure backoff per **(parent, plugin)** (30 s — one broken cluster must not hide the org's other clusters). Per-parent 10 s timeout with an abort flag (`withTimeout` can't cancel the promise, so the walker checks the flag at every loop boundary before mutating shared state); every skip goes through `deps.warn`, never silent. `LogStreamSelector` gained optional `parentResourceId`; it rides the existing sidecar plumbing end-to-end (`POST /resources/:pluginId/:typeId/logs` and desktop's `cloud_get_logs` already forwarded it), `logStreamKey` includes it (two clusters in one account can both run `kube-system/coredns-x`), store validation anchors on the **parent** row (the pod is never a stored row), and the alert pass resolves sidecar clients via `getClientForResource` cached per (account, parent) with stream names `cluster/ns:pod`. Sidecar chips aren't clickable (no detail page); pods being ephemeral means a saved query over a replaced pod errors until re-picked — tailing the workload type instead follows whichever pod is running (k8s `getLogs` resolves via label selector at fetch time).
- **DB/store** — `log_workspace_queries`: name (unique per org — the reopen handle), `resources` jsonb selector array (`{resourceId, accountId, pluginId, resourceTypeId, container?}`, not FKs — the `resource_changes` stance), `search`, `alertEnabled`, `next_eval_at` (due time AND claim lease, the schedules protocol; null while alerts off), `last_eval_at/match_at/alerted_at/eval_error/match_sample`. Store (`server-core/src/log-workspaces/store.ts`) validates via the shared helpers, rejects `alertEnabled` with a match-all expression (would fire forever), advisory-lock + unique-index create like schedules; editing the search/resources resets eval state and makes the query due immediately.
- **Alert pass** (`server-core/src/log-workspaces/pass.ts`, poller **ninth** pass, limit 4/tick, `[log-match]` log prefix): claim due rows `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (10-min lease; the lease instant is the claim token every completion write is guarded on, so edits beat stale runs), fetch `alertTailLines` per stream through `getOrgAccountClient` + `client.getLogs` (one client per account per run), evaluate capped, dispatch push/Slack/Teams under the **eighth trigger `logMatchAlerts`** (`PushData` variant `type: "log_match"` with `queryId`/`matchCount`; deep-links `/org/{orgId}/log-workspaces` web, the query viewer on mobile). Cooldown via `last_alerted_at`, stamped only when a channel actually delivered (the anomaly rule). Per-stream failures aggregate into `last_eval_error` — surfaced in every list, never silent. Tests: `server-core/src/__tests__/log-alert-pass.test.ts` (expiry-alerts mocking pattern).
- **API** — `/api/org/:orgId/log-workspaces` CRUD + `/resources` discovery. Reads `resources:read` (a saved query is a view over logs that permission already gates), mutations `resources:write` — the schedules stance; no new permission family. Audit `log_workspace_query.{create,update,delete}`.
- **Surfaces** — shared `ui/src/logs/LogWorkspacePanel.tsx` (host-injected `LogWorkspaceClient`; `savedQueries` sub-client **optional — undefined hides the saved-query bar**, which is how desktop local mode degrades): per-stream colored chips + container selects, interleaved (arrival-order merge, 3 000-line buffer) or split stacked panes, pause, unified filter, saved-query bar with alert toggle + last-eval status. New workspace-tab kind `"logs"` (`logsTabTarget`; the full 8-step chain — ui.store switches, both `workspace-tabs.ts`, both `WorkspaceTabsViewport`s, both route stubs, desktop `__root` revalidator kind list). Web `lib/log-workspace-client.ts`; desktop dual-mode client + `cloud_log_workspaces_*` IPC ×5 (`electron/cloud-data/log-workspaces.ts`, preload allowlist), stream fetches ride the existing `cloud_get_logs`; sidebar "Logs" tile on both hosts (desktop: both modes, the Graph/Expiring precedent). Mobile: read-only saved-query list + viewer (`features/log-workspaces/`, nested stack `org/[orgId]/log-workspaces/`), entered from the Accounts tab card and from a `log_match` push. No CLI subcommand (a live tail pane doesn't fit `--json` line output; the saved-query listing alone wasn't worth a command — revisit if asked).

### Cloud-credit burndown (cross-plugin, cloud-only)

Prepaid balances with a measured burn rate and a runway. API_VERSION 0.42.0 → **0.43.0**; migration `0076_easy_professor_monster` (three tables).

- **Its own capability, not part of `costs`.** `costs` is "what did this account spend" — a dimensioned, backfillable series. `credits` is "how much is left in the pot", which most providers do not have at all and the ones that do usually expose _instead_ of a cost API (DeepSeek's manifest literally says a cost chart built from its balance snapshot would be a fabrication). And running a prepaid pot to zero is an **outage**: the API stops answering. Contract in `plugin-base/src/credits.ts` — `CreditsCapabilityDeclaration` on the manifest, `fetchCreditBalance()` on the client, `CreditAccessError` for "your credential can't see this" (a permission gap, presented differently from a failure).
- **Wired for three plugins, and only three**: deepseek (`GET /user/balance`, per-currency), openrouter (`GET /credits`, needs a _provisioning_ key — declared via `requiresElevatedCredential`), deepgram (`/v1/projects/{id}/balances`, per project, admin/owner-scoped). All three shapes were already verified in-repo by existing resource types rather than taken from memory. DigitalOcean, Hetzner, Fly and the rest bill in arrears and have no pot.
- **The burn is measured by us, from our own series** — no provider reports one. `credits/burn.ts` is pure and heavily tested because it is the part that must not lie: **burn is the sum of the decreases between consecutive readings**, never `first - last`. A pot that went 500 → 300 → 900 → 700 burned 400 and was topped up 600; the naive answer is −200, i.e. a negative burn and an infinite runway — the most dangerous possible wrong answer, delivered with confidence. Top-ups are counted and surfaced separately, and the row says one happened inside the window.
- **Three refusals to guess**, all tested: below `MIN_BURN_SPAN_DAYS` (3) `perDay` is **null**, never 0 — two readings across a quiet night extrapolate to years, across a busy afternoon to days, and neither is a rate. A pot nothing has drawn on returns `neverEmpties`, never `Infinity`. And `runwayUrgency` maps an unmeasurable burn to `"unknown"`, never to `"ok"` — silence must not read as a pass.
- **The runway is bounded by the burn _and_ the credit's own expiry, earlier wins.** A trial grant lapsing in nine days does not have a 200-day runway however slowly it is spent, and that is exactly the case where the warning matters most. `limitedByExpiry` says which constraint bound it.
- **Storage is three tables**: `account_credit_balances` (latest per `(account, pot)` — one indexed read for the panel), `account_credit_snapshots` (the series the burn is derived from; a year retained via the poller's hourly slot, because the rows are tiny), and `account_credit_polls` (schedule + last error). The poll state is its own table rather than four more columns on `accounts` because credit-capable plugins are a small minority — the due-work query scans a tiny table instead of the whole account set, and the claim is an `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` over a LEFT JOIN so an account with no row yet is due immediately.
- **Every collection appends a snapshot even when the number has not moved**: a flat stretch is evidence of an idle account, and dropping it would make an idle pot indistinguishable from an uncollected one. A pot the provider stops reporting is **deleted** rather than frozen at its last reading — a stale "$40 remaining" is worse than nothing because somebody will plan against it.
- **Cadence is twice daily**, denser than costs (a pot is a live gauge; an account that starts burning hard shouldn't look fine for a day) but not much denser, since a denser series does not make a 30-day rate more accurate and these are the same rate-limited management APIs.
- **Surfaces**: `ui/src/cost/CreditBurndownSection.tsx` on the Costs panel above the savings sections (those are about spending less, this about not stopping), rendering **nothing** when the org has no credit-capable account. New optional `CostsClient.getCreditBurndown` + a new `CostsPanel` `onOpenExternal` prop (the provider's top-up page and the permission-gap help link; desktop routes it to the system browser). Desktop `cloud_credit_burndown` IPC + preload allowlist. `infrawrench credits [--json]`. Permission is `costs:read`. Docs: `features/credit-burndown.md`.

### Credential hygiene (cloud-only)

Unused API keys, unreferenced SSH keys, and members holding write permissions they never exercise — entirely from Postgres we already own. No provider call, no new table, no background pass. API_VERSION 0.41.0 → **0.42.0**; no migration.

- **The whole feature turns on one honest constraint: the audit log only witnesses writes.** Reads leave no row by design, so an absence of evidence says something real about `resources:delete` and nothing about `resources:read`. `hygiene/action-permissions.ts` is the audit-action → permission table and `WITNESSED_PERMISSIONS` is its value set; the "granted but unused" finding is computed **only** over that set, on both sides of the subtraction. A test asserts every mapped value is a real catalog permission and that the only `:read` permissions in the witnessed set are `secrets:read` and `session-recordings:read` — the two reads that genuinely are logged, because both are disclosures.
- **Exact matches, not prefixes.** `resource.update` → `resources:write` and `resource.delete` → `resources:delete`; a prefix rule over `resource.` would collapse exactly the distinction a reviewer cares about.
- **Second honesty guard: window vs. history.** An org three days old has three days of audit history and "unused in 90 days" is meaningless against it, so `buildHygieneReport` measures `min(audit_logs.created_at)` and **withholds** the unused-permission findings below 30 days, reporting `permissionFindingsWithheld` rather than a clean bill it has not earned. Every surface (page, CLI, `--json`) carries that flag.
- **Owners are skipped** in the member finding: an org must have one, `*` is the role by definition, and "the owner didn't exercise `billing:write`" is not actionable. Severity follows the _ratio_ of unused-to-granted, not the raw count — using none of a broad grant is a different conversation from using most of it.
- **API-key evidence** comes from `audit_logs.api_key_id` (stamped by the key auth path), and a key's actions are the key's evidence, **not** its owner's — the member query excludes rows with an `api_key_id` for exactly that reason, or a cron firing under a forgotten key would keep its owner looking active.
- **SSH-key evidence** is `metadata->>'sshKeyId'`, the shape shared by `ssh.exec`, `ssh.fanout.run` and `ssh.agent.session_opened`. That set had a hole: the interactive terminal proxy only logged agent-forwarded sessions, so an ordinary web SSH session left no trace and the report would have confidently called a working key unused. `ssh-proxy.ts` now emits **`ssh.session.opened`** for every session opened with an org key. No `last_used_at` column was added to `ssh_keys` deliberately: that buys a scan-free lookup at the cost of a write on the terminal's critical path, for a report nobody runs hot.
- **Permission is `audit:read`, not a family of its own** — every fact is already reachable by hand for anyone who can read the audit log, so a new permission would only mean granting two things to get one view. The report is a lens, not a disclosure.
- **Nothing is auto-revoked.** Deciding a key is dead is a judgement call with a blast radius; a report that acted on it would eventually take out something load-bearing at 3am. Each finding gets a "Fix" jump to the owning settings section instead.
- **Surfaces**: shared `ui/src/settings/CredentialHygieneSection.tsx` (window switcher 30/90/180/365, the read-caveat stated on every render — not just when something is flagged), web route wrapper, desktop allowlist entry, `infrawrench hygiene [--days N]` whose `--json` is the form worth scheduling. Docs: `team-and-billing/credential-hygiene.md`.

### Break-glass access — time-boxed permission elevation (cloud-only)

Ask for specific permissions, for specific minutes, with a reason; someone else approves; the grant lapses on its own. Built on the approval primitive `workflow_approvals` established, generalized one step. API_VERSION 0.40.0 → **0.41.0**; migration `0075_famous_arclight` (one table).

- **The generalization is the fan-out, not the table.** `approvals/notify.ts` (`fanOutApprovalRequest`) was extracted from `workflows/approvals.ts` when access requests became the second caller: push + Slack (buttons, tracked so a decision retires every copy) + Teams, parameterized on wording and deep link. The SMS leg stayed in the workflow module — it is throttled on a key only that caller knows. `SlackApprovalKind` gained `"access"` (a `$type` widening; the column was already `text`) and `slack-inbound.ts` routes it, passing the Slack decider's live permissions so the button obeys the same rules as the HTTP route. Everything rides the existing `workflowPages` opt-in, on the argument that module already makes.
- **One table, not two.** A grant is not a separate object from the request that produced it — splitting them would let a grant exist whose request said something else, which is exactly what an auditor is checking. `access_requests` is the request, the decision, and the window in one row; live means `status='approved' AND revoked_at IS NULL AND granted_at <= now < grant_expires_at`.
- **Three rules, all enforced server-side and all tested** (`__tests__/break-glass.test.ts`): (1) you cannot decide your own request — checked for denials too, because withdrawal is its own operation with its own audit action; (2) you cannot grant what you do not hold (`isSubsetOfCallerPerms` against the _approver's_ live set), though you may always deny something aimed higher than you, or over-ambitious requests would strand; (3) **the window is evaluated, never swept** — a sweeper that fell behind would be a sweeper that extended everyone's access.
- **The hook is `resolveEffectivePermissions`** (server-core `permissions/resolver.ts`), which is why an elevation reaches all five surfaces at once — HTTP middleware, the WS gateway via `POST /ws-token`, chat, MCP tools, step-up — without any of them knowing it exists. `EffectiveAccess` gained `elevations`, kept _beside_ `permissions` rather than folded into `role.permissions`: the role editor must not show a permission nobody assigned that vanishes an hour later. The elevation read runs concurrently with the role read (independent queries, union result) and fails **closed**.
- **API keys pass `includeElevation: false`** (`web/src/auth/effective-permissions.ts`). A grant is authority handed to a person for a bounded window on a stated reason; an unattended key they minted last quarter is none of those. Same for `custom-graph-host.ts` — a saved graph runs long after its author wrote it, and one that captured an elevation would keep exercising it on every render. Those two are the only call sites that should ever set it; every other caller is a live human request.
- **Permissions**: new family `access:read` / `access:request` / `access:approve`. The first two are in the `member` system role (members are who this is for; an elevation regime nobody can see is not a control), `approve` is not — and is deliberately **not** implied by `team:role:write`, because granting a role is a considered change while approving an elevation happens mid-incident.
- **`revoke` has no `REQUIRED_PERMISSION` entry**, deliberately: the holder may always end their own grant, so the required permission depends on who is calling and the one-permission-per-route map cannot express it. The handler owns that check (the `leases` autoDelete precedent).
- **Surfaces**: shared `ui/src/settings/AccessRequestsSection.tsx` — one screen, not two, because the asker and the decider look at the same queue and splitting it means an approver has to go elsewhere to review what they granted. Web route wrapper + desktop `cloud_settings_request` allowlist entry. `/team/me` returns `elevations` so a client can show what is temporary and when it lapses. CLI `infrawrench access` / `access active`, read-only on purpose. Docs: `team-and-billing/break-glass-access.md`.
- **Mobile carries this one and not the other three**, and that asymmetry is the point: a colleague blocked mid-incident is the definition of something you decide from a pocket. `features/access/` mirrors the approvals-inbox pattern (native card, two-tap confirm naming who/what/how long/why, 15 s poll) and the new `access_request` push deep-links to it with `?requestId=`. **Deciding works; raising does not** — asking needs a permission picker that cannot drift from the catalog and a reason someone reads months later, neither of which is composed one-handed. Session recordings (no player worth having on a phone), credential hygiene (a review you do at a desk) and credit burndown (surfaced on the Costs panel, which mobile mirrors read-only) are deliberate omissions.

### SSH session recording & playback (cloud-only)

Cloud SSH is already proxied server-side (`web/src/services/ssh-proxy.ts` bridges the browser WebSocket to ssh2), so recording is a **tee on a stream we already hold** — no agent on the customer's host, which is the entire economics of the feature. API_VERSION 0.39.0 → **0.40.0**; migration `0074_smart_slipstream` (three tables, no data migration — the permissions are a brand-new family, so nothing needs grandfathering).

- **Format is asciinema's, not ours** (`server-core/src/ssh-recording/cast.ts`, pure + unit-tested): asciicast v2 — a JSON header line then `[elapsedSeconds, "o"|"i"|"r", data]` per line. A recording only our player can open is worth almost nothing to the auditor it exists for; a `.cast` plays in `asciinema play` and the reference web player. Event times are **rounded to 1 ms** so a long session's lines don't grow a 17-digit float tail. `parseCast` drops an unparseable line rather than failing the document (the realistic damage is a truncated final chunk) and throws only on an unreadable header.
- **Schema** (`db/ssh-recording-schema.ts`): `org_session_recording_settings` (opt-in `enabled`, separate `capture_input`, per-org `retention_days`), `ssh_session_recordings` (index row), `ssh_session_recording_chunks` (base64(gzip(event lines)), unique `(recording_id, seq)`). Two tables because the write pattern (append while live, never hold a session in memory) and the read pattern (one cheap row per session) differ; rewriting one growing column per flush is O(session²). `account_id`/`resource_id` are deliberately **not** FKs — an audit artifact has to outlive what it was taken against.
- **`capture_input` is a separate switch on purpose.** Output capture answers "what happened on this host" (the compliance question); input capture also records what was typed at a prompt the host chose not to echo — a sudo password, a pasted token. Different promise to the people being recorded, so a different decision.
- **The recorder is subordinate to the terminal** (`ssh-recording/recorder.ts`). Nothing throws (a failed write sets `disabled` and the session carries on); memory is bounded (flush at 64 KiB or 5 s); writes are serialized on one promise chain so `seq` is ordered without a lock; `onOutput` is called from an ssh2 `"data"` handler and only buffers. Per-session ceiling 32 MiB of output → status `truncated` (an honest partial beats an unbounded table). `startSessionRecording` returns **null** for every not-recording case — opted out, settings unreadable, insert failed — so the proxy has one branch and can't mistake a broken recorder for a working one. In the proxy the tee is always _second_ in each handler: the operator's byte reaches the browser first.
- **The recorder opens before `conn.shell`**, not alongside it: a prompt or MOTD arriving while the insert was in flight would be missing from the top of the tape.
