That makes shell-open asynchronous even when nothing is recorded (the ssh-proxy suite awaits `conn.shellCalled` rather than asserting it synchronously), so the policy read is behind a 30 s per-org TTL cache invalidated on write — an org with recording off must not pay a query on every connect just to learn that.
- **Statuses**: `recording` | `complete` | `truncated` | `abandoned`. A web replica killed mid-session never writes its closing update, so the list view _derives_ `abandoned` (still `recording` >2 min after `last_activity_at`) and the poller's hourly retention slot **also** settles it durably (`settleAbandonedRecordings` on the same clock), so a SQL-level reader agrees with the UI. Activity is **both chunk flushes and a 60s idle heartbeat** on the parent row — a quiet but still-open shell is not abandoned, and is never pruned under a live writer.
- **Retention is per-org**, unlike the change timeline's fixed 90 days: recording is a compliance control and the regime you're under sets the window. Rides the poller's existing hourly retention pass (`pruneSessionRecordings`) — one answer to "when does old data go", not two. Only orgs with a settings row are scanned, and disabling recording does **not** shorten the window on tapes you already have. **Never prunes `status = 'recording'`**, and never prunes a row whose `last_activity_at` is still inside the retention window.
- **Permissions**: new family `session-recordings:read` / `:write`, deliberately not `audit:read` or `ssh-keys:*` — watching a colleague's terminal is a sharper capability than either, and the holders (compliance, security) are often not the key administrators. **Absent from the `member` system role**; admin/owner get them free (catalog-derived). **Every read is audit-logged** (`session_recording.view`, plus `.delete` / `.settings.update`) — unusual here and deliberate: an investigator has to be able to answer who has watched a tape.
- **Playback is a replay, not a seek.** A terminal's screen at t is the product of every byte before t — there is no keyframe — so scrubbing resets the terminal and rewrites all output up to the target with delays removed (`castOutputThrough` in `client-core/src/session-recordings.ts`). Input events are never written back: they were never rendered by the host, and replaying them would inject text the session never showed. The clock is one rAF loop, not a timer per event (tens of thousands of events, microseconds apart).
- **Surfaces**: `ui/src/session-recordings/RecordingPlayer.tsx` owns the clock; the **terminal is host-injected** (`MountPlaybackTerminal`, duck-typed) so `@infrawrench/ui` stays xterm-free — web and desktop each hold a ~30-line `lib/playback-terminal.ts` adapter (no fit addon: the cast's own geometry is authoritative, reflowing 200 columns into 80 renders something the operator never saw). Settings section `session-recordings` on both platforms + a web route wrapper + the desktop `cloud_settings_request` allowlist entry. Two new host-contract members: `fetchText` (an asciicast is newline-delimited JSON — `api.get` would `JSON.parse` and die on the header line) and the optional `mountPlaybackTerminal` (a host without one gets the `.cast` download instead of a dead player). CLI `infrawrench recordings` / `recordings get <id>`, which writes the cast to stdout unframed so `| asciinema play -` works. Docs: `features/session-recording.md`.

### Shared consoles — pair-on-prod (cloud-only, web-only)

The other consumer of the position that made recording cheap. The proxy already holds both ends of the pty, so sharing is "fan the output out to more sockets, accept input from exactly one of them". API_VERSION 1.13.0 → **1.14.0**; migration `0104_shared_consoles` (two tables plus two attribution columns on `ssh_session_recordings`).

- **The trust model, because this is the feature where getting it wrong hands somebody a shell on a production box.** A share creates **no capability**. Joining requires live org membership _and_ `resources:execute` — the same permission the WS gateway already demands to open a terminal at all. The invite token says _which_ session, never _whether_; a link forwarded outside the org does nothing. That is also why there is deliberately **no `shared-consoles:*` permission family**: a new family would imply a share is a lesser thing than a shell, and it is not — a guest can be handed the keyboard. Revoking `resources:execute` from a role removes direct terminals and every join in one move.
- **Everything that decides is pure** (`server-core/src/shared-console/arbitration.ts`, 46 unit tests). `evaluateJoin` / `evaluateInput` / `evaluateHandover` / `evaluateHandoverRequest` / `evaluateOwnerAction` / `evaluateAttached` / `resolvePtySize` take state and return a decision; no db, no clock, no socket. The routes and the hub read state, call them, and do what they say. `evaluateJoin` checks the **permission before the token** on purpose — a refusal that graded the link would make the link feel like the thing being tested.
- **An observer's keystrokes are dropped server-side**, in `hub.handleAttachedMessage`, not hidden by the client. A hand-written WebSocket gets the same answer. The owner goes through the same gate (`ownerMayWrite`): having opened the session is not a role that exempts you from the one-driver rule you agreed to.
- **One driver is a partial unique index**, not application code: `shared_console_participants (shared_console_id) WHERE role='driver' AND status='joined'`. A handover is demote-then-promote in one transaction, so two concurrent grants across two replicas cannot both commit — the loser raises 23505, which `setDriver` turns into `DriverRaceLostError` and the route into a 409. `evaluateHandover` deliberately says **yes to both**: it decides authority, the index decides order. A lock would only work within one process and there are two.
- **Handover is authorised by two different people for two different reasons.** The current driver may give the keyboard away (it is theirs); the sharer may take it back without asking (it is their box, and asking permission from someone who has stopped responding is not a control). An observer may only _request_, which sets a flag and grants nothing.
- **Authority is re-derived, never remembered.** `POST /join` checks, the socket's `console:attach` checks again from live state, and the hub's sweep re-derives every participant's permissions every 15th tick (~30 s). A narrowed role, a lapsed break-glass elevation or an org removal detaches them mid-session. The **owner is not exempt** — their socket is the pty's origin, so losing the permission ends the session for everyone, which is the correct outcome.
- **The sweep is also how revocation and ejection cross replicas.** Both are HTTP calls that can land on either pod; the one holding the pty learns either directly (`revokeLocal` / `detach`) or on the 2 s sweep. "Immediately" in the docs means "immediately on the holding replica, within two seconds otherwise", and it is written that way rather than rounded down. The sweep does not run when no share is live on the replica.
- **Reading the participant list is only half of acting on it — `setParticipants` is the other half, and it is the only place `live.participants` is assigned.** Every path that adopts a fresh list (`bindShare`, `attach`, `refresh`, the sweep) goes through it, and it detaches any attached socket whose row has gone or is no longer `joined`. This was a real bug, caught in review: swapping the array in and stopping there left an ejected guest's socket in `attached`, so the **output fan-out kept streaming somebody else's production terminal** until the far slower 30 s permission sweep happened to notice. Input was already refused within one sweep (`evaluateInput` re-reads the refreshed row), but that is the second layer, not the first — "removed" has to mean removed from the session, not removed from a list. Reconciling against the _list_ rather than against a specific event is deliberate: it catches an ejection, a departure processed elsewhere, and a row deleted out from under us, without each needing to be a message that could be missed. It runs before `broadcastState` so the ejected socket does not get one last frame on its way out. Regression tests in `web/src/services/__tests__/shared-console-hub.test.ts` fail against the pre-fix hub.
- **"Single-use-ish" is two different behaviours that look alike.** An invite is consumed by the first person it _admits_ (`admitParticipant` clears the digest in the same `UPDATE … WHERE invite_token_hash IS NOT NULL`, so there is no window for a second redemption). Somebody already on the console resumes their own row with no token at all — a reload is a session resuming, not a capability being spent. Token format is `<shareId>.<64 hex>` so one opaque string resolves the join URL without a second parameter; only the sha256 of the secret half is stored. Default TTL 15 min (1–120).
- **The registry is process memory**, exactly like the bastion dispatcher, and web runs two replicas. The joiner has to reach the pod holding the pty: the sharer's browser mints a `routingKey`, puts it in its own `/api/ws?sid=`, and a **second Ingress** (`infra/k8s/web-ws-ingress.yaml`) covering only `/api/ws` sets `nginx.ingress.kubernetes.io/upstream-hash-by: "$arg_sid"`. Separate object because that annotation on the main ingress would hash the whole app onto one pod; `$arg_sid` is empty for every other socket and nginx falls back to round-robin. When it misses anyway (rolling deploy) the server answers `console:error` `code: "console_not_here"` and the guest reconnects on a fresh ws token up to 4 times, which rehashes. **This is a real limitation and the durable fix is a cross-replica relay** — the same follow-up the bastion registry is waiting on. `POST /shared-consoles` refuses outright when the pty is not on the answering replica rather than writing a share row nobody can attach to.
- **The pty's geometry is the driver's, and everyone else letterboxes** (`resolvePtySize` + `letterboxScale` in client-core). `term.resize` to the announced grid, then a CSS transform — never a reflow, because reflowing shows an observer a screen the driver is not looking at, and resizing the pty to the smallest viewport would let a spectator shrink the terminal of the person fixing production. `console:pty-size` carries a **per-recipient** `youAreDriver` so the driver keeps its fit addon and nobody has to re-derive its own role from the participant list. An observer's (and a letterboxed owner's) window size is still recorded against their row, so a handover _to_ them resizes to something they can read.
- **A slow guest is dropped, never throttled into the session.** The owner's socket keeps ordinary `makeWsBackpressure` (pause the pty); an observer past 8 MiB of `bufferedAmount` is detached with a message. Pausing the pty for a spectator would be the wrong trade every time.
- **Recording attribution is both in-band and out.** `ssh_session_recordings` gained `shared_console_id` + `participants` jsonb (snapshotted, highest role held — somebody who drove for ten seconds drove the session, and demoting them back to "observer" because they gave it back would mislead the person reading the tape). The cast also carries asciicast **`"m"` marker** events for join/handover/leave/revoke, which meant widening `CastEventCode`; `castOutputThrough` already only writes `"o"`, so the player ignores them and third-party viewers still open the file.
- **API keys are denied every mutation** (`API_KEY_DENY_RULES`, `/shared-consoles`, `MUTATING`), by the break-glass argument verbatim: sharing a shell and accepting a place on one are acts a person performs, and a key redeeming an invite turns a link pasted into a channel into a durable unattended foothold. Listing stays open — that is the visibility half.
- **The routes that take access away carry no permission**, deliberately (`leave`, `DELETE /:id`, invite withdrawal, ejection): gating them on still holding `resources:execute` would lock an owner whose role was narrowed mid-incident out of closing the session they opened. They are gated in the handler on being the sharer or holding `org:settings:write` — the `leases` autoDelete precedent, and the reason those five entries are `null` in `REQUIRED_PERMISSION`.
- **Surfaces**: `web/src/components/SharedConsolePanel.tsx` (share affordance, participant list, role badges, invite link, handover/revoke) beside the terminal in `ResourceDetailClient`; `routes/share.$token.tsx` + `SharedConsoleViewer.tsx` for the guest, outside the org shell like `/invite/` (`__root.tsx` has three matching path checks). The join screen resolves the org by previewing the token against each of the visitor's memberships — a join link carries no org id, and one that could name an org would be a link that points somewhere the visitor is not. Wire types live in `client-core/src/shared-console.ts` even though only web speaks them, so a second client cannot drift from the contract.
- **Deferred, on the record**: the four-eyes destructive-command gate. It cannot be built honestly from a pty byte stream — you see keystrokes, not commands, and line editing, shell aliases, functions, heredocs, paste and any full-screen app all mean the bytes are not the command. A text match would be a guess presented as a control, and a control people believe in is worse than none. What ships instead are two things that _are_ enforceable: `allowHandover: false` (a read-only share; nobody but the sharer can type, checked server-side) and break-glass, where a second person approves before the session exists. Also deferred: desktop (its terminal is a direct ssh2 dial for local accounts and never passes the proxy; cloud-mode terminals are the natural follow-up) and mobile (a phone is not where you take the keyboard on production). Docs: `features/shared-consoles.md`.

### Right-sizing ("Oversized", cloud-only)

The savings finder's second pass: not "wasted entirely" (orphans) but "bigger than what it does". API_VERSION 0.32.0 → **0.33.0**; no Postgres/ClickHouse schema change.

- **Contract** (`plugin-base/src/resource.ts`): `rightsizing?: RightsizingDeclaration` on `ResourceTypeDefinition`, Zod-validated — `sizeFieldKey` (+ `createSizeFieldKey` when the create form's picker key differs: Azure stores `vmSize`, picker is `size`), `regionFieldKey`, `diskFieldKey`, `cpuMetric: { seriesLabel, scale?: "percent"|"fraction" }` (series identity is the **label string** the warehouse stores; GCE's cpu/utilization is a 0–1 fraction), `memoryMetric?: { seriesLabel, interpretation: "percent"|"used-bytes"|"available-bytes" }` (providers disagree; for available-bytes the p95 of _used_ is capacity − **q05**(available)), `priceCurrency` (Hetzner: EUR), `sizeFamilyPattern` (regex whose capture groups must match between current and candidate — the architecture guard; must compile and capture or the manifest fails) and `resizeNote` (plugin-authored confirm-dialog caveat). Candidate sizes are **the create form's own size-picker options** (`SizeOption.vcpus/memoryMb/diskGb/priceMonthly/availableFor`), prices hydrated per region through `getCreateSizePricing`. `plugin-loader.test.ts` validates the registry: declared field keys must exist, and a declared type must have `supportsUpdate` + `supportsMetrics` + an editable size field — the apply path is `updateResource`.
- **Wired for five types**: hetzner `server` (change_type upgrade_disk:false; new per-location `getCreateSizePricing`; lister now syncs `primaryDiskGb` for the disk guard; CPU-only — no memory metric exists), digitalocean `droplet` (resize action disk:false — reversible; `/sizes` now `per_page=200` with `availableFor` regions; memory only with the DO agent), aws `ec2-instance` (ModifyInstanceAttribute; **new `aws/src/pricing.ts`** — live Price List Query API `GetProducts`, SigV4 JSON-RPC against api.pricing.us-east-1 through the existing `fetchSigned`, 6h per-(region,type) cache; the static `cost-estimate.ts` table stays known-broken and unused for this), azure `azure-vm` (ARM PATCH `hardwareProfile.vmSize`; only editable field — VM names are immutable; labels stored are Azure's own `localizedValue`: "Percentage CPU"/"Available Memory Bytes"), gcp `gce-instance` (new `setGceInstanceMachineType`, TERMINATED-only). Excluded honestly: scaleway (capacity+price exist but CPU metrics need an optional Cockpit token and the resize path is unverified), ovh (flavors carry plan codes, no price amounts), fly (no size picker at all — machines have no size field). Resize constraints verified against provider docs 2026-08: Hetzner 422 `server_not_stopped` + disk ≥ current even with upgrade_disk:false; DO auto-powers-off, target disk ≥ current; Azure resizes running but restarts; GCP/AWS hard-require stopped.
- **Pure math** (`client-core/src/rightsizing.ts`, type-only plugin-base import, unit-tested): `resolveUtilisation` + `computeSizeRecommendation` — thresholds in code (p95 CPU < 20, p95 mem < 40 when measured, headroom 0.7, min coverage 3d of 1m samples, memory floor 0.5 when unmeasured), candidate = cheapest that clears headroom without growing either axis; disk/region/family guards drop provider-rejectable combinations. Wire types (`OversizedResource` etc.) + `fetchRightsizing` live here too.
- **Server**: `getMetricQuantilesBatch` (server-core clickhouse readers) — q05/q95/max/count over per-minute `avgMerge` from `metric_points_1m` (30d TTL covers the 14d window; the 1h rollup would flatten peaks). `web/src/services/rightsizing.ts` composes it: cheap CPU pre-filter before any provider call, then one `getCreateConfig` per (account,type) + one price overlay per (account,type,region), 10-min in-memory response cache with in-flight dedupe; stopped machines (per `lifecycle.stoppedValues`) are skipped. `GET /api/org/:orgId/rightsizing` (`resources:read`, `?refresh=true`), `list_oversized_resources` MCP tool. **`POST /resources/update` now checks the change freeze (423) and audit-logs `resource.update` (field keys only — password fields must not land in audit metadata)** — it didn't before; the apply flow rides it on purpose.
- **Surfaces**: shared `OversizedSection` in `ui/src/savings/` (host-injected `RightsizingClient`; `describeResizeConfirm` builds the confirm text; freeze/tag-policy errors render verbatim per row) on `CostsPanel` between Potential savings and Sleep schedules; web `lib/rightsizing-client.ts` (apply = `POST /resources/update`); desktop cloud-only (`cloud_rightsizing_list` IPC, apply rides the existing `cloud_update_resource`); mobile `features/savings/OversizedSection.tsx` **read-only** (no resource editors on mobile — the read-only-billing line); `infrawrench oversized` CLI (cloud-only, `--json`). Docs: `features/right-sizing.md`.

### Moment view ("what changed around 03:14?", cloud-only)

A **read-only union screen** over data already indexed — no new tables, no poller passes, no writes. API_VERSION 0.33.0 → **0.34.0**.

- **Contract + pure logic** in `client-core/src/moment.ts` (unit-tested): wire types (`MomentEvent` with `kind`/severity/refs/typed `link`, `MomentFeedStatus`, `MomentIncidentSpan`, `MomentResponse`), `momentWindowBounds`/`clampMomentWindow` (half-window, default ±60m, max ±3d), `compareMomentEvents` (chronological with stable tie-breaks), `incidentsCovering` + `describeIncidentBadge` ("during DigitalOcean incident"), and `buildMomentTimeline` (badges events inside incident spans and collapses ≥3 adjacent same-resource events within 10-minute gaps into a burst). Re-exported through `@infrawrench/ui`.
- **Server**: `web/src/services/moment.ts` `computeMoment(orgId, { at, windowMinutes, permissions })` unions nine feeds, each reusing the feed's own table/query and gated on the exact permission its own endpoint requires (`MOMENT_FEED_PERMISSIONS`): changes/incidents (`resources:read`), anomalies (`costs:read`, by `detectedAt`), workflow runs + deployments (start and finish edges each become an event when in-window), audit (`audit:read`), freezes (`freezes:read`, start/end edges). Incidents ride `getOrgStatusIncidents` with `resolvedWithinMs` widened back to the window start, then overlap-filtered — the spans also return as `incidents` for badging. **Drift/expiry "alerts fired" have no per-event history** — only the per-org cooldown claims (`org_drift_alert_settings.last_notified_at`, `org_expiry_settings.last_notified_at`), so each contributes at most one event per window; both gated `resources:read` (settings _editing_ is what `org:settings:write` guards). **Per-feed partial failure**: every loader is try/caught into `feeds[].status: ok|omitted|error` (+`truncated` at 300 rows/feed) — one feed erroring never blanks the response, and no-permission feeds are omitted, not errored. `GET /api/org/:orgId/moment?at&window` (`api/routes/moment.ts`) 403s only when the caller can read _none_ of the feeds; openapi permission map lists `resources:read` as the floor.
- **Surfaces**: shared `MomentPanel` in `ui/src/moment/` (host-injected `MomentClient`, Changes-template): datetime-local input + "Around now" + ±15m/±1h/±6h presets, feed-health chips ("Workflow runs unavailable"), incident-overlap box, timeline with burst expansion; `onQueryChange` lets hosts mirror `at`+`window` into the URL (deep-linkable/shareable — both web `org.$orgId.moment.tsx` and desktop `routes/moment.tsx` use `validateSearch` + `replace: true` navigation). Entered via an optional `onInvestigateMoment` button on `ChangesPanel`. Desktop rides a new `cloud_moment` IPC (`electron/cloud-data/moment.ts`, preload allowlist); plain route, not a workspace tab even after the Changes/Expiring/Fan-out/Alerts conversion — it's a deep-linked investigation view addressed by `at`+`window`, not a persistent surface (its document title comes from `plainRouteDocumentTitle`); audit/freeze links deliberately no-op there (no native screens). Mobile is `features/moment/` (native rewrite, `useMoment` react-query hook, route `org/[orgId]/moment` + hidden tab) — and `pushDataToPath` now lands `cost_anomaly`, `provider_incident` and `resource_drift` pushes on the moment view (drift computes centre = midpoint of `since→now` and window to cover it; the Changes screen still honours old `since` links). CLI: `infrawrench moment [timestamp]` (`cli/commands/moment.ts`), `-w/--window 30m|6h` via `parseDuration`, `--json` or a glyph-per-severity text timeline. MCP: `what_changed` (`web/src/tools/moment.ts`) — declared permission `resources:read` floor, then per-feed gating via `effectiveToolPermissions`. Docs: `features/moment.md`.

### Metric threshold alerts (cloud-only)

The one alert type that pages off _metrics_: rules like "CPU % > 90 for 15 minutes on these resources → alert", evaluated in the poller against the ClickHouse metric store. New Postgres tables `metric_alert_rules` + `metric_alert_events`; new `metricAlerts` boolean on `slack_channels` / `msteams_webhooks` / `push_preferences`; new permissions `metric-alerts:read` (members) / `metric-alerts:write`.

- **Selector is a query, never an id list**: plugin + resource type + tag (key optionally pinned to a value), resolved against the live `resources` table on every pass (`server-core/src/metric-alerts/selector.ts`), so rules cover resources created after they were written. Tags come from `extractRecordTags({...outputsJson, ...fieldsJson})` — the same `tags`/`labels` convention tag-compliance reads; keys match case-insensitively, values exactly. Capped at 500 resources/rule.
- **Evaluation** (`server-core/src/metric-alerts/`): `window.ts` is the pure half (`judgeWindow` → breaching/cleared/insufficient/no_data; "held for the whole window" = every per-minute sample breaches AND ≥3 samples spread across the window — earliest in the first two thirds, freshest in the last third). `eval.ts` reads per-minute `avgMerge` samples from `metric_points_1m` via `getMetricMinuteSeriesBatch` (clickhouse readers) in one batch per rule, opens/resolves events, and fans out under the `metricAlerts` trigger (push payload `type: "metric_alert"`; mobile deep-links it to the moment view). `pass.ts` is the poller pass: `metric_alert_rules.next_eval_at` doubles as the claim lease (accounts protocol, 5-min lease, ~60s cadence), run as the loop's ninth pass (`runMetricAlertPass({ limit: 8 })`).
- **Dedupe/cooldown conventions**: the partial unique index on `metric_alert_events (rule_id, resource_id) WHERE status='firing'` is the open claim (`paging_incidents_open_unique` shape — the replica whose `onConflictDoNothing` insert lands notifies); resolve is a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE status='firing' RETURNING`; the rule's `cooldownMinutes` suppresses re-notifying a pair whose previous firing was _notified_ within the window (anomaly stance: stored-but-unnotified rows don't suppress). Recovery notifications only follow a notified firing; `no_data` (metric stopped reporting) and leaving the selector both resolve.
- **API**: `/api/org/:orgId/metric-alerts` CRUD + `/events` + `/metric-keys` (distinct `series_label`s from `metric_points_raw` last 7d, optionally per plugin/type — feeds the picker so nobody types internal metric names) + `/selector-options` (distinct plugin/type pairs + tag vocabulary from a bounded 2000-row scan) + `/selector-preview` (live "matches N resources"). Zod schemas in `@infrawrench/ui/metric-alerts/config` (React-free entry, `cost/config` pattern, drift-guarded against client-core types); wire types + fetch helpers + `METRIC_ALERT_LIMITS` (window 5–1440 min, cooldown 0–10080) in `client-core/src/metric-alerts.ts`.
- **Surfaces**: shared `MetricAlertsPanel`/`MetricAlertRuleModal` in `ui/src/metric-alerts/`; workspace tab `metric-alerts` ("Alerts") on both hosts (sidebar "Alerts" tile; desktop cloud-only tile + `cloud_metric_alerts_*` IPC channels — see the tab-conversion note in the Changes section); mobile gets the push toggle + Slack/Teams trigger rows only (no rule editor — the read-only-billing line); CLI `infrawrench alerts` / `alerts events` (cloud-only, `--json`, pure formatters in `cli/format.ts` because client-core imports must stay type-only). Docs: `features/metric-alerts.md`.

### Quota & limit radar (cloud-only)

Provider quota exhaustion used to be discovered mid-incident — the autoscaler stops scaling, a deploy fails on `VcpuLimitExceeded`, a `terraform apply` halts on the fifth VPC. The limit was knowable the whole time and nothing was watching it. New tables `account_quota_usage` / `account_quota_snapshots` / `account_quota_polls` / `org_quota_settings` (migration `0103_quota_radar`); new alert trigger `quotaAlerts`; API_VERSION **1.12.0**.

- **Contract** (`plugin-base/src/quotas.ts`): `PluginClient.fetchQuotas?(accountId): Promise<QuotaUsage[]>` — credentialed, so it lives on the client beside `fetchMetricSeries` and `fetchCostData`, not on the manifest — plus a data-only `quotas?: QuotaCapabilityDeclaration` on the manifest (`label`, `increaseUrl`, `partial`, `requiresElevatedCredential`), Zod-validated like every other section. The declaration is what makes the host schedule a pass, and it exists separately from the method so "which of my providers can tell me this?" is answerable without loading credentials (the `costs`/`credits` stance). `QuotaUsage` is `{ id, service, name, region?, limit, used, unit?, adjustable?, docsUrl? }`.
- **Three rules, and they are the feature.** (1) **Both halves come from the provider.** A plugin must never fill a `limit` in from documentation or from its own memory of the defaults: an account with an approved increase would then read as exhausted while it has thousands of vCPUs spare, which is a false alarm about the one thing this is supposed to be trusted about. A quota whose limit the provider will not state is a quota `fetchQuotas` does not return. (2) **Absence renders as _nothing_, never zero** — the `networkFlows` rule. An org whose provider has no quota API and an org genuinely nowhere near its limits are not the same org, so a plugin with no declaration contributes no rows and the surface names it under `unsupportedPluginIds`. (3) **Throw rather than return a short list**, because the collector replaces the account's stored readings and a partial answer reads as quotas having disappeared. `QuotaAccessError` (the `CreditAccessError`/`NetworkFlowSetupError` shape) separates "your credential cannot see this" from "the provider is down"; the host persists it with its help link to `account_quota_polls.last_error` so a missing reading explains itself rather than reading as headroom.
- **`normalizeQuotaUsage` is shared because every provider produces at least one bad row and each wrong handling is a different lie.** A non-positive or non-finite `limit` is **dropped** (AWS returns `Value: 0` for quotas that do not apply, GCP returns `limit: -1` for unlimited; both divide into a utilisation that is infinite or negative, and both sort straight to the top of a worst-first list). A negative `used` is **clamped**, not dropped — losing the row would also lose the limit. `used > limit` is **kept as-is**: over-quota is a real state and clamping to 100% hides the one reading nobody should miss. Duplicate ids keep the first, deterministically, because a duplicate makes the snapshot's key ambiguous and the trend jump between two unrelated series.
- **Providers wired (4)**: **aws** (`quotas.ts`, `partial: true`) is the awkward one — Service Quotas reports the ceiling and **no usage**, so every reading is assembled from two calls. `ListServiceQuotas` is narrowed with `QuotaCode` so it stays one unpaginated request (`ec2` publishes several hundred quotas), and an **empty list falls back to `GetAWSDefaultServiceQuota`** — AWS omits a quota whose applied value is unavailable, which is every account that has never had an increase approved, so reading the empty list as "no such quota" would switch the radar off for exactly those accounts. Usage comes from CloudWatch `AWS/Usage`/`ResourceCount` (statistic `Maximum` — the ceiling is enforced against the peak) for the two vCPU families, and from `DescribeAddresses`/`DescribeVpcs` for Elastic IPs and VPCs, which publish no usage metric at all. Four quota codes (`L-1216C47A` standard on-demand vCPUs, `L-34B43A08` G/VT vCPUs, `L-0263D0A3` Elastic IPs — under **`ec2`**, not `vpc`, despite the name, and pinned by test because a wrong service code returns an empty list rather than an error — and `L-F678F1CE` VPCs under `vpc`). Usage is fetched **before** the limit and a quota at zero usage is skipped without asking for its ceiling: an account with 30 enabled regions uses two or three, and 120 rows at 0% buries the four that matter. **gcp** is the cheap one — `compute.projects.get` + one paginated `compute.regions.list` carry `quotas[]` arrays with `limit` **and** `usage` already joined, so the whole project is two requests and no per-region fan-out; deliberately **not** the newer Cloud Quotas API, whose `QuotaInfo` has limits and increase-eligibility but no current usage and so cannot answer the only question asked. **digitalocean** (`partial: true`) reads `/v2/account` and counts with `?per_page=1` `meta.total` reads; two field names are not what they look like and both would have produced silently missing rows — there is **no `volume_limit`** (block storage has no account limit in the API at all) and **no `reserved_ip_limit`** (DO renamed floating IPs to reserved IPs and kept the field name, so the id keys on `floating_ip_limit` while the label says "Reserved IPs"). **kubernetes** is the only native fit: a `ResourceQuota`'s `status.hard`/`status.used` **is** limit-and-used over the same unit, so one `GET /api/v1/resourcequotas` covers the cluster; values go through the existing `parseQuantity` (the `M` = 10^6 vs `Mi` = 2^20 trap under-counts memory by 4.9% in the same direction every time, so it never looks like a bug), it iterates `hard` not `used` (a key in `used` alone is consumption under no ceiling, which has no utilisation), a missing `used` entry is zero but an **unparseable** one is dropped, and an empty result is a **true statement about the cluster** rather than a misconfiguration — quotas are opt-in in k8s — so unlike AWS/GCP it never throws on emptiness.
- **Deliberately not wired**: **hetzner** enforces per-project limits and publishes them **nowhere in its API**, only in prose docs — reporting them from that prose is precisely the invented limit rule (1) forbids, so Hetzner accounts contribute nothing and are named unsupported. Same for cloudflare, vercel, fly and scaleway, none of which expose a documented account-limit endpoint. This is the feature working, not a gap.
- **Trend arithmetic lives in `client-core/src/quotas.ts`**, pure and unit-tested, because the poller alerts on the same projection the page draws and two implementations of "trending to exhaustion" is two chances to disagree in front of somebody deciding whether to open a support ticket. **Least-squares over 14 days**, not first-vs-last: the endpoints are exactly the two readings most likely to be atypical, and a fortnight flat at 40% with one spike to 90% this morning has a first-vs-last slope predicting exhaustion tomorrow. `QUOTA_TREND_MIN_POINTS` = **3** (two points always fit a line perfectly, and the line through two readings either side of one deploy projects that deploy repeating forever); `QUOTA_TREND_HORIZON_DAYS` = **30** (extrapolating a provisioning history to a quarter says nothing); a zero time span returns null rather than `Infinity` days. Null `perDay` renders as "no trend yet" and **never** as "no risk" — opposite claims, only one true. Severity is ordered `exhausted > critical > trending > ok` because a quota at 100% is also over threshold and also trending, and reporting the mildest true thing about it is the least useful possible summary.
- **The limit is stored on every snapshot, not just the current row.** A ceiling moves when a support ticket is approved, and a trend computed against today's limit would silently rewrite last week's utilisation the moment it did — an account that was at 95% and got an increase would retroactively read as having been fine all along, which is the opposite of the fact worth keeping.
- **Collection** (`server-core/src/quotas/collect.ts`, poller pass `pollAccountQuotas`): the `credits/collect.ts` template — current row upserted on `(account_id, quota_key)`, a snapshot appended **every** pass even when nothing moved (a flat stretch is evidence of a stable account; dropping it makes stable indistinguishable from uncollected), and a quota the provider stopped reporting **deleted** from the current table (a stale "94% of your Elastic IPs" is worse than nothing) while its snapshots stay. Claimed with `claimDueQuotaAccounts` — the credit claim's `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … WHERE next_poll_at <= now() RETURNING` verbatim, keyed off `account_quota_polls`, so N replicas produce one claim; unconditional, both callers would pay for the same metered CloudWatch reads. **6h cadence** with 45m jitter (faster than credits' 12h because a quota is filled by provisioning, which happens in minutes; not faster because AWS quota reads are the most expensive collection in the codebase and the trend is fitted over a fortnight), 30m→24h exponential backoff on failure.
- **Alerting** reuses the existing plumbing rather than a parallel one: a new `ALERT_TRIGGERS` entry (`quotaAlerts`, `warning` — the alert fires at a _threshold_, so by construction there are hours or days to act; the already-exhausted quota rides the same message and the title leads with it) and one `routeAlert` call. `quotas/alerts.ts` is `expiry/alerts.ts` verbatim including `releaseUnlessDelivered` and the `spent` flag: **`last_notified_at` means "last alert scan", not "last message"**, so a completed scan finding nothing keeps the window, and only a scan that reached nobody is rolled back. A hold counts as delivered (`succeeded + held`) — rewinding would rebuild the window and deliver it twice. The pass is deliberately **silent about collection failures**: an account whose read is failing produces no rows and therefore no alert, because a broken collector is an operational problem the page names per account, not a page at 3am about a limit we cannot see. Push payload `type: "quota_alert"` deep-links `/org/{orgId}/quotas`; never Twilio. `quotas/summary.ts` is the pure body (cap 8 named quotas, remainder collapsed) and prints the **absolute figures beside the percentage** — 89% of 1,024 vCPUs has 112 left to work with, 89% of 5 Elastic IPs has none, and the percentage alone does not say which.
- **Settings** `org_quota_settings` (one row per org, missing row = shipped defaults, the `org_expiry_settings` contract): `enabled`, `threshold` **0.8** stored as a fraction so it is never divided by 100 in two places that could disagree, bounded **0.5–0.99** and **rejected rather than clamped** (a form showing 0.5 after the user typed 0.4 reads as the setting not having saved). Below half every quota is "critical" and the radar stops meaning anything; at exactly 1.0 the provider is already refusing requests, so the alert reports an outage instead of warning about one.
- **Retention**: `pruneQuotaSnapshots` rides the poller's hourly retention slot beside `pruneCreditSnapshots`, same year window and same batched idempotent prune (no claim warranted — the `pruneResourceChanges` stance).
- **Surfaces**: `GET /api/org/:orgId/quotas` (`resources:read`) + `GET|PUT /quotas/settings` (`org:settings:write` — the threshold decides what the org's channels hear, the expiry-settings stance). The response carries `rows` **and** `accounts` **and** `unsupportedPluginIds` deliberately: `rows: []` alone cannot distinguish "nothing is near a limit" from "every collection is failing", and only one of those is good news. Shared `QuotasSection` in `ui/src/quotas/` (utilisation bar with a **threshold marker on every bar**, so colour is never the only evidence of where the line is; an over-quota bar fills and reports >100% in the figure rather than overflowing its track; the `ok` fill is a muted neutral rather than green, because "we found no problem" is not "everything is fine"), workspace tab kind **`quotas`** ("Quotas") on both hosts via the full chain incl. `validate-tabs` + the openapi `TabTarget` enum. Desktop is cloud-only with **no local half at all** — unlike Expiring, which computes over rows the machine already holds, a quota reading is a live credentialed call on a schedule the desktop does not run with history it does not store — one `cloud_quotas` IPC. Weekly digest gains a **Quotas** line counted from the same `alertableQuotas` predicate the alert pass fires on, so the digest and the page cannot disagree about what "at risk" means. Docs: `features/quota-radar.md`.
- **Not done**: mobile screen, CLI subcommand, MCP tool, org-config-as-code coverage, and a settings-page editor for the threshold (the API accepts it; nothing renders a form yet).

### Synthetic probes (cloud-only)

HTTP uptime/latency checks probed on an interval from **outside** the cluster — the egress-proxy Worker gained a `POST /probe` endpoint beside `/fetch` (same token, same `assertAllowedUrl` + per-hop redirect re-validation; body dropped unread; `ok` = completed AND status < 500, so a 4xx auth wall is "up" and a 5xx is "down"; endpoint failures return HTTP 200 with `{ result: { ok, status?, latencyMs, error? } }` — a probe failure is data, not a proxy error). New Postgres table `synthetic_probes`; new `probeAlerts` boolean on `slack_channels` / `msteams_webhooks` / `push_preferences`.

- **Schema → pass**: `synthetic_probes.next_probe_at` is the due column AND the claim lease with a twist — the claim (`server-core/src/probes/pass.ts`, `claimDueProbes`, accounts SKIP LOCKED protocol, run as the poller's eleventh pass `runProbePass({ limit: 8 })`) writes `now() + interval_seconds` so the lease IS the next cadence and completion never reschedules. Config rides the workflow proxy env pair (`WORKFLOW_FETCH_PROXY_URL`/`_TOKEN`) but probes **skip silently** when unconfigured (best-effort like ClickHouse) where workflow fetch throws; a proxy-level failure (unreachable/bad token) records _nothing_ rather than marking a healthy endpoint down. State machine on the row: `consecutive_failures` climbs to `failure_threshold` → status "down" + notify (`probeAlerts` trigger, push payload `type: "probe_alert"` deep-linking `/org/{orgId}/probes`); any success resets to "up", with a recovery notification only when previously down. CRUD in `probes/store.ts` (`ProbeInputError`, per-org cap under an advisory lock; URL/method change resets state to "unknown"); limits/validation/wire types shared via `client-core/src/probes.ts` (`PROBE_LIMITS`, `normalizeProbeUrl` — editor and API clamp identically).
- **Results are ordinary metric points**: each check writes series "Latency" (ms) and "Up" (1/0) via `insertMetricPoints` under `plugin_id "synthetic-probe"` / `resource_type_id "probe"` / `resource_id "probe:<id>"`, so `getMetricRange` and the existing `MetricChart`/`SparklineChart` render probe history with zero new chart code. The list's trailing-24h uptime is `avg("Up")` from the 1m rollup (`getMetricSeriesAverageBatch` in clickhouse readers, best-effort).
- **API**: `/api/org/:orgId/probes` CRUD + `GET /suggestions` (endpoint candidates mined from `resources.outputs_json`/`fields_json` keys url/endpoint/host/hostname/domain/publicIp/ipv4 — the expiry/feed stance, no plugin clients; bare hosts normalized to `https://`, single-label hosts dropped, deduped by URL) + `GET /:id/metrics?startMs&endMs`. Permissions ride the schedules stance (`resources:read` / `resources:write`); spec `openapi/paths/probes.ts` + `REQUIRED_PERMISSION` entries.
- **Surfaces**: shared `ProbesPanel`/`ProbeEditorModal` in `ui/src/probes/` (the editor's endpoint field is a suggestions **picker** first, custom URL second; rows expand into the 24h latency chart); workspace tab `"probes"` ("Probes") on both hosts — full chain incl. validate-tabs + TabTarget enum; desktop cloud-only tile + `cloud_probes_*` IPC ×6 (`electron/cloud-data/probes.ts`, preload allowlist). Mobile is a **read-only** list (`features/probes/`, route `org/[orgId]/probes`, entered from the Accounts tab card and from a `probe_alert` push) — no editor on the phone, the read-only-billing line: the suggestions picker is a web/desktop authoring surface. CLI `infrawrench probes [id|name]` (cloud-only, `--json`, hand-rolled `sparkline()` for the 24h latency detail). Docs: `features/synthetic-probes.md`.

### Public status pages (cloud-only)

The synthetic probes, pointed outward: a chosen set of probes published at an unauthenticated `/status/<slug>`. New tables `status_pages` + `status_page_components`; migration `0078_aberrant_shard.sql`; API_VERSION 0.43.0 → **0.44.0**.

- **The security model is the file split**, not a filter. `getStatusPageWire` answers the org's editor; `getPublicStatusPage` (`server-core/src/status-pages/store.ts`) writes the anonymous payload _from scratch_ rather than narrowing the private one — with narrowing, a field added to the org shape later becomes public by omission. What is deliberately absent: probe URLs/methods/intervals/thresholds, last error text and status codes, resource/account/plugin ids, the org id, and the probe ids themselves (components carry their own row id). An unpublished page and an unknown slug both 404, so the endpoint can't confirm a slug is real.
- **Two structural safety properties.** `published` defaults **false** (creating a page can never expose anything; publishing is a separate confirmed step). The slug is `generateStatusPageSlug()` — 22 chars of rejection-sampled `randomBytes` over a no-confusables alphabet, ~109 bits — never derived from the title, and globally unique rather than per-org because the public URL must contain no org id. `POST /:id/rotate-slug` is the reroll for a leaked link.
- **Routing**: `publicStatusRoutes` is mounted on `api` at `/api/status` **before** and outside the org tree and every auth middleware (the cost-ingest/pages stance, but for the opposite reason — those authenticate themselves, this one must not authenticate at all). Web's `__root.tsx` gained `isPublicRoute()`: `/status/` renders `<Outlet/>` without ever calling `/api/auth/me`, which would redirect an anonymous visitor to sign-in. The route fetches with a bare `fetch` + `credentials: "omit"`, not `apiGet`. Response carries `Cache-Control: public, max-age=60` (a status page is exactly what gets hammered during an incident) and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`.
- **No new measurement.** Current state comes from the `synthetic_probes` rows; 24h uptime from the existing `getMetricSeriesAverageBatch`; 90-day history from a new `getMetricDailyAverageBatch` over the **1h** rollup (`metric_points_1h` keeps a year, `_1m` does not). ClickHouse is best-effort — an outage costs the numbers, never the page. Days with no rows are emitted as `uptime: null` on a **dense** day axis, so a gap renders grey rather than shifting the bars and claiming a shorter, greener history.
- **A paused probe reads `unknown`, not its last green** (`componentStateFromProbe`) — the page is a claim about what is being checked _now_. The rollup (`rollUpStatusPageState`) ignores unknown components rather than blanking a page that is otherwise reporting.
- **Surfaces**: shared `ui/src/status-pages/` (`StatusPagesPanel` under the probe list on both hosts' Probes tab — the "section of Costs, not a tab" stance; `StatusPageEditorModal`; `PublicStatusPageView`, which lives in `ui` so the editor's preview and the real page are the same component). Desktop cloud-only (`cloud_status_pages_*` IPC ×5) and takes `appOrigin` from `CLOUD_URL`, since it isn't served from the cloud app. Mobile is a **read-only** list of pages with live/draft state that opens the URL in the browser. CLI `infrawrench status-pages [name|id]`. MCP `list_status_pages`. Docs: `features/status-pages.md`.
- `probeMetricResourceId` moved to a db-free leaf `probes/metric-ids.ts` (the `cost/workflow-cost-ids.ts` split) — importing it from `probes/pass.ts` dragged the poller's whole Slack/Teams/push stack into the MCP tool module and broke `tools/__tests__/generic.test.ts`'s schema mock.

### Incident mode — **declared** incidents (cloud-only)

One object that performs the six errands somebody currently runs by hand at 03:14. New tables `incidents` + `incident_notes` + `incident_artifacts` + `status_page_notices` (`server-core/src/db/incident-schema.ts`, migration `0105_incident_mode.sql`); new permissions `incidents:read`/`incidents:write`; new `incidentAlerts` alert trigger; API_VERSION → **1.15.0**.

- **The name collides and the code says so out loud.** `provider_status_incidents` / `OrgStatusIncident` / `status-incidents.ts` / `infrawrench incidents` are a **provider's** outage scraped from their status page ([provider status correlation](#provider-status-correlation-is-it-me-or-is-it-them-cloud-only)). This feature is the org's **own** declaration. Every type here is `Incident*`, every provider one stays `*StatusIncident*`, and the two meet in exactly one place: a provider incident overlapping the window is evidence on the declared incident's timeline (`MomentEventLink.kind "incident"` is renamed to `"provider-incident"` when it crosses into `IncidentTimelineLink`, which is the one line that makes the collision impossible to get wrong silently). The CLI could not take the short name — `infrawrench incidents` was already the provider command — so it is **`infrawrench declared-incidents`**; taking it would have broken a shipped command and left two features answering to one word.
- **Everything is composed; almost nothing is reimplemented.** `web/src/services/incidents.ts` calls `createChangeFreeze`/`endChangeFreeze` (`services/change-freezes.ts`), `routeAlert` (`server-core/alerts/route.ts`) under the new `incidentAlerts` trigger so the org's existing rules/quiet hours/escalation/ack button all apply unchanged, `createStatusPageNotice`/`resolveStatusPageNotice`, and — for the timeline — **`computeMoment` itself**. That last one is the reuse that earns the most: the moment union already loads nine feeds with per-feed permission gating and per-feed partial failure, so the incident window is expressed as a centre + half-width (`clampMomentWindow` caps at ±3 days) and handed to it. Two feeds the union has no events for are queried locally: probe transitions and `metric_alert_events`.
- **Persist first, then attempt, then record the outcome — that ordering _is_ the partial-failure design.** `server-core/src/incidents/store.ts` is deliberately side-effect free so the durable record can always be written; the composition layer then fires each opted-in artefact in a `Promise.all` of individually try/caught calls, each writing an `incident_artifacts` row. `failed` is a **stored state, not a thrown error**, carrying the error text. Doing the errands inside a transaction with the insert would mean a Slack outage at 03:14 deletes the incident somebody just declared. The unique index on (incident, kind) makes retry an upsert rather than a pile of attempts, and makes "did this incident open a freeze?" a single-row question at resolve time.
- **Four artefact states, not three.** `failed` is "never made it"; **`close_failed`** is "made it, could not put it away" — resolving could not lift the freeze or close the public notice, so both are still live. They are distinct because the repair is opposite: `failed` is re-created, `close_failed` is **re-closed**, and re-creating one of those would open a second freeze or post a duplicate notice. The bug this prevents is subtle and was caught in review: recording only an error string and leaving the status at `created` made the row invisible to the retry path (which selects on status), stranding the incident resolved with a change freeze nothing in the product could lift. `planIncidentArtifactRetry` (pure, client-core) is the one place that decision lives, so it is unit-testable without a database.
- **`incident_artifacts.request_json` exists so a retry asks for the same thing the declaration did.** Recorded on the failure path too — the status-page artefact keeps the operator's chosen `componentIds`, because a retry that fell back to "no components" would publish the outage against the **whole page**, silently widening a customer-visible announcement as a side effect of pressing Retry.
- **Resolve undoes what _this_ incident created**, by artefact `ref_id` — the freeze it opened, never whichever freeze happens to be in effect (which might be somebody's planned change window). A freeze deleted by hand resolves to `closed`, not an error: the incident's freeze is gone, which is what resolving wanted. `ref_id` is plain text with **no FK**, the sidecar stance: the row is a record of _what we did_ and must stay legible after the thing is deleted.
- **`mitigated` is a real state.** Collapsing it into `resolved` makes every postmortem's time-to-mitigate a guess; collapsing it into `open` keeps people paged after the bleeding stopped. `updateIncidentRecord` is the only place status and its timestamps are set, so they cannot disagree — resolving without a prior mitigation back-fills `mitigatedAt` from `resolvedAt`, and reopening clears both.
- **`buildIncidentTimeline` + `renderPostmortemMarkdown` are pure**, in `client-core/src/incidents.ts`, unit-tested (`__tests__/incidents.test.ts`: ordering across six sources, the empty window, a failed artefact, deterministic tie-breaks, truncation). The window is `[startedAt, resolvedAt ?? now]` **inclusive at both ends**, and entries outside it are dropped even if a loader passed them, so a sloppier range cannot widen the incident's story. Nothing is copied into the incident's tables — the timeline is a join, so an upstream correction shows up on the next read. The postmortem pre-fills the facts and leaves impact/root-cause/action-items **blank on purpose**; being pure is what makes the browser's preview and the API's bytes identical.
- **`incidents:write` is a member permission**, unusually for a write scope: the people who notice an outage at 03:14 are rarely admins, and a product where declaring needs an admin is a product where nobody declares. The governance side effects keep their own gates — `openFreeze` checks `freezes:write` in the service (not the route) and records a failed artefact naming the permission instead of escalating or refusing the declaration.
- **`status_page_notices` is the one genuinely new primitive.** Status pages were purely derived — no way to say "we know, we're on it". The public assembler still writes from scratch (`getPublicStatusNotices` → `PublicStatusNotice`), and the incident id, the declarer and the org are deliberately **not** in it; the read is `.catch(() => [])` because during an incident that endpoint is the one thing that must stay up.
- **Operator text is control-character-stripped at both ends.** An incident title/summary/note is written by one member and read by another, and the CLI reads it into a terminal — which _executes_ ANSI/CSI/OSC bytes rather than showing them (clear the screen, walk the cursor back over printed output and rewrite it, set the window title). Browsers render them inertly, which is why the exposure survives review until somebody runs the CLI. `stripControlCharacters` (client-core) sanitises on write; `safe()` (`cli/output.ts`, a deliberate copy because the CLI's client-core imports are type-only) sanitises again at the render boundary for rows written earlier or out of band. **Order matters: sanitise the value, then colour it** — `c.*` wrapping happens after. CR is stripped along with ESC: it needs no escape sequence and is the simplest spoofing primitive there is. `safe()` is exported for the other CLI commands that print org-controlled text; adopting it across them is a follow-up rather than part of this change.
- **Surfaces**: shared `ui/src/incidents/` (`IncidentsPanel` list+detail in one component — during an incident the useful navigation is "back and into the other one", not a route transition per click; `DeclareIncidentModal`; `IncidentTimelineView`). Workspace tab `"incidents"` on both hosts — full chain incl. `validate-tabs` + the `TabTarget` enum; the tab is single-instance but compares `incidentId` so reactivation restores the incident. Web mirrors the selection into the path (`/org/:orgId/incidents/:id`, shareable); desktop into `?incident=` (search passed explicitly so going back to the list clears it). Desktop is cloud-only with `cloud_incidents_*` IPC ×10. **Entry points where an incident actually starts**: an optional `onDeclareIncident` on `ProbesPanel` (down probes only) and `MetricAlertsPanel` (per firing), with the modal opened by the host on that same screen — navigating away and retyping is how incidents end up declared in Slack instead. Mobile is `features/incidents/` and **breaks the read-only line on purpose** (declare + timeline + note are the three things you do away from a laptop); its declare sheet is title+severity with the safe defaults hard-coded, because freezing an org is not a lock-screen decision. CLI `infrawrench declared-incidents [id|title]`. Docs: `features/incident-mode.md`.

### Resource ownership (cloud-only)

Owner, purpose and ticket link as first-class fields on any resource — the thing that turns "34 orphaned resources" into a list with a name against each row. New table `resource_ownership`, same migration and API bump as status pages above.

- **Sidecar stance** (`resource_schedules` / `resource_leases`): `resource_id` is deliberately **not** a FK — the answer to "whose is this?" must survive sync churn — and cleanup rides the `account_id` cascade. UNIQUE (org, resource).
- **Owner is two columns on purpose.** `owner_user_id` (FK users, `set null`) is the routable one and the only thing an alert can reach; `owner_label` is free text for a team/rota/contractor and is display-only. The member wins for display and routing when both are set, and a deleted user falls back to the label — which is exactly why keeping both is worth the column. `toOwnerAnnotation` (server) / `toOwnerSummary` (client-core) implement that tie-break identically and are unit-tested.
- **An annotation only exists when somebody can be _named_.** A row carrying only a purpose annotates as `null`, because the finder's question is "who do I tell?". `upsertOwnership` **deletes** a row that ends up all-null, so "no record" and "empty record" are not two states.
- **Contract**: `ResourceOwnerAnnotation` is defined once, in **plugin-base** beside `OrphanCostAnnotation` (client-core's plugin-base import is type-only, so the dependency can only point that way); `client-core/ownership.ts` re-exports it and aliases `OwnerSummary`. It is deliberately **not** also re-exported from `client-core/orphans.ts` — two sibling modules re-exporting one name under one barrel makes `export *` ambiguous and the dts rollup drops it (the `ShowbackReport` incident).
- **The orphan finder is the payoff.** `OrphanedResource.owner` + `OrphanListResponse.unownedCount` (`countUnownedOrphans`); `collectOrphanGroups` stays pure and always emits `owner: null`, with the web service annotating from one batched `lookupResourceOwners`. Best-effort like cost. **Local mode reports `unownedCount === totalCount`** — honest, since it knows of no owners — and every surface drops the owner column there rather than labelling each row "Unowned", which would be a different claim. `Unowned` is _printed_, never blank: a blank cell reads as "not looked up".
- **Owner-routed alerts** (`server-core/src/ownership/notify.ts`): `notifyResourceOwner` + `sendPushToOrgUser` (a `userId` filter on `resolveTargets`, still joined through `organizationMembers` so an ex-member is unreachable). Wired into the lease pass (all three announcement sites) and probe down transitions. Three invariants: **additive, never a replacement** for the org fan-out (an outage must not go unseen because one owner is away); never routes to a label (`isLabel` skips); never bypasses the owner's own trigger preference. Never throws — callers are poller passes.
- **`GET /ownership/members` is `resources:read`, not `team:read`** — deliberately. Requiring permission to see roles and membership dates in order to say "this VM is mine" puts ownership out of reach of the people who create resources, and ownership nobody can record is ownership that stays empty. Same reasoning gates the whole feature on `resources:read`/`resources:write` rather than an admin permission.
- **Surfaces**: `ui/src/ownership/ResourceOwnershipPanel` behind a new host-driven `renderOwnershipTab` on `DetailView` (the `renderLeaseTab` pattern — ungated by type, any resource can have an owner). A plain form, not a modal: this is reference data people come to _read_, and reading it behind a button is why ownership metadata goes stale everywhere else. Web wires it always, desktop cloud-only (`cloud_ownership_*` IPC ×4). Owner column in `SavingsSection` (web/desktop), the mobile Costs tab, and `infrawrench orphans`. CLI `infrawrench ownership [query]`. MCP `list_resource_ownership`. Docs: `core-concepts/resource-ownership.md`.

### Sync protocol

- `POST /api/v1/sync/pull` — returns entities with `syncVersion > lastSyncVersion`
- `POST /api/v1/sync/push` — upserts entities with last-write-wins by `updatedAt`
- `GET /api/v1/sync/status` — returns max syncVersion

Auth via `Authorization: Bearer <api_key_or_oauth_token>`. Scopes: `sync:read`, `sync:write`, `resources:read/write`, `dashboards:read/write`.

### Server-core package (`@infrawrench/server-core`)

Shared backend code consumed by `@infrawrench/web` and `@infrawrench/poller`. Lives at `app/packages/server-core/` with subpath exports:

- `@infrawrench/server-core/db/client` — Drizzle/postgres client (reads `DATABASE_URL`)
- `@infrawrench/server-core/db/schema` — table definitions
- `@infrawrench/server-core/encryption` — AES-256-GCM helpers
- `@infrawrench/server-core/drivers` — sql/kv/docker/storage driver registries
- `@infrawrench/server-core/host-services` — `HostServices` builders for plugin clients
- `@infrawrench/server-core/tunnel-resolver` — credential rewriting through SSH tunnels
- `@infrawrench/server-core/sync-resources` — `syncAccountResources` + `syncAccountResourceType`
- `@infrawrench/server-core/plugin-loader` — `loadPlugins` / `getPlugin` (static-import registry)

Web's `web/src/db/client.ts`, `web/src/db/schema.ts`, `web/src/services/{encryption,drivers,host-services,tunnel-resolver,sync-resources}.ts`, and `web/src/plugins/loader.ts` are now thin re-exports — no consumer-side import paths changed.

### Plugin response shape: `{ resource, warnings }`

`PluginClient.createResource` may return either a bare `ResourceInstance` (legacy) or a `ResourceCreateResult = { resource, warnings: ResourceWarning[] }` envelope (new). Use `normalizeResourceCreateResult(result)` from `@infrawrench/plugin-base` to flatten before consuming. The host surfaces `warnings` as `toast.warning(...)` via the new shared toast feature in `@infrawrench/ui`. Currently emitted by the DigitalOcean plugin when `assignToProjectIfNeeded` fails (resource created, project assignment lost).

### Stripe billing

$20/month per seat. Free tier: 1 user, 3 accounts, no audit/API keys/team.

- `POST /api/v1/webhooks/stripe` — handles checkout.session.completed (+ `async_payment_succeeded`), invoice.paid/failed, subscription.updated/deleted, charge.refunded
- Server Actions: createCheckoutSession, createBillingPortalSession, getSubscriptionStatus

### Prepaid capacity slots

A **capacity slot** is one seat bought outright — **$200 for 24 months** (`CAPACITY_SLOT_PRICE_USD` / `CAPACITY_SLOT_TERM_MONTHS` in `server-core/src/billing/capacity-slots.ts`) — instead of rented at $20/month. `capacity_slots` (migration `0070`), `POST /api/org/:orgId/billing/capacity/checkout` in Checkout **`payment` mode**, priced by the optional `STRIPE_CAPACITY_SLOT_PRICE_ID` (**must be a one-time price**; unset ⇒ `purchasable: false` in `GET /billing/status`, the billing card hides, the route 503s).

Four things about this are load-bearing:

- **Capacity is the sum of two sources.** `subscriptions.seatCount + activeCapacitySeats()`. Every seat path reads both: `checkSeatAvailability` gates invites on the sum, and `releaseSeat`'s floor is `remaining − prepaidSeats` — an org whose slots already cover its members owes nothing monthly, and flooring at the raw member count would bill twice for the same people.
- **A slot is paid access on its own.** `planAccess` returns `reason: "capacity_slot"` with **no subscription row at all**, checked _before_ the none/inactive verdicts so a lapsed subscription can't revoke a term that is already paid for. Miss this and an org pays $200 and is then told to upgrade.
- **A slot-only org has no monthly seat to add.** `SeatLimitStatus.canAddSeat` is false there, and the invite 409 carries it — retrying with `addSeat: true` would reach `addSeat` and find no subscription item to increment (a 502 dressed as a Stripe outage). The prompt has to send the user to Billing instead.
- **Expiry is computed, never swept.** Capacity is `sum(quantity)` over rows that are `active` and inside `expiresAt`, so a lapsing term takes effect with no cron. Nothing mutates a row except a refund (`charge.refunded` → `status: "refunded"`, keyed on the PaymentIntent, which is why the purchase route mirrors its metadata onto `payment_intent_data`).

Grants happen **only in the webhook**, gated on `payment_status === "paid"`, with quantity read back from the settled line items (Checkout lets the buyer adjust it, so our own metadata would grant what we offered rather than what they paid for). Idempotency is the unique index on `stripe_checkout_session_id` + `onConflictDoNothing` — Stripe redelivers, and a delayed payment method fires `completed` (unpaid) then `async_payment_succeeded`, so both events route through one handler and grant once between them.

Surfaces: shared `BillingSection` card (quantity input + purchase history with per-term expiry), desktop via the `billing/capacity/checkout` allowlist entry, mobile **read-only** slot rows (the read-only-billing line). No CLI subcommand — billing has no CLI surface at all.

### Complimentary orgs (platform admin)

`organizations.complimentary` (migration `0024`) marks an org as never-billed with every paid perk: `getMonthlySpend` in `web/src/chat/billing.ts` counts it as paid (uncapped chat unless the org set its own `chat_monthly_cap_micros`), `reportUsageToStripe` skips the meter event (usage rows still recorded for internal cost tracking), `POST /billing/checkout` returns 400, and `GET /billing/status` returns `{ complimentary, subscription }`. Toggled by platform admins — emails in the `INFRAWRENCH_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` env allowlist (`web/src/auth/platform-admin.ts`) — via `/api/admin/organizations[/{orgId}/complimentary]` and the standalone `/admin` web page. Platform admin is a deployment-operator concept, entirely separate from org roles.

---

## Desktop cloud sync

### OAuth PKCE

`desktop/electron/cloud-auth.ts` — WorkOS OAuth2 PKCE flow. Custom protocol `infrawrench://callback`. Tokens encrypted in `cloud_sync_state` SQLite table.

### Sync engine

`desktop/electron/cloud-sync.ts` — 60-second interval, **push-only by design**. Push sends rows modified since `lastPushAt`; credentials are decrypted locally, sent plaintext over TLS, and re-encrypted on the server.

There is no downward apply, and adding one is not a matter of writing upserts:

- **Cloud mode is a thin client.** `electron/cloud-data/*` proxies every read to the web API and `src/lib/ssh-dispatch.ts` routes cloud-key SSH through the WS proxy, so private keys never leave the server. There is no local mirror to refresh.
- **The local schema forbids a credential-free mirror.** `accounts.encrypted_credentials` is `NOT NULL` and `resources.account_id` is `NOT NULL REFERENCES accounts(id)`, with associations and pins hanging off resources — no account row means no resource row means nothing downstream.
- **A credential-bearing mirror has a second cost**: it would make the desktop an independent caller of provider APIs, outside the poller's per-plugin token buckets (`packages/poller/src/poll-account.ts`), which nothing coordinates.

The server's `/api/v1/sync/pull` still exists and is correct; it simply has no first-party consumer. `status: "synced", pushOnly: true` is the permanent shape of the sync-status event, not a temporary caveat.

### Desktop SQLite v3 migration

Added `cloud_sync_state` table + `cloud_id`, `sync_version`, `deleted_at` columns to synced tables.

---

## API key system

Format: `iwk_` + 32 random bytes (base64url). Stored as an HMAC digest keyed off `ENCRYPTION_MASTER_KEY` (`keyedHash`), with a plain-SHA-256 fallback for pre-migration rows that rehashes on hit and dies at `legacyHashSunsetAt`. Prefix (first 12 chars) shown for identification. Revokable, rotatable.

Authorization is scopes ∩ the owner's current role permissions, and authentication additionally requires the owner to still be a member of the key's org — see [Permissions](#permissions).

---

## OpenAPI spec

`app/packages/web/src/api/openapi/` builds an OpenAPI 3.1 document from hand-written Zod schemas plus enums sourced live from `loadPlugins()`. So `pluginId` and `typeId` path params are typed as enums of the actually-installed plugin / resource-type IDs — adding a plugin extends the spec automatically.

- `index.ts` — `buildOpenApiDocument()` orchestrates registration; `getOpenApiDocument()` caches the result for runtime serving. Auto-injects `operationId` for every op (method + path → camelCase) so generated SDKs have stable function names.
- `dynamic.ts` — calls `loadPlugins()` and emits `PluginId` / `ResourceTypeId` / `CredentialFormatId` Zod enums.
- `common.ts` — shared schemas (`Uuid`, `Email`, `IsoDateTime`, `Ok`, `ErrorResponse`, `ResourceId`, `JsonObject`). `strict()` helper wraps `z.object().strict()` so `additionalProperties: false` is the default.
- `paths/*.ts` — one file per route module (`auth.ts`, `accounts.ts`, `resources.ts`, `connection-features.ts`, etc.). Each exports a `register*Paths(ctx)` function that registers operations against `ctx.registry`.
- `public-spec.ts` — the difference between what the spec knows and what we publish. `injectInternalMarkers()` stamps `x-internal: true` on operations whose path matches `INTERNAL_PATHS` / `INTERNAL_PATH_PREFIXES`; `toPublicDocument()` returns a copy with those ops removed, the `sessionCookie` scheme removed (`UNPUBLISHED_SECURITY_SCHEMES`), and the fallout cleaned up (paths with no ops left, tags nothing references any more, component schemas no longer reachable via `$ref`). Currently internal: `/api/admin/*`, `/api/v1/webhooks/*`, `/api/v1/sync/*`, `/api/push/*`, the browser auth redirects (`/api/auth/sign-in`, `/api/auth/sign-out`, `/callback`), `ws-token`, and the org-scoped `push/*` routes.

The cookie still authenticates — dropping `sessionCookie` from the published spec is presentational: the only way to obtain one is the (internal) browser redirect flow, and while it was advertised Scalar defaulted to it and generated `Cookie:` snippets instead of `Authorization: Bearer`.

Servers: `defaultServers()` advertises `PUBLIC_BASE_URL`/`APP_URL` alone when set (prod does), else just `http://localhost:3000` — a deployment should never tell readers to call localhost. `generate:openapi` overrides with production-first + local dev, since generators take the first server as the SDK's base URL.

Runtime serving: `GET /openapi.json` returns `getPublicOpenApiDocument()` (cached), and `GET /docs` renders the Scalar reference UI over it, with `hiddenClients`/`defaultHttpClient` narrowing ~35 snippet clients to curl (default), js/fetch, node/fetch, python/requests and go/native. Both are public. `getOpenApiDocument()` is the unfiltered document; only `generate:openapi` uses it, so the committed `openapi.json` keeps internal routes (marked `x-internal`) and `sessionCookie` while the published site shows neither. When you add an internal-only route, add it to `public-spec.ts`.

Build artifact: `pnpm --filter @infrawrench/web generate:openapi` writes `app/packages/web/openapi.json`. Commit it so PR diffs show API surface changes.

When you add a new HTTP route, also register it under `paths/`. The spec validates with Redocly (`npx @redocly/cli lint openapi.json`); CI doesn't run this yet but it's a useful local sanity check.

---

## Generated client SDKs

Nine targets: `typescript`, `csharp`, `go`, `java`, `php`, `python`, `ruby`, `rust`, `swift`. All MIT, all dependency-free except Rust (no stdlib HTTP, so `reqwest` + `serde`). `app/packages/web/scripts/sdk/` turns the OpenAPI document into standalone client packages. Output goes to `<repo>/sdk/<target-id>/` — **outside** the `pnpm-workspace.yaml` globs (`app/packages/*`, `plugin-architecture/packages/*`) and gitignored, so the emitted package is never a workspace member and never resolves `workspace:*`. `/sdk` is also in `.prettierignore`. Entry points: `generate:sdk` (reads the committed `openapi.json`; `--rebuild` builds from the live plugin registry instead), and `generate:openapi`, which chains into it.

Pipeline is IR-then-target so more languages can be added without touching the front half:

- `ir.ts` — runs the doc through `toPublicDocument()` (so internal ops are gone by construction, not by a second filter that could drift), normalizes JSON Schema into the closed `TypeRef` union in `types.ts`, and derives the dotted call tree. **Names come from the URL path, not `operationId`**: static segments are namespaces and the last one names the call (`POST /accounts/{id}/sync` → `accounts.sync()`); when the path ends in a parameter there's no segment to use, so the whole static run becomes the namespace and a verb names the call (`DELETE /accounts/{id}` → `accounts.delete()`). One repair pass then handles a name two operations both want (`GET`+`PUT /accounts/{id}/credentials`) or a name that's already a child namespace (`GET /team/roles` next to `/team/roles/{id}`) — both push the op down into a namespace of that name and let a verb name it, which is why `team.roles` gets the full `list`/`create`/`update`/`delete` set. `GET` picks `list` vs `get` off the response being an array, not off pluralization. Last resort is `camelCase(operationId)`, and it warns when it fires. `orgId` is detected as the scope param and marked `defaultable` — optional at the call site, filled in from client config.
- `target.ts` / `targets/index.ts` — the `SdkTarget` contract and the registry. Adding a language = one file plus one array entry.
- `targets/typescript/runtime.ts` — the hand-written request plumbing (`APIV1Client`, `ApiTransport`, `ApiError`, `ClientOptions`). **Not imported by anything**: the generator reads it off disk and inlines it, so it's still typechecked by `pnpm typecheck` and formatted by `pnpm format`. Two tokens (`"@@BASE_URL@@"`, `"@@SCOPE_PARAM@@"`) are substituted at generation time; keep them quoted string literals so the file typechecks standalone. Everything below the `// --8<--` sentinel is what gets inlined.
- `targets/typescript/index.ts` — emits `index.ts`, compiles it with the **TypeScript compiler API** to `index.js` + `index.js.map` + `index.d.ts`, then **deletes `index.ts`**. Compiling rather than printing `.js` and `.d.ts` separately is what stops them drifting, and it typechecks the generated code as a side effect — an emitter bug fails the run instead of shipping. Compile uses `strict` + `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` + `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `declarationMap: false`, and `inlineSources` — that last one is what makes deleting the `.ts` safe, since the map then carries the source itself. Schema names that collide with globals get a `Model` suffix (`components.schemas.Error` → `ErrorModel`, since `class ApiError extends Error` needs the real one) — `TypeRef` refs carry the _spec_ name and the remap lives entirely in the target's `NameTable`.
- `package-metadata.ts` — author (Infrawrench LLC / astrid@infrawrench.com), contributors (Astrid Gealer), license, repo, homepage, keywords. Deliberately outside the targets: a future Python target's `pyproject.toml` wants the same values and they must not disagree. The npm manifest omits `repository.directory` on purpose — that field points at a package's home inside the repo, and this one is never committed.

**The generated clients are MIT, not BUSL-1.1.** A client library gets linked into other people's software, so shipping it under a source-available license with a production-use restriction would make calling the API a legal question before it's a technical one; the BUSL terms protect the service, not the wire format. This is the one place in the tree where the emitted license differs from the repository's — the generator itself stays BUSL-1.1. MIT obliges us to distribute the notice, so `LICENSE_TEXT` is written as a real `LICENSE` file (listed in both `files` and the target's `artifacts`, so a deleted one forces regeneration) and the module banner is a `/*!` block, which minifiers preserve — the whole client is one file someone may well inline.

Multipart bodies emit `form: { ...params.body }`, not `form: params.body` — an `interface` has no implicit index signature so it isn't assignable to `Record<string, unknown>`, but a fresh object literal is.

Cross-target conventions, arrived at independently by several targets and worth preserving: **enums stay open** (a `PluginId` the server adds later must decode, not throw — hence constant tables in Java/C#/PHP, an `unrecognized(String)` case in Swift, `#[serde(untagged)] Other(String)` in Rust); **`allOf` is flattened**, never expressed as inheritance/embedding (Go promotes embedded fields so a name clash marshals neither; Java records and C# records can't extend); and **optionality must survive the wire** (Go uses pointers rather than bare `omitempty`, which would silently drop `false`/`0`/`""`).

Two traps that cost real debugging: **XML manifests cannot contain `--`** anywhere in a comment, and the shared banner's `pnpm --filter …` hint trips it — both `pom.xml` and `Infrawrench.Sdk.csproj` failed to load until each grew an `xmlComment()` helper that rewrites `--filter` to pnpm's `-F` alias. And **filenames must derive from type names, not path segments**: Swift's `Profile` is both a model and a namespace, and two `Profile.swift` in one target collide at the object-file level.

Staleness: each output dir carries `.sdk-stamp.json` (`apiVersion`, `specHash`, `generator`). The trigger is the API version changing; the spec hash and a `GENERATOR_REVISION` constant are safety nets so an unbumped route or an emitter change still refreshes. **Bump `GENERATOR_REVISION` in `scripts/sdk/generate.ts` when you change any emitter**, otherwise existing outputs stay stale. The reason for each rebuild is printed. Regeneration empties the output dir but **preserves toolchain build caches** (`TOOLCHAIN_BUILD_DIRS` — `target/`, `.build/`, `bin/`, `obj/`, …): wiping `sdk/rust/target` turned every spec bump into a ~90s cold `cargo build` and raced `ENOTEMPTY` against a running cargo.

Tests: `scripts/sdk/__tests__/ir.test.ts` (vitest `include` covers `scripts/**/*.test.ts`) runs a miniature spec through the naming rules, generates a real TypeScript package into a temp dir (since `generate()` throws on a compile error, that is the guard against the emitter producing plausible-looking garbage), and then a `describe.each` over `SDK_TARGETS` asserts the cross-language contract every target owes: declared `artifacts` all exist, `LICENSE` carries the MIT text, `APIV1Client` appears, and no internal operation leaks. Assert artifact _existence_, not truthiness — Python's `py.typed` is a PEP 561 marker whose job is to be empty.

**Docs code samples**: `scripts/sdk/code-samples.ts` renders a per-operation snippet for all nine SDKs (`x-codeSamples`, the extension Scalar reads) from the same `buildSdkIr` lowering, and `getPublicOpenApiDocument` injects them into the **served** spec only — the committed `openapi.json` stays snippet-free so its diffs show surface changes. This is the one place server runtime code imports from `scripts/sdk`; the per-language spellings (argument passing, `null` for Java optionals, Go's params-struct/nil/no-arg trichotomy, enum idioms `.aws` / `PluginId::Aws`) are asserted against the emitted SDK conventions in `scripts/sdk/__tests__/code-samples.test.ts`. Go's identifier casing lives in `targets/go/naming.ts` (extracted so the renderer doesn't import the fs-reading emitter). A call-shape change in a target must change the matching renderer — the test file is the tripwire.

---

## BigQuery plugin

BigQuery has two-level hierarchy exposed as parent/child resources:

- `bigquery-dataset` (parent) — created via POST `/bigquery/v2/projects/{p}/datasets`
- `bigquery-table` (child, `parentTypeId: "bigquery-dataset"`) — created via POST `/datasets/{id}/tables` with optional `schema.fields` JSON

Listing hydrates each dataset/table with its full metadata (the BigQuery list API only returns references) — `listBigQueryDatasets` and `listBigQueryTables` in `resource-listers.ts` do a GET per item to pull `numRows`, `numBytes`, partitioning, clustering, primary keys, etc.

### SQL editor query cost estimation

The SQL editor capability (`SqlEditorCapability` on `DetailViewSchema`) has an optional `supportsQueryCost: boolean`. When true, the host renders an "Estimate" button next to "Run" that calls `PluginClient.estimateQueryCost(resourceId, accountId, sql)`.
