A cluster has no billing API, so cost is derived in `plugin-kubernetes/src/cost-model.ts` (pure, db-free): split each node's price into CPU and memory pools (**65/35**, from GCP's published per-vCPU/per-GiB component rates — the only vendor publishing them; it moves money _between_ tenants on a node but never changes the cluster total or any efficiency figure), charge each pod `max(request, usage)` per dimension, measure shares against node **capacity** not allocatable (capacity is what the invoice is for), clamp overcommit proportionally, and split the remainder into named `idle` and `systemReserved` buckets. **Neither is spread across tenants** — doing so overcharges them and hides the finding, which is that the cluster is oversized.

Node rates arrive via the existing `credentialMappings` mechanism (`nodeHourlyRates` on all six cluster resource types); DigitalOcean gives real billed price, AWS/Azure list price, GCP/Scaleway/OVH none. **With no rate, capacity and efficiency render with no money attached** rather than a fabricated number. `metrics.k8s.io` is optional — a 404 degrades to requests-based allocation.

Surfaced through the plugin: peer-pane subtitles and status (`PeerPaneSchema` has no chart/table slot, so real breakdowns go elsewhere), `dashboard-stats.ts`, `TableNode` breakdowns in `detail-renderers.ts`, and `fetchMetricSeries` merged onto the parent cluster's Metrics tab via `exposeMetricsToParent`. Cost rows use `maxHistoryDays: 1` with namespace/workload as tags. **Allocation deliberately does not inherit `SYSTEM_NAMESPACES`** — it reads pods directly so kube-system's cost cannot silently vanish.

## Cost reports, currency, query language & exports

**Cost reports** (`cost_reports`, migration `0075`) are first-class: named, addressable, with a `cost-reports` workspace tab (`reportId` compared in `workspaceTabTargetsEqual`, fixed tab id) and a `cost_report` widget kind. Deletion is asymmetric like budgets — removing a card leaves the report; deleting the report removes every card. `folder_id` is a reserved nullable column with no folders table yet. The ad-hoc `cost_graph` widget is untouched.

**Currency** (`org_currency_settings`, `org_exchange_rates`, migration `0076`) is opt-in conversion at the org's _own_ stated rates with effective dates — no live FX, because finance reconciles against the rate their accounting system used. Rates are `numeric(20,10)` (drizzle returns strings; float becomes one only at the multiply). Conversion happens in app code after per-currency aggregation, never in SQL. A zero/unparseable rate reads as _no rate_, conversion is all-or-nothing per currency, and an unconvertible currency keeps its own series rather than being dropped. Budgets fold in other currencies only when the budget currency is the display currency, so conversion can only ever count _more_ spend and cannot un-fire an alert.

**Cost query language** (`client-core/src/cost-query-language.ts`, pure) compiles text to the existing `CostFilter[]` and nothing else — inexpressible input is a parse error, not a new code path, and `"query"` is in the `Omit<>` on the reader so text is type-level barred from SQL. Round-trips both ways (property-tested over generated inputs). `OR` is rejected naming the fix. The CLI compiles locally and posts `filters`, never `query` — an older server would ignore the field and return _unfiltered_ spend.

**Cost exports** (`cost_exports`, migration `0077`) stream rows via `ResultSet.stream()` into an S3-compatible or HTTPS sink, re-chunked into 8 MiB multipart parts; steady-state memory is one part, and back-pressure throttles the query. Restatement is handled twice: a trailing re-export window rewrites overlapping periods in full at the same key, _and_ every row carries `exported_at` + `collection_watermark` (the minimum newest-collected-day across accounts). Object keys are deterministic per period so re-export overwrites. Writes are `org:settings:write`, not `costs:write` — creating one is standing authorisation to ship the whole billing history to a destination the creator chose.

## Jira integration

`jira_integrations` + `jira_issue_links` (migration `0074`), org-scoped, credentials encrypted like Twilio/Teams. Findings (cost anomaly, orphan, oversized, posture, expiring, probe) become issues; the link row is unique on (org, kind, source, key) so the UI shows "already filed" rather than offering a duplicate. Descriptions convert to **ADF server-side** — passing a plain string to the v3 API does not error, it writes the raw characters, so the failure would be invisible. Project and issue type are API-backed pickers, never typed keys. The site host is checked against an Atlassian suffix allowlist in `jira.ts`: **the bastion registry is not the right control** (it is keyed by plugin id for a connected cloud account; this is an org integration with no account row and no bastion in the path), and without it a member-supplied URL is an SSRF primitive. Members get `jira:read` but not `jira:write`.

## Issue filing (Jira + Linear)

Two parallel integrations (`jira_*` / `linear_*` tables — deliberately not generalized, so either is removable alone) behind one tracker-neutral affordance in `ui/src/issue-filing/` (`ui/src/jira/` is compat re-exports). The button labels itself by what is configured and the caller's per-tracker write permission; a tracker radio appears only when both are connected. Jira: per-org site URL guarded by an Atlassian suffix allowlist (member-supplied host = SSRF primitive), descriptions converted to ADF server-side (a plain string writes raw characters without erroring). Linear: fixed `api.linear.app` GraphQL host so no allowlist (commented where jira.ts has its guard); personal API keys go **bare** in `Authorization` (Bearer is the OAuth form and silently fails); descriptions are markdown; GraphQL errors arrive inside HTTP 200 and rate limits inside HTTP 400 — parse the body, don't trust status. Both: member gets `:read` not `:write`; link tables unique on org+kind+source+key so a finding can't be double-filed.

## Provider cost coverage (Aug 2026 sweep)

Cost collection added for **deepgram** (real USD daily), **elevenlabs** (money; successor endpoint is POST with an array `group_by`, so product type and region arrive together — on the deprecated fallback only _one_ breakdown is requested, since each is a complete decomposition and emitting both double-counts), **cartesia** (credits → money at the lowest published tier, `estimated`), **speechmatics** (hours → money; the endpoint returns a window aggregate with no buckets so daily rows are one request per day, `maxHistoryDays: 90` because history costs _requests_ here, `estimated`) and **hetzner** (inventory × `/v1/pricing`, `estimated`; emits rows **only for the day it runs** because a past month rebuilt from today's inventory omits everything deleted since — and traffic counters are _cumulative per billing period_, so those rows are dated to the period's first day and restated in place, `restatementDays: 31`; all money is scaled-integer, never float).

**Verified as having no usable billing API** (do not retry without new evidence): assemblyai, gladia, revai, cohere, deepseek, gemini (route via `gcp`), workos, groq, together, replicate, fly (GraphQL billing is undocumented), netlify. **Cloudinary was declined deliberately** — it is a flat subscription with an included allowance, so credits are not proportional to money and no rate makes the series correct. `mongodb`/`redis`/`kafka` are connection-string protocol plugins here, not SaaS clients, so they have no account to bill.

## Commitments & the savings planner

Plugins declare `commitments?: { kinds }` and implement `fetchCommitments(accountId)` (throw-rather-than-partial). `CommitmentRecord` lives in `plugin-base/src/cost.ts` beside `CostRow`, joined by `CostRow.commitmentId`. The load-bearing split is `hourlyCommitmentAmount` XOR `unitCommitments` — it decides which utilization question is even askable. **Omit, never substitute**: GCP CUDs carry units and no money at all; Azure's list carries no price but does return its own utilization, passed through verbatim and never blended with derived numbers. `termDays` comes from the provider, never from the dates (split/merged commitments have dates that no longer span the term).

Coverage is a **range** (broad = all uncovered usage, lower bound; narrow = only ever-covered `(plugin, service, region)` cells, upper bound) because there is no single honest denominator. Utilization's obligation counts only `active ∩ window ∩ days-with-data` — counting a day collection never ran reports a healthy plan as under-utilized. Unit-denominated → `null` with reason, never 0%. The planner recommends at nearest-rank **p10** of daily uncovered spend, gated PRESENCE → NOT-IN-DECLINE → FLOOR → MATERIALITY in that order (floor-before-trend misreports a 50% decline as "spiky"); break-even is exactly `1 − discount`. Until collectors stamp `commitmentId` onto rows, coverage reports unavailable with excluded accounts named. Collection is a poller pass on the credits-poll pattern (`account_commitments`, `account_commitment_polls`).

## Commitment attribution (what makes coverage non-zero)

Coverage was inert on ship: no collector stamped `commitmentId`, so it honestly reported "unavailable". Turning it on needed three model corrections, because the naive version reports a confident **0%** — strictly worse than unavailable.

**Coverage is computed on the amortized basis, never cash.** AWS prices RI/SP-covered usage at an unblended rate of **zero**, and Azure prices reservation-covered usage at an EffectivePrice of **zero** in ActualCost. Covered _cash_ spend is structurally zero on both, so a cash ratio reads 0% for every org that has ever committed to anything. `amortizedAmountExpr()` is exported from `cost-readers.ts` and shared by `commitment-readers.ts` and `cost-exports/rows.ts` so the three cannot drift — a restated copy in the exporter already had.

**`commitment_covered_usage` exists because AWS cannot supply a commitment id.** `SAVINGS_PLAN_ARN` / `RESERVATION_ID` are filter-only dimensions in Cost Explorer, never groupable. Azure can (`BenefitId`, EA/MCA only). So the coverage numerator is "charge type is covered-usage **or** a commitment id is present", not the id alone. Every `chargeType === "usage"` site was audited: the coverage and delivered denominators widened; the `hashTags` fold, the ingest default and the `charge_type DEFAULT 'usage'` did **not** — the back catalogue genuinely is usage, and widening the fold would collide a covered row with the plain usage row of the same cell.

**`amortized_reported UInt8` distinguishes absent from zero.** A commitment purchase's honest amortized amount _is_ 0, and the old `if(amortized_amount != 0, …)` fell back to full cash — rendering every purchase at cash **and** its amortized slices. Pre-existing rows default the flag to 0 and land on the `OR amortized_amount != 0` arm, i.e. bit-for-bit the old expression.

**Two-GroupBy limits shape both collectors.** Cost Explorer and Azure Cost Management both cap grouping at two, so each runs multiple passes per chunk. AWS's passes are literal complements (`Not` of the usage record-type union), so spend is conserved even if a record-type token is misspelled. Azure's second pass is deliberately **unfiltered** because the API has no `NotIn` — a filtered complement would silently drop any charge type Azure adds later. Azure derives covered usage as the per-cell gap between ActualCost and AmortizedCost, gated on both being non-negative: Azure emits negative corrections, and an ungated subtraction _fabricates_ coverage.

**Accumulate before emitting.** Any pass whose normalizers are many-to-one must aggregate into a keyed map first. Two provider rows that normalize to the same `(date, service, region, currency, chargeType, commitmentId)` are one host key, and `ReplacingMergeTree` keeps whichever arrived last — silently losing the other. Azure shipped this bug; its test suite had encoded it.

## Unit costs, scenarios, billing rules & invoicing

**Business metrics** (`business_metrics` + `business_metric_values`, Postgres both halves) divide spend to give cost-per-unit and margin. The series lives in Postgres, not ClickHouse, because the join is _not_ per-point: ClickHouse sums the numerator to the requested buckets, Postgres sums the denominator to the same buckets, and they meet once in application code — the shape `currency-convert.ts` already uses. **A missing denominator renders as a gap, never 0 or ∞** (`value: null` + a typed reason); a genuine zero stays `0`, and the two must stay distinguishable. Ratios are computed at the requested bucket — a daily unit cost summed over a month is not the monthly unit cost. `kind: "count" | "currency"` is a DB check constraint in both directions, and margin is offered only for `currency` metrics. Note `infra.metrics` was already taken by workflow key/value metrics (and is a Proxy returning `null` for unknown keys, so `infra.metrics.write` would have silently been `null`) — the binding is `infra.businessMetrics.write`, mapped to `costs:write` in `workflows/authorize.ts`.

**Scenario models** are the half forecasting cannot see: known future cost. Rate changes apply first as _deltas against the untouched baseline of their own scope_, so overlapping rates compose additively (+10% and −20% is −10%, not ×1.1×0.8) and their order provably cannot matter; absolute amounts are added afterwards. `response.forecast` stays the unadjusted trend and `response.scenario` is a **second** field, never a replacement. **Budgets keep the raw trend unless a budget opts in** via `budgets.scenario_model_id` — a hypothesis someone typed must not silently change when a real person is paged.

**Billing rules** adjust spend **at query time and never write `cost_daily`** — collected spend is the audit trail. Percentages compile to a _product of conditional factors_ (`amount * if(c1,1.1,1) * if(c2,0.85,1)`), because markups genuinely compose; reallocation is first-match-wins in a `multiIf` and rewrites only the _group expression_, so conservation is structural rather than arithmetic. `adjustment.rawTotals` is returned whenever `adjusted` is set — even for an org with no rules, so the field's absence has one meaning. Exports, unit costs, anomalies, change alerts and the digest stay **raw**: they either page a human or feed a unit-economics figure. Writes are `org:settings:write`, like currency and exports.

**Invoicing**: `draft` recomputes on every read; **approval is the freeze** — lines, totals, exchange rates _and the day they were read_ are written to the row, and a frozen invoice never touches ClickHouse again (the test asserts `getShowbackSpend` was not called, not merely that the number matched). Void, never edit or delete; corrective invoices link both ways. Permissions are a three-way family (`invoices:read|write|issue`) because approve/send/void are irreversible and `write` is not — that split is what makes maker-checker expressible.

**Nested cost centres**: `parent_id` on the existing table rather than a second "segment" noun, so there is one allocation model and one rule list. `totals` keeps its exact old meaning (directly-allocated spend) and `subtreeTotals` is new, so a consumer summing centres still gets the org total and cannot be made to double count. The `multiIf` still resolves a row to one centre in one scan; the tree is assembled in application code afterwards.

## Network flow attribution

Egress and cross-zone transfer are billed as a total with no cause attached. The answer is a **pair** — who talked to what, across which boundary — which no existing cost dimension expresses.

**Raw flows never move.** The `GROUP BY` runs inside the provider's log query service; only the grouped result crosses the wire. A second low-cardinality totals query exists so the truncated tail is an exact subtraction rather than an estimate — that second scan is the price of having a denominator. Addresses, ports and protocols are resolved to resource refs _before_ storage and never persisted: a churning address would mint a new row per day for the same workload, which is the cardinality bomb here.

**`network_flow_daily` is its own table, deliberately not `cost_daily`.** Merging would stack a derived second opinion of data transfer on top of the provider's own billed `DataTransfer` line and double-count it into every budget, anomaly, export and invoice. The key is `(scope, direction, attribution, src/dst ref, zone, service)` folded into a `pair_hash`, capped at ~518 rows per account-day, TTL 90 days — this explains a bill rather than being a record of one.

Collection is **forward-only** behind a watermark: flow logs do not restate, so re-querying a settled day charges the customer again for a byte-identical answer. A day missed during an outage stays missed, and that is stated rather than papered over.

Amounts are **unconditionally `estimated`** with no off switch, because no configuration makes them reconcile: the free monthly allowance is consumed by services these records cannot see, and volume tiers and negotiated rates are invisible. All errors run in known directions, so the _ranking_ holds even where the absolute figure does not. Cross-zone is priced **per direction** on purpose — AWS captures at both ENIs and bills both ends. GB is 10^9; GiB would under-report by 7.4%, small enough to read as drift.

Enabling collection is `org:settings:write`, not `costs:write`: it authorizes daily queries **billed to the customer's own cloud account per GB scanned**, in perpetuity. The claim predicate joins on the switch so a collector bug cannot run a billable query for an org that never opted in.

## Kubernetes cost beyond node compute

Volumes follow their mounting pod to a workload; a claim mounted by several goes to the **namespace** rather than being split N ways, because dividing a shared disk is an invention. Load-balancer Services match through `spec.selector`, which is equality-only, so the match is an exact subset test rather than a partial selector engine.

Two buckets join `idle` and `systemReserved` as nobody's cost: the **control plane** (a flat per-cluster fee with no per-workload quantity to divide by — correctly absent for self-managed clusters, whose control-plane nodes are already in `/api/v1/nodes`) and **unattached volumes** (bound and billing, mounted by nothing; the usual cause is StatefulSet `volumeClaimTemplates` defaulting to `Retain`).

**Egress is not attributable from inside the cluster.** `metrics.k8s.io` carries a `ResourceList` of CPU and memory only — there is no per-workload byte counter anywhere in the Kubernetes API, so any figure would be invented. It belongs to the flow-log path.

The efficiency report **could not reuse `cost_reports`**: that config column is validated against a closed graph schema, `runCostReport` is hard-wired to the cost warehouse with no data-source indirection, and the numbers this report is about — requested, used, wasted — are computed live and never written there. It lives on the cluster's own surfaces instead.

It also **stops at the argument and names no target size**. The existing right-sizing is a discrete priced-SKU matcher over stored p95 quantiles; a pod request is continuous, two-dimensional per container, applied by patching a manifest, and the cluster metrics API is an instant with no history. None of those four pieces transfers.

## Anomaly acknowledgement

Acknowledging writes the explanation onto the anomaly _and_ creates an org-wide annotation on its day, in one transaction — a failure between them orphans a note and a retry mints a second. Linked by one FK with a partial unique index, resolved backwards by a join rather than stored twice: two columns for one fact is how the two disagree.

An explained anomaly is **marked, not hidden or deleted**; the heading count becomes "N unexplained". **Detection never reads the acknowledgement columns**, so a later spike on the same key fires normally — an acknowledged spike is explained, not exempt, and a repeat of something you believed you understood is the finding you most want. Suppression was considered and declined; if ever wanted it should be a separate time-boxed per-key mute with an expiry.

Re-acknowledging rewords the note **text-only** (its date and scope may have been edited deliberately since). Deleting the note nulls the link but leaves the anomaly explained — removing a marker is not a retraction of the work.

## Period-native plugins must date to the period _start_

Mistral dated an in-progress month's running total to a clamped month-end — a date that moved every collection, so each pass wrote a new `cost_daily` key instead of replacing the last. The month summed to the sum of its own prefixes: an inflation factor of **(N+1)/2**, roughly 16x over a full month, and permanent, because incremental collection never revisits days outside the restatement window.

**A `periodNative` row is dated to the period start**, which is stable across the period — the discipline Cloudflare, PlanetScale, Scaleway, Turso and OVH already followed. `restatementDays` must then be wide enough to _contain_ that start: a 3-day window straddles the 1st on three days a month and misses it on the rest, so the running month simply stops being collected. Mistral uses 62 — the smallest constant always containing the 1st of both the current and previous month.

Reconciliation's day-scoped guard is what let this rot: a monthly-native plugin files a whole month on one day, so "only days the plugin wrote" never covers the rest. `cost/period-scope.ts` widens the restated _unit_ — for a `periodNative` plugin a row on the 1st restates that calendar month. Deliberately only the 1st: Cloudflare anchors on arbitrary days, Turso on a due date, OVH on an issue date, and widening to "any written day's month" would let one flaky invoice page zero a sibling row.

## Invoice approval is a compare-and-swap

`computeInvoiceFigures` reads ClickHouse, so it cannot run inside the Postgres transaction without holding a row lock across an analytics query. Approval snapshots its inputs, computes, then re-reads the invoice **and** the customer under `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` and refuses with a 409 if anything feeding the figures moved — period, managed account, the customer's cost centres, accounts, cost basis, billing-rules flag, currency, name, or the customer being soft-deleted. The lock closes both orderings. Without it, a draft edited mid-approval froze the new period against the old period's numbers.

Void-with-supersede is one transaction covering the void, the corrective draft and **both** link directions; a half-applied supersede leaves an irreversibly void invoice with no path forward. The invoice-number retry loop wraps the transaction rather than sitting inside it, because a unique violation poisons a transaction.

Delivery attaches the breakdown rather than linking it — a link has to keep resolving and would serve a customer's spend to whoever holds it. Retry versus resend is drawn on whether anything landed: a failure with no delivered recipient retries freely, anything partly or wholly successful needs an explicit resend, and an attempt whose outcome was never recorded is treated as unknown rather than as failure.

## Efficiency alerts, and the null that must never become zero

Three trigger kinds — commitment expiry, idle commitment, unit-cost regression — routed through `alerts/route.ts` like every other kind. One `ALERT_TRIGGERS` entry in `client-core/src/alert-routing.ts` derives the rules picker, mute lists, push defaults and the OpenAPI enum. The **one axis that is not derived** is `PushNotificationData`, whose mobile `pushDataToPath`/`parsePushData` switches have no `never` guard — a missing arm compiles and silently drops the deep link.

**Idle detection returns an explicit skip before any arithmetic when utilization is `null`**, never `?? 0`. Unattributed accounts, unit-denominated commitments, missing data days and zero obligation all report null deliberately; treating any of them as zero pages someone about a commitment that is fine. Unit-cost regression applies the same rule to gaps: a day with no reported metric contributes to neither window, so flat spend across gap days reads as _improved_, not as a fabricated regression. Below the minimum reported days a window returns a distinct insufficient-history reason rather than a quiet "no regression".

Idle is `info`, not `warning`: nothing is breaking, the money is already spent, and the finding restates monthly.

## Cost per change / cost per deploy

"What did this change cost?", answered by joining the two halves that already existed: `resource_changes` / `deployment_runs` on one side, collected `cost_daily` on the other. For a change on day `T`, the resource's **per-day** spend over `[T-w, T-1]` is compared against `[T+1, T+w]` and the difference reported as a **run-rate delta** (money per day). A total would describe two spans of time; only a rate describes what the estate now costs to own, which is what the edit actually altered. `cost/change-impact.ts` is the arithmetic (pure, db-free, `__tests__/change-impact.test.ts`), `cost/change-impact-load.ts` the gathering, `client-core/src/change-cost-impact.ts` the wire contract plus every surface's phrasing. **Name collision to keep straight**: `cost/change-detect.ts` is _cost change alerts_ ("spend on this scope moved >X% vs the prior period"); this is a _resource change event's_ cost.

**Computed lazily on read. There is no `change_cost_impacts` table and nothing is cached.** This is the correctness property the feature lives or dies on and it is a design decision, not an optimisation deferred: provider billing arrives late and is then restated (`DEFAULT_RESTATEMENT_DAYS`, and 62 days for the period-native plugins), so a number computed the morning after a deploy is computed against data still filling in. A stored answer freezes that wrong number and needs an invalidation rule mirroring every collector's restatement horizon — a rule that is wrong the moment a plugin changes its own. Recomputing on read makes "the number improves as data arrives" a property of the shape rather than a background job that must not fail. The cost is two ClickHouse range-scans per request, the same ones the cost graphs already run, and the batch endpoint means one pair per _feed page_ rather than per row. The one thing that _is_ persisted is `change_cost_impact_annotations` (migration `0103`) — the link from a subject to the cost annotation someone pinned, so re-pinning a restated finding **rewords the same note** instead of leaving a second marker on the same day. Text only, like anomaly re-acknowledgement: the note's date and report scope may have been edited deliberately.

**"One note per subject" is claim-then-CAS, not read-then-insert.** The obvious ordering — read the link, write the note, insert the link — is wrong under concurrency in a way that costs exactly what the table was added to prevent: two requests pinning the same subject both find no link, both mint a note, and the second insert dies on the unique index with its annotation already committed and now unreachable. So `writeChangeImpactAnnotation` inserts the link **first** with a null `cost_annotation_id` under `onConflictDoNothing` (both writers agree on one row before either writes anything), then attaches the note with a **conditional UPDATE on the still-null column** — the `claimDueDeploymentTriggers` / `last_sent_week_start` protocol, because step one only agrees on the row and not on who fills it. The writer whose swap returns nothing deletes the note it just made and rewords the winner's, so both requests end up describing one note. A null in that column is also exactly what a deleted note leaves behind (ON DELETE SET NULL), so the re-pin path needs no separate branch. The claim retries once, because "conflict" then "row not found" is possible if an org cascade lands in between.

**Every loaded row gets a lookup, and every lookup gets an answer or says it failed — anything else is a silent omission.** On this surface **blank is already an answer**: it means "we looked and there is nothing measurable to say". So any path that leaves a row blank for a different reason converts that reason into a confident, wrong claim about the bill, on a row that otherwise looks fine. Two such paths existed and both are closed:

- _The cap._ The batch endpoint takes 50 ids, which is one page of the web/desktop feed — so those need no batching. Mobile scrolls infinitely, and `ids.slice(0, MAX)` left every row past the cap blank. `chunkChangeImpactIds` (client-core, pure) splits the loaded ids and the screen keys one query per chunk. Chunks are cut from the **start**, so appending a page leaves earlier chunks' keys byte-identical and only the new chunk is fetched.
- _The failure._ Those same stable keys are what would make a failed chunk stick: nothing re-requests it, and `retry: false` meant nothing retried it either. So the impact queries **take the app-wide `retry` default** from `_layout.tsx` instead of opting out (the `retry: false` cards elsewhere are whole optional sections, and a section that quietly does not appear costs the reader nothing — a blank _inside_ a row that is on screen is a claim), pull-to-refresh refetches them alongside the feed, and `collectChangeImpactResults` (pure) derives an `unresolved` set that renders as "Cost impact unavailable" rather than as nothing. That set is **derived from the current results every render, never accumulated**, so a chunk that fails and then succeeds stops being unresolved with no latch to clear; a chunk reporting `data` _and_ `isError` (a failed background refetch) only marks the ids that actually have no answer.

**The charge-type basis is chosen, echoed and printed — never assumed.** One basis per result (`cash` default, `amortized` available), both windows read on it, `costBasis` on the request _and_ on the response, and the label rendered on every line by `costBasisLabel`. There is no code path that can mix them, deliberately: an amortized "after" against a cash "before" on a commitment-covered resource is indistinguishable from a saving nobody made. `parseCostBasis` returns null for an unrecognised value rather than falling through to the default — a caller who asked for a basis we do not have is told.

**A null must never become a zero, and coverage is what separates them.** Two facts look identical in a table of cost rows: "billed nothing" and "we hold no billing". They are told apart by `getCostCoverage` — the inclusive day span the org actually collected for the resource's _account_. Inside coverage a day with no row genuinely cost nothing and is zero-filled; outside it, the day is not in any window at all. A resource with no spend anywhere in either window is `unknown`, never `$0.00/day`: a security group that was never billable must not report that changing it saved nothing, because that sentence implies we looked. `formatChangeCostImpact` returns **null** in compact mode for any non-`measured` status, so the feed renders no line rather than a column of "unknown" that trains people to ignore it. A resource billed on one side and genuinely not on the other _is_ a measurement — a deleted instance reports its saving, a created one reports its full cost.

**`periodNative` providers are refused outright.** They date a whole invoice period to the period's start (see "Period-native plugins must date to the period _start_"), so a 7-day window either swallows a month's bill or misses it entirely; both are wrong by a factor of thirty. Those resources return `unknown` with reason `period_native_provider`, read from `manifest.costs.periodNative` via the plugin loader.

**Window rules, all of which are load-bearing.** `T` itself is in **neither** window — on the day of the edit the resource is billed partly under each shape, which drags both means together and understates every real delta. Today is in neither either (an accruing day always reads as a dip). The half-window **shrinks symmetrically** to what both sides support (`min(requested, availableBefore, availableAfter)`), because a settled 7-day mean against a noisy 2-day one prints the noise as a finding; below 2 days per side the status is `insufficient_data` with no delta rather than a number. Default 7, clamped 2–30 client-side _and_ server-side.

**Attribution is honest by construction.** The delta is correlation and the response says so: `overlappingChanges` counts other `resource_changes` rows for the same resource inside the union window (excluding the subject itself), adds reason `overlapping_changes`, and **demotes the confidence one tier**. Confidence otherwise comes from comparable days per side (≥7 high, ≥4 medium, else low). A deployment's impact is a per-resource breakdown over `deployment_runs.created_resources` — the only set attributable to a run with certainty — and `sumChangeCostImpacts` totals **only the measured rows**, counting the rest in `unknownResources` so the breakdown always adds up to the figure above it; the run's confidence is its weakest row's. A deploy that only re-shipped an image links to nothing and says so rather than blaming whatever drifted in the same hour. Resources a deploy merely _updated_ are not linked to a run at all — that is a schema gap, not a policy.

**Money is never formatted by hand, and not by `formatMoney` either.** `formatSignedPerDay` goes through `formatMonthlyEstimate` (`client-core/src/cost-estimate.ts`), for exactly the reason that function was split out: `formatMoney` drops cents above $10, which is right for a month of reported spend and wrong for a **daily rate**, where $12 and $12.37 are eleven dollars a month apart. Its name says "monthly" only because that was its first caller.

**API** (`API_VERSION` → 1.17.0, three new routes, nothing existing changed): `POST /changes/cost-impacts` (`costs:read` + `resources:read`; a POST because the body is up to 50 ids, not because it writes), `GET /deployments/runs/:id/cost-impact` (`costs:read` + `deployments:read`), `POST /cost-annotations/change-impact` (`costs:write`; one route for both subject kinds, discriminated in the body, since they write the same note through the same link table). An unmeasurable subject is a **400**, not a note reading "$0.00/day". The Terraform provider covers `/cost-annotations` CRUD and `/changes/alert-settings` but none of these — no mirroring was owed.

**Surfaces.** Shared `ui/src/cost/ChangeCostImpactLine.tsx` on both the change feed and the deploy history; `ChangesClient.costImpacts` / `annotateCostImpact` and `DeploymentClient.costImpact` / `annotateCostImpact` are **optional** members so a host that has not wired them (or a build a release ahead of its server) renders without the column rather than failing — the `CostsClient.listAnomalies` rule. Desktop adds `cloud_changes_cost_impacts`, `cloud_deploy_cost_impact` and `cloud_cost_impact_annotate` IPC. Mobile renders the line natively from `client-core`'s formatters and **deliberately omits annotating**: it is a `costs:write` action on shared org state, the same line the anomaly thresholds and the custom-graph editor already sit on. The digest gains a `costMover` line (`DigestCostMover`, composed purely, bounded at `MAX_CHANGES_PER_COST_MOVER` = 200 newest changes in batches of 50) which prints the basis, the window and "other changes overlapped" when contested — and is **omitted entirely** when nothing could be measured, because "no cost-moving changes" would claim we looked.

**Deferred**: a GitHub check on the PR that caused the deploy (the run carries `repo`/`gitSha`, and `github/app.ts` already writes check runs — the missing piece is deciding when the number is settled enough to post, which is the same restatement question the lazy read exists to avoid answering).

## API keys on the org route tree

`iwk_` keys used to 401 against everything under `/api/org/:orgId` — `sessionMiddleware`'s Bearer branch only verified WorkOS tokens — so keys worked on four routes while nine published SDKs advertised key auth. `apiKeyOrgMiddleware` runs first in the chain and the other three are wrapped in `unlessApiKey(...)`, so the existing path is untouched rather than edited.

A key's effective permissions are **its scopes ∩ its owner's current role**, through the same `effectivePermissions` the older `authenticateOrgRequest` uses — so a key stops working the moment its owner is demoted, and a live break-glass elevation is never folded in. `role` is `null` for keys, which fails owner-only guards closed.

`auth/api-key-route-policy.ts` is an explicit table, matched on path segments below the org id (not string prefixes — `/team` must not swallow `/teams-of-things`). Closed entirely: `/api-keys` (a key minting a key outlives its own revocation), `/billing`, `/push`. Mutations closed, reads open: `/team`, `/access-requests`. Audit attribution rides an `AsyncLocalStorage` principal so all ~122 call sites record the key with no edits — note `enterWith` reaches descendants but **not the caller of an awaited function**, which is why it is established in middleware and pinned by a test named for that trap.

## Host-side re-collection reconciliation

When a plugin starts stamping charge types, a `(day, service, region)` cell whose spend was **entirely** non-usage has no row rewriting its old key, so the stale combined row lingers and the day reads high. `clickhouse/cost-reconcile.ts` solves it generically: before inserting a chunk, read back the keys already stored for that account over those days, subtract the keys about to be written, and write a zero-amount row at each leftover key.

This lives in the host, not the plugins, because **a plugin only knows the keys it is writing** — it cannot know a stale row filed under a service label it no longer emits. Guards: nothing written → nothing zeroed; only days the plugin wrote at least one row for; only that plugin's own rows.

**Degraded passes must not tombstone.** Both collectors fall back to an unattributed query on error. Without a signal, one transient flap zeroes every attribution row for the chunk — and since a backfilled account only re-fetches `restatementDays`, a flap that ages past that window is _never_ repaired. `fetchCostData` may return `{ rows, degraded }`; a bare array or absent flag normalizes to not-degraded, so plugins that never opted in are unaffected.

Load-bearing and invisible: `hashTags` folds synthetic `infrawrench:` entries into `tags_hash` **without storing them in `tags`**, and reconciliation depends on that — if they were stored it would skip every attribution row and silently disable itself. Commented at both ends.

## Report folders, saved filters, delivery & change alerts

**Folders** (`cost_report_folders`): 3-level nesting enforced against the whole subtree being moved; delete drops contents to root (both FKs SET NULL — reports are never deleted by a folder operation); cycle prevention is one shared client-core rule so the UI greys out exactly what the server rejects; a stale folderId degrades to top-level rather than hiding a report.

**Saved filters** (`saved_cost_filters`): referenced by `savedFilterId` on graph configs, budgets and `CostQueryRequest`, resolved **server-side at query time** by one resolver used by `runCostQuery` and `budgetMonthStatus` — so every surface behaves identically, and editing a filter once changes every consumer. AND-composes with inline filters. Resolve failure **errors, never falls through to unfiltered** — unfiltered is the failure this feature must never produce. Deleting while referenced is a 409 naming referents (found by scanning `config->>'savedFilterId'` + `budgets.saved_filter_id`); a dangling budget reference would silently re-scope its alert to all spend.

**Scheduled report delivery** (`report_notifications`): the digest pattern, deliberately NOT alert routing (commented against in four places — a schedule is not an alert). Composes from the digest's `DigestSegment` line model so Slack/Teams/email renderings cannot drift; converted totals carry the conversion caveat; **an empty period still sends and says so**; partial delivery is terminal (retrying double-posts). Day-31 clamps to each month's last day without skipping or double-firing; DST keeps the chosen local hour. Writes are `org:settings:write` (per-transport permission splitting would make the required scope depend on the request body). Poller pass on the cost-exports claim protocol.

**Change alerts** (`cost_alerts`, `cost_alert_events`): the third alert family — budgets are absolute totals, anomalies are unconfigured outliers, change alerts are configured relative change on a chosen scope. Windows compare like for like: daily = same weekday last week; monthly = MTD vs the same number of days of the prior month clamped to its length (never the full month); weekly fires once per ISO week; today never counts. Both thresholds must hold when both set. New group = "new", not an infinite %; vanished = −100%; currencies compare per currency (converted first only where rates exist; no rate → compared in itself, never dropped). Fires exclusively through `server-core/src/alerts/route.ts` as the `costChangeAlerts` trigger, so rules/quiet hours/escalation apply; the registry design means one `ALERT_TRIGGERS` entry propagates to the rules picker, mobile mutes, push defaults and the OpenAPI enum.

**Migration caution learned here:** the hand-written `0079_alert_routing_rules.sql` left snapshots stale, so a later `drizzle-kit generate` re-emitted DDL 0079 had already applied — which would abort the whole migration against any real database. `0088` strips the duplicates (reasoning in its header) and its snapshot reconciles the drift. If `generate` ever prompts interactively or re-emits existing tables, suspect snapshot drift from a hand-written migration.

## Change freezes (cloud-only)

Org-wide freeze windows that block destructive mutations server-side. Table `change_freezes` (migration `0050_salty_mach_iv.sql`): name/reason, `starts_at` (default now), nullable `ends_at` (null = open-ended), `active` flag cleared by "End now". "In effect" = active AND started AND not past end — computed in `web/src/services/change-freezes.ts getActiveChangeFreeze`, never stored.

**What's gated** (all in the web API layer; `checkChangeFreeze(c, target)` returns a 423 `Response` or null): resource DELETE (`resource-detail/lifecycle.ts`), destructive `invoke-action` (`resource-detail/actions.ts`), secret-version `destroy` (enable/disable pass; `resource-detail/secret-versions.ts`), deployment rollback (`routes/deployments.ts`). The MCP tools `delete_resource` / `invoke_action` / `rollback_deployment` use `checkChangeFreezeForTool` (no override affordance — agents are told to ask a human). The 423 body is `{error, code: "change_freeze_active", freeze:{id,name,reason,startsAt,endsAt}}`; `lib/api.ts` throws `ChangeFreezeBlockedClientError` for it.

**Destructive actions are plugin-declared, host-enforced**: `plugin-action` HostActions in plugin-base gained optional `destructive?: boolean` (examples: opensearch delete-index/delete-repo, cloudflare purge-cache-all, openai delete-fine-tuned-model). There is no server-side action registry, so during a freeze the server re-renders the resource's detail (`getResource` + `renderDetail`) and walks the schema for the flag (`isActionDestructive`); unresolvable/unflagged actions fail open. The walk only runs while a freeze is in effect.

**Override**: header `x-change-freeze-override: true` + permission `freezes:override` (admin/owner). Audit actions: `change_freeze.{create,update,end,delete,block,override}` — blocks log the attempted action + `overrideRequested`, overrides log what was allowed. Permissions `freezes:read` (all roles incl. member — the banner polls status), `freezes:write` (admin+). API: `/api/org/:orgId/change-freezes` CRUD + `/status` (`routes/change-freezes.ts`, spec `openapi/paths/change-freezes.ts` which also exports the shared `FreezeLockedResponse` 423 slot). UI: `components/ChangeFreezeBanner.tsx` (mounted in `__root.tsx`, 60s poll + `infrawrench:change-freeze-changed` event) and `routes/org.$orgId.settings.freezes.tsx`. Deliberate omissions: desktop local mode (no org concept — cloud API calls are enforced server-side anyway) and mobile UI (mobile surfaces no gated destructive action today); a mobile 423 shows the server's error string.

## Tag policy, untagged spend & showback (cloud-only)

Org-level tag governance in four pieces: a required-tag policy, per-account compliance scores, an untagged-spend report over `cost_daily`, and cost-centre showback via allocation rules. Migration `0057_misty_moon_knight.sql`; API_VERSION 0.27.0 → **0.28.0**.

- **Contract** — `client-core/src/tag-policy.ts` is the shared pure module (types + `extractRecordTags`, `tagPolicyViolations`, `describeTagViolations`, `complianceScore`, `taggedSpendPercent`, `fieldsDeclareTagField`, limits, the 422 code/header constants). The generic tag convention lives HERE, not in any plugin: a record "carries tags" when a field named `tags`/`labels` (case-insensitive) parses as a string map, JSON-encoded map, `k=v` comma list, `k=v` string array, or `{key,value}` object array. `null` from `extractRecordTags` means "cannot be tagged" (no tag-shaped field at all) vs `{}` "taggable but untagged" — enforcement and scoring both hinge on that distinction. Zod lives in `ui/src/cost/config.ts` (`tagPolicySchema`, `costCentreInputSchema`, `allocationRuleInputSchema`) with the `Exact<>` drift assertions.
- **DB** — `org_tag_policies` (one row per org, missing row = defaults, same protocol as `org_cost_anomaly_settings`; `required_tags` jsonb `[{key, allowedValues?}]` + `enforce_on_create`), `cost_centres`, `cost_allocation_rules` (`match` jsonb of AND-ed optional fields tagKey/tagValue/accountId/pluginId/service; empty = catch-all; ascending `priority`, first match wins; centre FK cascades). server-core services: `cost/tag-policy.ts` (get/set + normalize), `cost/allocation.ts` (CRUD, org-checks the centre id, 200-rule cap).
- **Reports** — `clickhouse/cost-readers.ts`: `getUntaggedSpend` (one pass with `sumIf(NOT mapContains(tags, key))` per required key + a top-15 untagged (account, service) query; presence-only — value validation is deliberately resource-side, not spend-side) and `getShowbackSpend` (compiles the ordered rules into ONE `multiIf` over `cost_daily FINAL`, unmatched rows come back as `''` → labeled "Unallocated"). No ClickHouse schema change — everything reads the existing `tags` Map. Web services: `services/tag-policy.ts` (compliance over `resources.fieldsJson`+`outputsJson` in TS; untagged report with account labels), `services/showback.ts` (every defined centre appears even at zero spend).
- **Enforcement** — mirrors the change-freeze pattern exactly, one HTTP-status level down: `checkTagPolicyOnCreate(c, target)` in `services/tag-policy.ts` returns a 422 `Response` (`{error, code: "tag_policy_unmet", violations, requiredTags}`) or null, called in `resource-detail/lifecycle.ts` before `createResource`. Override header `x-tag-policy-override: true` + new permission `tag-policy:override` (its own family so "can create but cannot skip governance" stays expressible; admin/owner hold it via wildcard). Audit: `tag_policy.{update,block,override}`, `cost_centre.*`, `cost_allocation_rule.*`. Types whose create form has no tag field are exempt: when the submitted fields carry no tag-shaped key, the check calls `getCreateConfig` and looks for one (provider round-trip confined to enforcing orgs; unresolvable configs fail open, the `isActionDestructive` stance).
- **API** — `/api/org/:orgId/tag-policy` (GET `resources:read` — the create form needs it; PUT `org:settings:write`), `/tag-policy/compliance`, `/cost-centres` + `/cost-centres/rules` CRUD (`costs:read`/`costs:write`), `/costs/untagged` + `/costs/showback` (`costs:read`, `?from&to` defaulting to trailing 30 days). OpenAPI `paths/tag-policy.ts` (also exports the `TagPolicyUnmetResponse` 422 slot, wired onto `/resources/create`) + `paths/cost-centres.ts`. MCP read tools in `tools/costs.ts`: `get_tag_compliance`, `query_untagged_spend`, `query_showback`.
- **UI** — shared `ui/src/cost/TagGovernanceSection.tsx` on the `CostsPanel` (web + desktop; renders only when the host wires the three optional `CostsClient` reads — web `lib/cost-client.ts`, desktop `lib/cloud-costs.ts` over new `cloud_tag_policy`/`cloud_tag_compliance`/`cloud_costs_untagged`/`cloud_costs_showback` IPC). Policy/centres/rules editing is web-only (`routes/org.$orgId.settings.tag-policy.tsx` + settings nav), the change-freeze precedent; rule pickers populate from `/costs/dimensions` so nobody pastes ids. Create form: shared `CreateResourceModal` takes optional `requiredTags`, shows the policy notice and pre-fills `key=` stubs into an empty `tags`/`labels` field of kind string-list/text; web fetches the policy per open, desktop only in cloud mode. Mobile mirrors the read surfaces on the Costs tab (`features/costs/TagGovernanceSection.tsx` + `useTagGovernance.ts`; hidden when no policy; showback deliberately omitted on the phone, and editing stays web-only like anomaly tuning).
- **CLI** — `infrawrench tags` (policy + compliance table + untagged spend bars) and `infrawrench showback` (`cli/commands/tags.ts`), both `--json`-capable, type-only client-core imports, range via the existing `--last/--from/--to` flags.
- **Docs** — `website/src/content/docs/features/tag-policy-and-showback.md` (+ cli.md, mcp.md, cloud-costs.md, mobile-app.md touches).

## Custom graphs (cloud-only, paid-plan)

Script-defined dashboard charts: org-authored TypeScript run in the same QuickJS isolate as workflows, but through a **separate, fail-closed dispatcher** — a graph host is NOT a `WorkflowHost`, so graph code can never reach create/delete/SSH/storage powers, and unknown RPC methods error instead of falling through. The script gathers data, declares interactive controls, and calls `graph.render({title, chart, refreshSeconds, notice})`; clients render the returned spec and post control changes back for a re-run. Primary authoring surface is the MCP/chat tools; web/desktop get a Monaco editor modal.

- **Runtime** — `workflow-runtime/src/graphs/`: `prelude.ts` (builds `globalThis.graph` + the same `fetch`/`console` a workflow gets), `run.ts` (`runGraph({source, host, controls, button})`; the dispatcher validates EVERY guest argument host-side — cost-query normalization, data-key/value caps, render-spec validation with per-type caps (series/points/slices/table sizes, hex-or-name colors only), per-run call budgets (10 cost queries, 20 metric queries, 25 fetches, 100 data ops)), `codegen.ts` (`generateGraphDts()` — **static** dts, unlike workflow typings, so one string serves Monaco, headless typecheck, and the MCP typings tool; also exported from the sandbox-free `./client` subpath so the desktop renderer needs no fetch), `types.ts` (`GraphHost` interface + limits). `runIsolate` gained an optional `dispatcher` override for exactly this. Controls are **synchronous** in guest code: their values arrive via `__event` at run start, `graph.controls.select(...)` just records the declaration and returns the resolved value, and the declarations ride along with the final `graph.render` RPC. `graph.event.button` names the pressed button, and `graph.event.kind` reports the run's trigger — clients send `trigger: "manual"|"refresh"|"interaction"` in the render request (web/desktop card + editor pass it explicitly; mobile tracks it in a ref since react-query's refetchInterval can't tag its own ticks), so a script can branch on refresh ticks to save its call budgets. `graph.data.get` returns the stored value bare (NOT an RPC envelope — the dispatcher deliberately returns it unwrapped, and there's a pre-seeded second-run test pinning that). Limits: 30s/64MiB (`GRAPH_RUN_LIMITS`), `refreshSeconds` clamped to [30, 86400]. The guest-side codecs/fetch-shim/console blocks are shared with the workflow prelude via `src/guest-lib.ts` (`GUEST_CODECS`/`GUEST_FETCH`/`GUEST_CONSOLE`, spliced into both `String.raw` preludes) so the two program kinds can't drift on the host contract. Real-isolate tests in `graphs/run.test.ts` (including a prelude-bypass test proving workflow methods fail closed).
- **`infra.*` in graphs (read + SSH, author-authorized)** — the graph program now includes the full workflow PRELUDE (before the graph prelude, same `__accountsTree`), and the graph dispatcher forwards a WHITELIST (`INFRA_METHODS` in `graphs/run.ts`: accounts/resource list-get-resolveOutput-logs-describe-getManifest-metrics as `read`, storage.list/get as `storage`, query/kv.list/kv.get/nosql/ssh.\* as `execute`) to the real workflow `dispatch` against a caller-supplied `GraphInfraAccess {accountsTreeJson, host, authorize}`. Everything NOT whitelisted still fails closed — create/update/delete/applyManifest/importYaml/publish/kv.put/sftp/costs.write/page/prompt are unreachable. Caps: 100 infra calls (ssh stream polls excluded), 10 SSH commands, per-command timeout clamped to 30s (ssh is in PAUSED_METHODS, so the clamp+count is what bounds render wall time). **Authorization is definer-style**: `custom_graphs.source_author_user_id` (migration `0046`) records who last wrote the source (re-attributed on every source change — editing someone else's graph makes it run as YOU; a source update with no session user NULLs it, disabling infra); `buildOrgCustomGraphInfra` (web custom-graph-host) resolves the AUTHOR's `resolveEffectivePermissions` fresh per render and `authorize` gates read→`resources:read`, storage→`storage:read`, execute→`resources:execute` — because the render itself is triggered by any viewer with dashboards:read. Null author (pre-feature rows) → infra disabled with a "re-save the graph" error. Typings are now ORG-SPECIFIC and async: `generateCustomGraphTypings(orgId)` = `generateGraphDts({omitFetch:true})` + `generateInfraDts({readOnly:true, interactive:false, costs:false})` — codegen gained `readOnly` (strips create/update/delete/importYaml/publish/sftp emission and types `page: never`) — with a static-half fallback if account enumeration fails. Desktop fetches typings over new `cloud_custom_graph_typings` IPC (`cloudFetchText` in cloud-data/shared.ts) instead of the static import. API_VERSION → **0.17.0** (`sourceAuthorUserId` on CustomGraphFull, `trigger` on the render request shipped with it).
- **Contract** — `client-core/src/custom-graphs.ts` (`CustomGraphRenderSpec`, `CustomGraphControl` — select/checkbox/text/number/button, `CustomGraphChart` — line/area/stacked_bar/multi_bar/pie/stat/table, render request/result, widget config, `DEFAULT_CUSTOM_GRAPH_SOURCE`). Widget kind `custom_graph` added to `DASHBOARD_WIDGET_KINDS`; zod in `ui/src/cost/config.ts` (`customGraphWidgetConfigSchema`, `widgetConfigSchemaFor` is now a lookup table).
- **DB** — server-core `db/custom-graph-schema.ts`: `custom_graphs` (org FK, name/description/source, soft delete) + `custom_graph_data` (the `graph.data.*` KV store: org **and** graph scoped on every query, unique (graphId,key), 200 keys × 64KiB cap). Migration `0045_ambitious_chronomancer.sql`.
- **Web** — `services/custom-graphs.ts` (CRUD/render/typings/check; `CustomGraphError`) + `services/custom-graph-host.ts` (`buildOrgCustomGraphHost`: costs via `runCostQuery` mapped chart-ready with mixed-currency key suffixing, resource listing over the `resources` table, metrics = ClickHouse `getMetricRange` then live `fetchMetricSeries` fallback like the resource-detail route, fetch via `buildWorkflowFetch` → egress proxy; `graph.data.set` writes the value as an explicit `::jsonb` SQL parameter because drizzle binds a JS null as SQL NULL, which would violate the column's NOT NULL — `set(key, null)` is a legitimate write). Routes `api/routes/custom-graphs.ts` (`/api/org/:orgId/custom-graphs`: CRUD + `GET /typings` + `POST /check` + `POST /:id/render`; **`/typings` and `/check` are registered before `/:id`**, and every handler including `/typings` calls `requirePermission`). The `write_custom_graph` typecheck gate runs only when the call actually sends `source` — a metadata-only rename must not re-litigate source deliberately saved with `skipTypecheck`. Reuses `dashboards:read/write` permissions like workflows. **Paid-gated** via `requirePaidPlan(orgId, "Custom graphs")` on create/update/render (NOT list/get/delete, so a lapsed org can still see and clean up) → 402 / tool error naming the upgrade path. Deleting a graph soft-deletes every `custom_graph` widget pointing at it (the `softDeleteBudget` precedent — `config.graphId` has no FK) and hard-deletes its data rows. OpenAPI `paths/custom-graphs.ts`; API_VERSION 0.15.0 → **0.16.0**.
- **UI** — `ui/src/custom-graphs/`: `CustomGraphCard` (render loop: control change / button press / `refreshSeconds` timer → re-render; sticky control state in a ref), `CustomGraphChart` (recharts; pivots `{x,y}` series, sorts only when all-numeric or all-ISO-date, `niceAxis` owned y-scale), `CustomGraphControls` (+ `controlStateOf`), `CustomGraphPickerModal` (pick existing or create seeded with the default source; each row also carries Edit/Delete, making the picker the graph MANAGEMENT surface — a graph is an org object that can legitimately sit on zero dashboards, and without these affordances a cardless graph could only be edited by re-adding it and deleted only over MCP/API. Delete confirms, then the host's `onDeleted` drops the dashboard's own widget copies to match the server-side cascade), `CustomGraphEditorModal` (reuses `WorkflowEditorView` with the graph dts; check-before-save mirrors `write_custom_graph`; preview runs the SAVED script). Host-injected `CustomGraphsClient` (types.ts) — web wraps `apiFetch` in `DashboardView`, desktop `lib/cloud-custom-graphs.ts` over new `cloud_*_custom_graph*` IPC (`electron/cloud-data/custom-graphs.ts` + preload whitelist). Both add-menu entries + `col-span-2` + editor/picker modals mirror web↔desktop; desktop is cloud-mode only like other widgets.
- **Mobile** — renders custom-graph widgets natively: `features/dashboard/CustomGraphCard.tsx` (render query with `refetchInterval` from `refreshSeconds`; selects as chip rows, checkboxes as toggle chips, text/number with an explicit Apply chip — a render per keystroke would hammer the sandbox) + `CustomGraphChart.tsx` (react-native-svg, same shape rules as web) + `CustomGraphPickerSheet` (add an existing graph; `AddCardChoice` gained `custom_graph`). **Deliberate omissions:** no script authoring/editing on the phone (needs a code editor — the Configure button is hidden for custom_graph widgets) and no create-new in the picker. Mobile line charts draw one polyline per contiguous non-null run (a lone point becomes a dot) — `y: null` is a gap by contract, matching web's `connectNulls={false}`. Known deliberate divergence: mobile stacked areas still treat null as 0 (as the cost chart does), and mobile's chart drawing duplicates `CostChart.tsx` primitives — flagged for a future extraction into client-core + a shared chart-primitives module.
- **Docs** — `website/src/content/docs/features/custom-graphs.md` (+ dashboard.md add-menu, mcp.md tool list, billing-and-plans.md plan table).

## Dependency graph (synced cloud data + output references as topology)

The org-wide "Graph" page and the per-resource "Dependencies" tab draw three things at once: output-reference links, edges a plugin **declares** on its resource types (`dependsOn`, see the sidecar section), and the topology **inferred from data the poller already stores**. The host stays generic over providers in all three cases — a declaration is data it interprets, not provider code it runs. Output references alone left the canvas empty for any org that never hand-wired one, which was most of them.

- **Model/layout (pure, shared)** — `client-core/src/dependency-graph.ts`: `buildDependencyGraph` (dedupes edges by `(consumer, fieldKey, provider)` — the `associations` row and the `secret_field_states` output-ref row describe the same reference; the provider is in the key because one comma-joined field value legitimately yields several edges — and drops dangling/self edges), `directDependencies`, `collectDependents` (blast radius, includes the start node), `collectDependencies`, `layoutDependencyGraph` (longest-path layering, providers left, plus two barycenter sweeps; cycle-safe), `dependencyEdgeLabel`. Edges carry `kind?: "output-ref" | "declared" | "containment" | "field-match"` (absent = output-ref) and an optional plugin-supplied `label` ("in VPC"), which `dependencyEdgeLabel` prefers over the `field ← output` caption.
- **Inference (pure, shared)** — `client-core/src/dependency-inference.ts`: `inferDependencyEdges(resources, {rules, existingEdges, focusResourceId, maxEdges})` runs three passes in confidence order — plugin `dependsOn` rules (`collectDependencyRules` builds the `DependencyRuleSet` from loaded plugins; hosts pass it in), then `parent_resource_id` (containment), then field values that exactly match another resource's identity — `external_id` plus identity-ish field/output keys (`name`, `uuid`, `arn`, `endpoint`, `host`, `ip`…). Noise controls, all in that file: ambiguous tokens (claimed by two resources) are dropped entirely; claimants are capped **per resource type** (4, hard per-token bound 64) rather than per token — a per-token cap discarded GCP auto-mode's `default` network along with its forty same-named subnets, so a rule naming `vpc-network` resolved nothing; the caps never reduce a list to one entry, so trimming can't fabricate the uniqueness the guessing pass requires; weak tokens (`staging`) match only inside one account, cross-account needs a machine-looking token (digits + a separator, ≥7 chars); descriptive keys (`status`, `region`, `tags`…) never source a match; matching is exact, never substring; one edge per (consumer, provider) pair; an existing output reference for a pair wins. Declared rules skip the weak-token and length guards (the rule already disambiguates by type) but still refuse to pick between two equally-valid targets, preferring the consumer's own account; and a field a rule speaks for is off-limits to the guessing pass even when the rule matched nothing. A rule's non-default `targetKey` is indexed **only for the plugin/type it targets** (`extraIdentityKeysFrom` + `isIdentityKey`): a flat set made one plugin's choice everyone's problem — Docker matching images on `tags` turned every `tags` field in the app, DigitalOcean's included, into an identity the guessing pass could match on. `resourceIdentityTokens` / `resourceReferenceTokens` exist so hosts can narrow a focused SQL query. All re-exported from `@infrawrench/ui` per the shared-contract rule.
- **Server** — `GET /api/org/:orgId/dependency-graph` (`web/src/api/routes/dependency-graph.ts`, `resources:read`): unions org-scoped `associations` with `resolution_kind = 'output-ref'` `secret_field_states` rows, runs inference over the resource rows (which now carry `external_id`, `parent_resource_id`, `fields_json`, `outputs_json`), returns only nodes participating in ≥1 edge plus a `truncated` flag when the edge cap hit. `?resourceId=` narrows to one resource's direct neighbourhood — the Dependencies tab asks for that, and the org-wide answer would have it pull the whole topology on every detail-page mount. The focused path can't just fetch by id any more (inference needs the resources that _mention_ the focus), so `loadFocusedResources` prefilters with `lower(external_id) = token` plus `ILIKE` patterns over the JSON bags for the focus' 8 longest tokens; patterns are quote/comma/colon-anchored so they hit values rather than keys. Trade-off: ambiguity is only detected among candidates that share a token with the focus. OpenAPI `paths/dependency-graph.ts`; shipped as API **0.26.0**.
- **UI** — `ui/src/components/graph/`: `DependencyGraphView` (hand-rolled SVG, no graph library; arrows point consumer → provider; click = select + highlight transitive dependents, click again = open resource; theme-aware via `var(--color-*)` since Tailwind utilities don't reach SVG attrs). **Identical siblings collapse** before layout via `collapseIdenticalNodes` (`client-core/src/dependency-collapse.ts`): nodes merge only when they match on plugin/type/account/displayName **and** on their full neighbour signature (direction + field + the other endpoint), so a GCP auto-mode VPC's dozen `default` subnets become one `×12` stacked tile while a subnet that actually holds a VM keeps its own. Per-group Show all / Regroup, plus a global toggle; the collapse runs on the _visible_ edge set, since hiding inferred edges changes what counts as identical wiring. Provenance is a line style — solid for output-ref _and_ declared (the plugin stated it, so it is as certain as a hand-wired one), dotted containment, dashed field-match — with a header legend and a "From cloud data" checkbox that filters back to hand-wired references only (the empty state offers to turn it back on). `ResourceDependenciesPanel`, `GraphIcon`. `DetailView` grew optional `dependencies`/`onOpenDependency` props → a "Dependencies" tab that only appears when the resource has ≥1 edge.
- **Hosts** — new workspace tab kind `graph` (store id/title/equality + `graphTabTarget` + both hosts' navigate-args/path-sync/viewport/sidebar tiles, plus the two `validate-tabs` kind lists — server route and openapi enum). Web: `WebGraphPanel` fetches the endpoint; `ResourceDetailClient` fetches it per-resource and filters with `directDependencies`. Desktop: the tile shows in BOTH modes (unlike Costs) — cloud mode proxies over new `cloud_dependency_graph` IPC, local mode assembles the same shape from local SQLite in `src/lib/local-dependency-graph.ts` (same tables, same dedupe rule, same inference pass — local always builds the whole graph, so there's no focused-query path to mirror); `-DetailViewContainer.tsx` wires the per-resource tab the same dual-mode way. Adding a tab kind means hand-editing both `routeTree.gen.ts` files if you don't run the router codegen.
- **CLI** — `infrawrench graph` (`desktop/electron/cli/commands/graph.ts`): `--json` emits `nodes`/`edges`/`truncated`, text mode renders an ASCII tree (roots = resources nothing depends on; children = what a node depends on; `↺` for a link back onto the branch, `…` at the depth cap). `--resource <id>` (or a positional id) prints the Dependencies tab in the terminal — a **Depends on** tree, a **Depended on by** tree, and the blast-radius count. Traversal is `buildDependencyGraph`/`directDependencies`/`collectDependents`/`collectDependencies` from client-core, never a reimplementation. Two things this cost: the focused path deliberately fetches the **org-wide** graph and filters locally (the endpoint's `?resourceId=` answer is one hop deep, which cannot produce a transitive blast radius — a one-shot command has no per-mount loop to protect); and client-core had to be added to the main build's `externalizeDepsPlugin` exclude list, because the CLI's module graph is CJS and client-core is ESM-only, so the runtime import is `await import(...)` resolved into the bundle (same trick `workflow-host.ts` uses for `@infrawrench/workflow-runtime`). Pure render/tree helpers live in `cli/format.ts` so they are unit-testable without Electron.
- **Mobile** — `features/graph/`: `ResourceDependenciesCard` (a section on the resource detail screen, mirroring web's Dependencies tab) over the endpoint's cheap `?resourceId=` form, plus `DependenciesScreen` (`app/org/[orgId]/dependencies.tsx`, `?resourceId=` in the query string) rendering the focused view as two indented trees with the blast-radius count. Traversal is `buildDependencyGraph`/`directDependencies`/`collectDependents` from client-core, never reimplemented; the reader is a new `fetchDependencyGraph` in `client-core/src/dependency-graph.ts`. Two calls worth remembering: the **org-wide canvas is a deliberate omission** (see the mobile omissions list up top), and the blast-radius screen fetches the **org-wide** graph the way the CLI does, because `?resourceId=` is one hop deep and cannot produce a transitive radius — which is why it is a screen you open rather than a section every resource open pays for. Tree flattening stayed local to the feature: the CLI's `renderTree` emits ANSI strings, this needs rows to lay out as views, and the only shareable part (the adjacency walk) already lives in the model.
- **Follow-ups** — the CLI has no `--local` half, since desktop's local graph assembly lives in the renderer (`src/lib/local-dependency-graph.ts`) over the renderer DB client.

## Blast radius ("what breaks if I delete this?", cloud-only)

The delete confirmation's missing sentence, computed from data the product already had. API **1.13.0**; no migration — it is a read-side join over existing tables.

- **The dependency-graph assembly moved to a service** rather than being reimplemented: `web/src/api/routes/dependency-graph.ts` is now a five-line route over `web/src/services/dependency-graph.ts` (`loadDependencyGraph(orgId, focusId | null)`), and blast radius calls the same function. A second walker over a second assembly would be two answers to one question — a dependant shown on the Dependencies tab and missing from the warning above the Delete button is the bug that makes the feature untrustworthy. Blast radius always asks for the **org-wide** graph (`focusId: null`): the endpoint's `?resourceId=` answer is one hop deep by design, and a transitive radius cannot be built from it. The CLI's `graph --resource` and the mobile blast-radius screen already pay the same price for the same reason. That is the report's dominant cost, and the reason the delete dialog loads it asynchronously.
- **One traversal, depth-stamped.** `collectDependentsWithDepth` (client-core `dependency-graph.ts`) is now the only walk in the module; `collectDependents`/`collectDependencies` drop the depths and keep the key set, so "who is reachable" and "how far away" can never disagree. It is **breadth-first**, which the old stack walk was not — a depth is only meaningful when it is the shortest one, and a resource reachable in one hop and again in four is a _direct_ dependant. Cycles terminate on enqueue-stamping and the start stays at depth 0 (it is what is being deleted, not something the deletion breaks).
- **Three kinds of evidence, deliberately not summed.** Dependants (graph, inbound), traffic (network flow attribution, matched on `external_id` within the resource's account — flow refs are the _provider's_ id, never the composite one), and soft references: dashboard pins, custom graphs and workflows whose source names the id, probes, status pages, firing metric alert _events_, leases, schedules, saved log queries, and the ownership record. Deleting the resource does not break a soft reference the way it breaks a dependant; it silently empties it, which is the failure people find a fortnight later.
- **A flow peer is identified under scope, or not at all.** `resources.external_id` carries no uniqueness constraint — the index is `(plugin_id, external_id)`, deliberately non-unique — because plenty of providers use a name as the id, so two accounts genuinely both hold a `default` VPC or a `kube-system` namespace. The first version looked peers up org-wide and kept the first row it found, which attributes measured traffic to an arbitrary resource in a report read seconds before a delete. `resolveFlowPeerIdentities` (client-core, pure, unit-tested) states the strictest rule that involves no guessing: the candidate query is scoped to the flow row's **plugin** (a provider-native ref can only mean that provider), and then **exactly one claimant is a link, anything else is not**. A ref matching nothing is an ordinary external endpoint, not a gap; two or more claimants become an `unchecked` entry naming the contested ids. There is deliberately **no tie-break on the collecting account**: `network_flow_daily` carries one `account_id` per row — the _collecting_ one — and its endpoint columns (`ref`, `label`, `zone`, `region`, `service`, `resource_type_id`) name no account, while the scope values are topological (`private_interconnect` is precisely the cross-account peering case). An intermediate version did prefer a local claimant on the grounds that most traffic is intra-account; that is a prior about which answer is _usually_ right dressed as a resolution, and on a local-vs-remote contest it links a cross-account peer to whichever same-named resource happens to sit in the collecting account. Dropping it costs a hyperlink in the contested case and nothing in the common one (a lone local claimant is still a single claimant); keeping it cost a wrong resource name in front of somebody about to press Delete.
- **`unchecked` is the field that makes the other three honest.** Every gatherer that cannot answer pushes a full sentence onto it — flow collection off, no warehouse, a failed query, and unconditionally: workflows/custom graphs are matched by `ILIKE` on the source and only find a **literal** id, and metric alert _rules_ select by plugin/type/tag so only firing events can be named. `blastRadiusSeverity` returns **`unknown`, never `none`**, when nothing was found and something went unchecked, and the summary component renders gaps even on a clean report. An empty dependant list with a non-empty `unchecked` is a shrug, not a clean bill of health, and nothing in the stack is allowed to render the two the same way.
- **Nothing throws for a partial answer.** `getBlastRadius` (`web/src/services/blast-radius.ts`) runs the three gatherers under `Promise.all` and the eight reference queries under `Promise.allSettled`; a failure costs its own `unchecked` line, never the report. The route answers 200 with a partial report — it is called from a dialog that must open regardless.
- **Severity puts anything user-facing above any count.** One probe on a _published_ status page outranks four internal dashboards: a blank dashboard is an annoyance, a status page going red is something a customer sees. Five direct dependants is the other route to `high`.
- **The delete dialog never gates on it.** `ConfirmDeleteModal` gained an optional `summary?: React.ReactNode` slot, filled with `<BlastRadiusSummary/>` — the dialog opens immediately, the summary arrives underneath, and confirmation is never blocked. A failed fetch renders a visible "couldn't check", because an absent warning reads as "nothing to warn about", which is the one thing a failed fetch does not mean. `useBlastRadius` keeps loading/report/error as three states for exactly that reason.
- **Permission is `resources:read`, not `resources:delete`.** The person writing the change request usually is not the person who will run it; gating the explanation behind the destructive permission puts it out of reach of the people who need it most.
- **Surfaces**: `ui/src/blast-radius/` (`BlastRadiusSummary` for the dialog, `BlastRadiusPanel` for the tab, host-injected `BlastRadiusClient` — the `OwnershipClient` pattern). New host-driven `renderBlastRadiusTab` on `DetailView`, ungated by type like `renderOwnershipTab`; wiring it also fixed `hasOwnershipTab` never having been added to the `hasTabs` OR-chain, which would have hidden an ownership-only tab strip. Web wires it always, desktop cloud-mode only (`cloud_blast_radius` IPC ×1). CLI `infrawrench blast-radius <id>` (`--output json` carries `unchecked`, so a decommissioning script can refuse on it). MCP `get_blast_radius`, and `delete_resource`'s description now points at it.
- **Deliberate omissions.** No mobile surface — mobile already has a graph-only blast-radius screen, and the org-wide walk plus a ten-section report is a planning surface, not a phone one. No desktop **local** half: most of what the report reads (dashboards, probes, status pages, leases, schedules, owners, flows) does not exist locally, so a local report's "nothing else found" would be structurally untrue rather than merely empty.

Docs: `website/src/content/docs/features/blast-radius.md` (+ dependency-graph, cli, mcp).

## Pushing in from outside (pages & cost rows over the API)

Two endpoints let a server that isn't Infrawrench push _into_ it — the mirror image of everything else, which pulls. Both are the HTTP twin of a workflow primitive, and in both cases the workflow path was refactored to share the mechanism rather than being copied.

**Routing/auth.** `POST /api/org/:orgId/costs/rows` (`web/src/api/routes/cost-ingest.ts`) and `POST|DELETE /api/org/:orgId/pages` (`routes/pages.ts`) are registered on `api` **before** the `orgScoped` mount, exactly like chat, because `sessionMiddleware` 401s an `iwk_` key and an unattended caller has nothing else. They authenticate through `web/src/auth/org-request-auth.ts` `authenticateOrgRequest(c, pathOrgId, scope)` — session cookie / WorkOS bearer / API key, pinned to the path org, permission enforced on **every** path via `effectivePermissions` (so a key never exceeds its owner's role). That module is chat's old `chat/auth.ts` generalized; `authenticateChat` is now a four-line wrapper that only narrows the scope type. New permissions: `costs:write`, `pages:write` — deliberately **not** in the member system role.

**Cost push.** `server-core/src/cost/cost-ingest.ts` holds the validator/mapper/writer for every non-collected cost row; `cost/workflow-costs.ts` (`infra.costs.write`) and `cost/external-costs.ts` (the endpoint) are thin callers differing only in their `CostIngestSource` — plugin id, reserved tag, fallback account id, error prefix, per-call cap. The reserved tag is the whole invariant: `cost_daily`'s ORDER BY doesn't include `plugin_id` and is frozen, so pushed rows carry `infrawrench:workflow=<id>` or `infrawrench:source=<name>` to keep their key space disjoint from the pollers' and from each other. Ids live in db-free `cost/workflow-cost-ids.ts` / `cost/external-cost-ids.ts`; API rows report provider `external` and, absent an `accountId`, account `external:<source>`. `web/src/services/cost-query.ts` labels both synthetic providers in one `providerNames()` helper and now labels synthetic accounts in `labelSeries` too, not just in the dimension list.

**Paging.** `server-core/src/paging/deliver.ts` owns the protocol — resolve key/cooldown, claim, fan out over Twilio/push/Slack/Teams, roll the claim back when every transport reached nobody — behind a `PageCooldownStore` interface, because the cooldown row differs per caller but nothing else does. `workflows/paging.ts` backs it with `workflow_pages` (keyed by workflow); `paging/external-pages.ts` backs it with the new `external_pages` table (keyed by org+source+key, migration `0036`). Both claims are a single conditional upsert whose `setWhere` reads the existing row, which is what stops two replicas double-paging. API pages reuse the **`workflowPages` notification trigger** rather than adding a fourth column to three tables — the user-facing label across web/mobile is now just "Pages". Push payload gains an `api_page` variant (`client-core/src/push.ts` + `mobile/src/lib/push.ts` parse/route, deep-links to the org home).

`source` is one idea across both surfaces, so its rule lives once in `server-core/src/source-name.ts` (`isValidSourceName`, `SOURCE_NAME_HELP`).
